# Workspace Decision Log

Decisions made while turning this vault into the workspace that defines the EA-to-MDSE translator and the working conventions for the final vault. Newest last. Each entry: what was decided, why, and where it landed.

This log covers workspace and convention decisions. Translation-rule decisions (element, property, relationship mappings) will get their own entries as we work through them.

## Decided

**W-01 · 2026-09-28 · Vault strategy.** One vault. No reference/working split and no cross-vault links, so any visual can be built without cross-vault connections. The final vault holds engineering notes plus a few definitions and diagrams for people working in it. Methodology and translation material stays out of it. This vault (`Test_Vault_`) is a workspace for defining the translator, not the vault that will be built.

**W-02 · 2026-09-28 · Nomenclature.** MDSE "things" are `Object` (replaces `Thing`). Note classification uses `type` plus `subtype` (replaces `kind`). EA's own metaclass is written "EA Object" so it is never confused with MDSE Object. The translator tool (v2.6.0) still uses the old terms and needs the rename.

**W-03 · 2026-09-28 · EA traceability.** The old `eaGUID` appears only at the bottom of the note body, for traceability to the original element. It is not a frontmatter property and is not used for anything else.

**W-04 · 2026-09-28 · Legacy scaffolding removed, `99_System` kept.** The 21 empty legacy model folders (10_Objects to 71_Artifacts, 00_Home, 90_Concept) were deleted; they held navigation files only and the final structure will follow the EA model's package structure. `99_System` stays and is edited topic by topic, because this vault defines plugins, templates and conventions. Commit `7c1dddd`.

**W-05 · 2026-09-28 · Plugin stack.** Cross-vault plugins archived (Ampure Cross-Vault Resolver, Multi-Vault Navigator); the resolver source and notes on both are under `99_System/01_Admin/Archived Plugins/`. Kept: MDSE Bootstrap (auto-installs the pinned plugins on a new machine for team rollout), Dataview (needed for some functions Bases lacks; the functions are still to be listed), and the rest of the stack. The Obsidian Git binary stays committed because it is in use on the Mac; pinned at 2.40.0. Commits `003a146`, `7af1132`.

**W-06 · 2026-09-28 · Templates and identity.** People create notes by hand, so templates serve both hand-authored and translated notes, and a template is the single definition of a note class.
- Every note created in the vault gets a `uid` and a human-readable `id`. Notes brought in from outside keep their own identification.
- `uid` and `id` are filled automatically when a template is applied.
- `uid` is the global identity and also records creation time and original author.
- Duplicate `id` values from concurrent work are an accepted risk, handled by a periodic AI sweep that finds and repairs overlaps.
- Templates will not all be identical; they get class-specific fields as the rules are built, and some subtypes will get their own templates.

**W-07 · 2026-09-28 · Working agreement.** Claude may push directly to `main` on `Test_Vault_`, so the team learns first-hand how AI participation works and where it goes wrong. Topics are decided one at a time.
**W-08 · 2026-09-28 · Property definitions.** Every property gets its own definition note, so someone new to the vault can look up what it means without guessing. Each note states what the property is, how its value is determined, and what it impacts, plus the other fields that remove ambiguity (see the property definition standard once written).

**W-09 · 2026-09-28 · uid from EA history and name entry.**
- For translated notes, `uid` should carry the original EA creation time and original author, instead of the import time and the person who ran the translator. Feasible: EA has a creation date on every element and an author on nearly all. Detailed rules still to be decided.
- Each person enters their name once, and the author code is generated from it according to one written definition. Entered through a popup the first time a note is created from a template, and also during install (MDSE Bootstrap) so the standard is followed from the start.

**W-10 · 2026-09-28 · Author code rule.** The 13-character author code is the person's last name followed by first name, lowercase letters only, truncated to 13 characters, and padded with hyphens on the right when shorter. Examples: Spencer Skelly gives `skellyspencer`, Chesca Legaspi gives `legaspichesca`, Florian Koerfer gives `koerferfloria`, Jesse Rivera gives `riverajesse--`. This fits the existing `uid` pattern (13 characters, lowercase letters and hyphens).

**W-11 · 2026-09-28 · Author registry.** Ten people are mapped from the EA Author values to their author codes, recorded in `99_System/03_Schemas/authors.yaml`. Login names and display names of the same person are treated as one author (for example `SpencerSkelly` and `skellys`). Jesse Rivera and Ray Virzi are different people. All ten codes follow the W-10 rule and are unique. Every EA author value is covered except 722 blank authors and one junk value (`7.1.0-2`).

**W-12 · 2026-09-28 · Unknown EA authors.** EA elements with no usable author (722 blank, 1 with the value `7.1.0-2`) get the author code `sparxeaauthor`, meaning "created in Sparx EA, author not recorded". It is not a person and is recorded in `authors.yaml`.

**W-13 · 2026-09-28 · uid time is local time.** The timestamp in a `uid` (`yyyyMMddHHmmssSSS`) is local time, not UTC, for every note. Translated notes carry EA's recorded creation time exactly as stored; notes created in the vault carry the creator's local time when the template is applied. Reason: simplicity and consistency, with no timezone conversion. Consequences accepted: `uid` order is only approximately chronological across people in different timezones, and a clock change (the repeated hour when daylight saving ends) could produce a duplicate for the same author, which the uniqueness check catches. The translator tool currently defines this as UTC and needs changing. Duplicate timestamps are resolved by advancing one millisecond until unique, with ties ordered by EA GUID so the same EA data always yields the same `uid`.

**W-14 · 2026-09-28 · id numbering for translated notes.** Every translated note gets an `id`, numbered per class prefix in original EA creation order, oldest first, with ties broken by EA GUID. Numbers are assigned after the translator has decided which elements become notes, so there are no gaps. Same EA data gives the same numbers. Once the migration is final the numbers are permanent; notes created later in the vault continue after the highest migrated number. The tool currently orders by EA GUID and needs changing.

**W-15 · 2026-09-28 · id format.** Every `id` uses a five-digit number, for example `REQ-00042`, for every class. An `id` is never reused once issued. Five digits leaves room for 99,999 notes per class and keeps text sorting correct. The migration itself needs less: after the tool's fold rules only about 3,936 Requirement notes are emitted.

**W-16 · 2026-09-28 · External documents.** An external document (standard, regulation, stakeholder document) is brought in as one note per document edition, with an anchor for each clause. Links go straight to the clause with the anchor. Clauses that have no relationships fold into the document note; clauses that are connected to other elements become their own notes and link back to the clause anchor. To revisit only if a document proves too large to read whole (the largest, UL 2594, is about 610 clauses, roughly 33,000 tokens).

**W-17 · 2026-09-28 · External requirement ids.** Requirement notes that come from outside (standards and stakeholder documents) use the source's own designator as their `id` whenever one can be derived, written as document plus clause, for example `UL2594_13.1`. Standards with no derivable designator get `STD-#####`, and stakeholder requirements with none get `STK-#####` (five digits, chronological, per W-14 and W-15). Stakeholder requirements take their id from the source document name plus the requirement's own numbering. Evidence: 3,248 of 3,363 regulatory requirement names parse into a standard and a clause, and all 3,248 designators are unique. Stakeholder requirements come from about 9 source documents (GSE_SW alone has 1,877), and most carry their own numbering in the name.

**W-18 · 2026-09-28 · Retire, don't delete.** A note that is no longer valid is retired, not deleted: it stays in the vault with `status` set to Retired, so its `id` stays reserved and its history stays readable. This is how "an id is never reused" (W-15) is enforced. A validator flags any gap in a class's numbering as a possible accidental deletion. The ID script should also skip retired numbers by design, since retired notes remain in the vault.

**W-19 · 2026-09-28 · Property definitions location.** Property definition notes live in one folder, `Definitions/Properties`, one note per property, named exactly as the property so `[[uid]]` resolves directly. A Base, `Property Dictionary`, lists them all. Each note has: what it is, format, how it is determined, what it impacts, what blank means, what it is not to be confused with, and the decisions behind it. Frontmatter carries the quick facts: which notes it applies to, whether it is required, who or what sets it, and whether it can change. These notes ship with the final vault. First two written: `uid` and `id`. A `Property Definition` template is in `99_System/05_Templates`.

**W-20 · 2026-09-28 · How uid and id are stamped.** Templates fill `uid` and `id` automatically through two small Templater snippets in `99_System/08_Scripts/02_Snippets` (`Snippet - uid`, `Snippet - id`); every class template calls them. The uid uses the creator's local clock plus their author code, and adds a millisecond if that uid already exists. The id is the class prefix plus the highest existing number plus one, counting retired notes and `formerIds`, so an id is never reused. Each person's author code is created the first time a template is applied: a popup asks first and last name, shows the derived code, and saves it in a local file, `.obsidian/author-code.txt`, which is git-ignored. The snippets were tested against mock Obsidian objects (19 checks pass); they have not yet been run inside Obsidian. The `Freestyle` template is deliberately bare and stamps nothing. The old `next-id.js` is superseded (it filled gaps and used four digits) and is to be removed once the snippets are confirmed working. Templates now use Templater syntax for the title.

**W-21 · 2026-09-28 · AI authorship.** A note created by an AI without a user's direct instruction gets an AI author code in its `uid`: `claudeai-----` (Claude), `chatgpt------` (ChatGPT), `rovoai-------` (Rovo), `geminiai-----` (Gemini). When a note is created under a user's direct instruction, the user's own code is used, even if an AI writes it. The `status` property is what keeps a person in the loop where needed. Codes are recorded in `authors.yaml`. The four notes written in this session were created at Spencer's direction, so they carry `skellyspencer` and are stamped `INFO-00001` to `INFO-00004` in creation order (uid times are the first-commit times converted to Pacific time). The snippet notes are exempt from stamping because Templater outputs whatever is in them.

**W-22 · 2026-09-28 · AI code standard (supersedes the ChatGPT code in W-21).** Every AI author code is the tool name, then `ai`, then hyphens to 13 characters, so a new tool follows the same rule without a decision: `claudeai-----`, `chatgptai----`, `rovoai-------`, `geminiai-----`. Tool names longer than 11 letters are cut to 11 so the `ai` is never lost. The `chatgpt------` code from W-21 is replaced.

**W-23 · 2026-09-28 · AI instructions.** `99_System/02_AI/AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md` is the one set of AI instructions, and `AGENTS.md` at the vault root is a short pointer to it. AI tools follow the same note format as templates: copy the class template, fill in `uid` and `id` by hand with the same rules the Templater snippets use, use the directing person's author code when a user directed the note and the AI's own code only when it created the note alone, leave `status` at the template default, and retire instead of deleting. The previous instructions were replaced because they referred to Ruleset 1.19, cross-vault identity, and a drafting folder (`90_Concept/AI_Workspace`) that no longer exists; a person now reviews through `status` instead of a folder. The uid time uses a time zone: the directing person's if listed in `authors.yaml`, otherwise `defaultTimezone` (Pacific time).

**W-24 · 2026-09-28 · Author registration and changing a code.** MDSE Bootstrap registers each person at install: it asks for their name, derives the author code, saves it locally, and adds the person (code, name, time zone) to `authors.yaml` so AI tools can find it. The plugin's source is not in this repository, so the requirements are written up in `99_System/01_Admin/MDSE Bootstrap - Author Registration Spec.md`, with test cases. `Definitions/Changing Your Author Code.md` explains how a person changes their code if something goes wrong: existing `uid` values are never edited, the old code is kept as `previousCodes`, and only new notes use the new code. The code rule now also removes accents (Müller becomes muller) and turns ß into ss; the first-use snippet was updated and tested for that. Both new notes carry `skellyspencer` (written at Spencer's direction) and are stamped `INFO-00005` and `INFO-00006`.

**W-25 · 2026-09-28 · One note per person.** People are recorded as one note each in `99_System/04_People` (named `First Last`), created from a new `Person` template, instead of a single list. This avoids merge conflicts when several people register at once, lets a person edit their own entry in Obsidian's Properties panel, and leaves room to personalize the interface per person later. Each person note holds `code`, `name`, `timezone`, `previousCodes` and `eaNames` (the names the person appears under in the EA source). The ten people from EA were moved into notes and stamped `INFO-00007` to `INFO-00016` (`skellyspencer`, at Spencer's direction). `authors.yaml` is now configuration only: default time zone, the AI codes, and the code for unknown EA authors. Registration spec, change-your-code guide and AI instructions were updated to match. The translator's EA name mapping will be read from the `eaNames` fields of these notes when the rules are built.

**W-26 · 2026-09-28 · Sequence-style EA ids are dropped; clause identity comes from the element data.** The EA `id` tag on regulatory and Webasto requirements (4,840 elements) and the `SysML1.4::id` tag on information items (2,254) hold sequence-style values such as `IEC_62752_1755`. They do not identify a section and are not carried into the vault. A regulatory clause is identified from the data itself: the clause number in the element's name (5,622 elements), or, where the name has none, the EA `Stakeholder ID` tag, which holds clause numbers (556 more). About 79 elements share a clause with another element in the same standard and need a tie-break rule. Another 2,379 have no clause number; most are the standards and regulations themselves (for example "FCC Part 15 A") or headings, which belong in document notes, not clause notes. Two smaller groups of `id`-tag values look meaningful and are not covered by this decision: 172 design-guide identifiers such as `DG_0307_001_02` (design constraints and requirements) and 137 requirement codes such as `CC115` (on `SysML1.4::id`).

**W-27 · 2026-09-28 · What an `id` is, and where other identifiers go.** The `id` property is either an internal identifier defined in this vault (`REQ-00042`, `STD-00042`, `STK-00042`) or an external identifier that shows the document and the section (`UL2594_13.1`). No other value is put in `id`. Identifiers found in EA that might still be useful, such as the `DG_` design-guide identifiers (172) and the `CC` requirement codes (137), are kept in the note body at the bottom, next to the `eaGUID`, until an AI cleanup after import shows whether they add value. The sequence-style values dropped in W-26 are not kept. General rule for the tag review: when a value might be useful and we are not sure, keep it in the body and decide in the cleanup; drop only clear noise (placeholders such as `unassigned` and `<memo>`, and values known to be meaningless). The `designation` property is not used for these codes.

**W-28 · 2026-09-28 · Layout of EA values in the note body.** A translated note ends with a `Source: EA` section. The `EA GUID:` line is first, and every other value taken from EA that is kept in the body goes below it, one line per value as `- <EA tag name>: <value>`, unless a decision says otherwise. Only real values are written: no placeholders, no empty values, and nothing already moved into a property. After review, the cleanup deletes what has no value and leaves the GUID as the last line. Described for newcomers in `Definitions/EA Source Section.md` (stamped `INFO-00017`). The translator's current "Source properties" table becomes these bullet lines.

**W-29 · 2026-09-28 · Tags with no real value are dropped.** Of the 121 EA tagged values, 69 are dropped: 44 never have a value and 25 only ever hold a placeholder (`unassigned` or `<memo>`). The other 52 carry real values (about 30,700) and are decided in groups. Every tag and its disposition are recorded in `99_System/03_Schemas/ea-tag-dispositions.yaml`. Tags not yet decided are marked `pending`, and default to the body below the EA GUID if left undecided. The `id`, `SysML1.4::id` and both `Stakeholder ID` tags are recorded per W-26 and W-27.

**W-30 · 2026-09-28 · The import runs in two stages.** Stage 1 copies EA faithfully and applies only approved, deterministic rules; the translator does not decide meaning on its own. Stage 2 applies meaning (reclassification, consolidation, cleanup) as separate reviewed changes, each one a git commit that a person approves and can undo. This replaces the earlier approach of deciding meaning during import. Decided from the handoff package review, topic 1. When stage 2 finishes relative to the team starting to use the vault is not yet decided.

**W-31 · 2026-09-28 · Temporary `eaType` property.** Translated notes carry an `eaType` property holding the classification the element had in EA: the stereotype if it has one, otherwise the EA object type (71 distinct values across the source; no stereotype spans more than one object type, so nothing is lost). It sits in the properties directly below `type`, `subtype` and `id`, and is there so the MDSE `type` can be set while people can still filter by the original EA type during review. When the full review is complete it moves below the GUID or is removed. Defined in `Definitions/Properties/eaType.md` (stamped `INFO-00018`). Templates for hand-made notes do not include it.

**W-32 · 2026-09-28 · Element mapping worklist.** Every EA element type is mapped for stage 1 one at a time, largest first, and nothing counts as mapped until it is approved here. The worklist is `99_System/03_Schemas/ea-element-mapping.yaml`: 31 EA object types including packages (35,969 elements, 72 combinations of object type and stereotype), each starting as `pending`. What the earlier semantic translator did with each type is listed beside it as evidence only, not as a decision. If every combination is mapped before the import, no note needs a placeholder type such as `Unclassified`; that question is closed unless something stays unmapped.

**W-33 · 2026-09-28 · Field dispositions.** Every source field and tag ends in exactly one of five dispositions: `property` (a property on the note), `body` (a line below the EA GUID), `structure` (used by the translator to build something, and also copied into the source reference), `archive` (kept only in the source reference), or `drop` (kept nowhere). `pending` is a temporary marker, and undecided fields default to `body`. The handoff's REFERENCE is our `archive`; its REVIEW is not a final disposition. `ea-tag-dispositions.yaml` now uses these words (`clause-source` became `structure`).

**W-34 · 2026-09-28 · Source reference.** The source reference is keyed by the EA GUID. Every source element is listed in it, whatever happened to it, including folded, suppressed and dropped ones, with what became of it. Data used through `structure` and data kept as `archive` is copied into it. Until all reviews are complete, its files stay inside the vault, in the workspace. Afterwards they move out of the final vault. **Superseded by W-35.**

**W-35 · 2026-09-28 · Source reference is a ledger, not a copy of values.** The original `.qeax` and the CSV export already hold every value, and the export is keyed by GUID, so values are not copied into a second file. The source reference is two small files, regenerated on every run: (1) a ledger with one row per source element and connector, keyed by EA GUID, recording its disposition, the rule applied, and the `uid` of the note it became or was folded into; and (2) a run manifest with the date, tool version, input identity and counts by disposition. `archive` therefore means "stays in the original export only", and `structure` means "used by the translator, with the rule recorded in the ledger"; neither is copied. Values kept in the note body are also still in the export, so a cleanup cannot lose them. The `.qeax`, the CSV export, its manifest and a note on how it was produced are kept together outside the final vault, because the export is the tool-independent copy once EA is retired. Until reviews are complete the ledger and manifest live in the vault; commit only validated runs, since each version is stored in git history.

**W-36 · 2026-09-28 · Fix by changing the tool and starting fresh.** If the import has a problem, the tool or its rules are updated and the import is run again as a fresh start. The tool is never run over an existing vault, and the vault is never repaired by importing into it. Consequences: the import must be deterministic (the same input and rules give the same `uid` values and `id` numbers); `id` numbers can move from one fresh run to the next until the run that is accepted, so they should not be quoted in documents before then. Refined by W-37.

**W-37 · 2026-09-28 · The import and review loop.**
1. Run the import into a fresh vault. That run is committed as the baseline.
2. The vault is reviewed, and every change made during the review is recorded.
3. Then one of two things happens: the vault is judged ready for finishing touches and release, or the import is stopped and run again from a fresh start with improved rules, using what the review recorded.
4. This repeats until the vault needs only finishing touches. The finishing touches are made by hand in that vault, and it is released. It is never re-imported.
Stage 2 (W-30) is the review: the changes people make in it are recorded and either become rule changes for the next fresh import or, once the vault is accepted, remain as hand-made finishing touches. They do not have to be written as repeatable rules. The ledger and run manifest (W-35) are kept only for the accepted run. After release, `id` numbers are permanent and new notes continue after the highest one.

**W-38 · 2026-09-28 · Recording review changes.** The changes made during a review are recorded as the `git diff` between the baseline import commit and the reviewed vault, so nothing has to be listed by hand. The reasons go in one short file, `99_System/10_Docs/Review Changes Log.md` (stamped `INFO-00019`): one row per reason, keyed by EA GUID where there is one, otherwise the note's `uid`. An AI turns the diff and the reasons into proposed rule changes for the next fresh import. Commit messages are not relied on, because Obsidian Git makes generic "vault backup" commits.

**W-39 · 2026-09-28 · Administrative element fields.** On EA elements: `Author` and `CreatedDate` are `structure` (they build the `uid`, W-09). `ModifiedDate`, `Version`, `Phase`, `Complexity`, `Effort` and `Status` are `drop`. The existing EA status values (`Proposed` on 32,434 elements, `Implemented` on 12) are not carried over, and the vault starts with its own `status`. `ea_guid` is `body`, as the EA GUID line (W-28). All other element, connector and package fields are listed in `99_System/03_Schemas/ea-field-dispositions.yaml` as `pending`, each with the handoff package's suggestion beside it (57 element fields, 66 connector fields, 23 package fields), and the count of rows with a real value. The suggestions are not decisions.

**W-40 · 2026-09-28 · Element fields with no data are dropped.** Of the 48 element fields left `pending` after W-39, 21 have no row with a real value in the extract (0 of 34,583; empty and `0` count as blank): `Abstract`, `Tagged`, `PDATA5`, `Concurrency`, `Visibility`, `Persistence`, `Cardinality`, `GenFile`, `Header1`, `Header2`, `GenOption`, `GenLinks`, `RunState`, `IsRoot`, `IsLeaf`, `IsSpec`, `IsActive`, `StateFlags`, `PackageFlags`, `ActionFlags` and `EventFlags`. All 21 are `drop`, the same rule as the tags that never hold a value (W-29). Nothing is lost: the `.qeax` and CSV export keep every column (W-35), and a fresh import (W-36) can reopen a field if a later export fills it. The handoff's "open question" marks on nine of them came from not checking for data. 27 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-41 · 2026-09-28 · Presentation, code-generation and display-style element fields.** Nine element fields are `drop`: `Backcolor`, `BorderStyle`, `BorderWidth`, `Fontcolor`, `Bordercolor`, `GenType`, `Scope`, `Style` and `StyleEx`. The colour and width fields hold EA defaults on all 34,583 rows; `GenType` (32,365) and `Scope` (33,256) are defaults too. `BorderStyle` (49 rows) was checked: every value is `3`, on 44 Boundary and 5 ActivityPartition elements, so it is a drawing style, not meaning. `Style` (2,336) and `StyleEx` (1,114) are encoded display strings; the handoff suggested `archive`, and Spencer chose `drop`. `TPos` (118) and `Diagram_ID` (4) are `archive`, not `drop`, because diagrams may still be imported. They move to `structure` only if diagram import shows they are needed. Nothing is lost: the `.qeax` and CSV export keep every column (W-35). 16 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-42 · 2026-09-28 · Note, Alias and Multiplicity on elements.** All three are `body`, and `body` now has two placements, recorded as `placement` in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml`. Refines W-33, which defined `body` only as a line below the EA GUID. `main text`: the content is the note's own text at the top of the body, above the `Source: EA` section (W-28 is unchanged for everything else). `source section`: a line below the EA GUID, as W-28 says. A `body` field with no `placement` means `source section`.
- **Note** (about 14,900 rows) is `main text`, the element description. 172 values carry HTML-style markup and 535 carry character entities; cleaning them is a separate translator rule, not decided here.
- **Alias** (103 rows) is `source section`: `- Alias: <value>`. It is used for several concepts, often a manufacturer part number.
- **Multiplicity** (73 rows, all on Part elements) is `source section`. Where a Part has a value, the retained block note gets one line per relationship: `quantity for <parent assembly> is: <value>`. A Part represents a block or class already defined elsewhere in the model; the block is the retained element and inherits the Part's useful data, so Part elements are expected to merge into their blocks. The rule for Parts belongs to the element mapping (W-32). Evidence for it: `99_System/CSV_EA/t_object.csv` has 3,137 Parts; 2,822 resolve to a block through `PDATA1` or `Classifier_guid` (`PDATA1` holds the block's GUID; `Classifier` does not); 1,968 have no name; and only Multiplicity (73), Alias (2) and Style (3) carry any data of their own.
13 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-43 · 2026-09-28 · Part quantities go on the assembly; unlinked Parts become Object notes.** Changes W-42 for `Multiplicity` and corrects one W-42 number.
- **Quantity placement.** The line sits on the note of the assembly the Part is nested under, one line per Part: `quantity of <block or part> is: <value>`, linking to the block note. It replaces the line on the block note in W-42. The data supports it: all 73 Parts with a Multiplicity are nested under a Class (59) or a Part (14). For a Part nested under a Part, the assembly is the parent Part's block; all 283 Parts nested under a Part have one. Two Parts of the same block under one assembly (13 of the 73) each keep their own line. 42 of the 73 have no name, so the child is named by its block.
- **Unlinked Parts.** A Part with no resolvable block becomes an `Object` note with `subtype` Part until it is reviewed. Its quantity line links to that note. Evidence: 339 of 3,137 Parts have no `PDATA1` block (241 have no link field at all); 250 of them are named, 3 have a Multiplicity, 2 have a connector, 4 have children, and 310 sit under a Class.
- **Correction to W-42.** 2,798 Parts resolve to a block through `PDATA1`, not 2,822. The extra 24 have a `Classifier` that points to another Part, and that Part has no block.
- **Not yet decided.** How a Part's block is chosen when `PDATA1`, `Classifier` and `Classifier_guid` differ; the assembly for the 446 Parts nested under an Object, Activity, State, ActivityPartition, Port or Package rather than a Class or Part; and connectors on Parts. The rule for these belongs to the element mapping (W-32).

**W-44 · 2026-09-28 · The block a Part represents comes from `PDATA1`.** For Part elements, `PDATA1` holds the GUID of the block, and the translator uses it to find the retained block note (`structure`). `Classifier` and `Classifier_guid` are `archive` for Parts: they point to another Part (a part property declared in an assembly), which has the same block in 334 of 335 cases. The one disagreement (a blank-named Part under `pCableManagement`, GUID `{A548A5EC-7148-406c-915D-85345499B6F1}`: `PDATA1` gives "Cable-tie - tighten the power-cables", the path through `Classifier` gives "Cable-tie") takes the `PDATA1` block and is flagged for review, not resolved by a rule. Scope: Parts only. The three fields are still `pending` for other element types and carry a `partsDecision` line in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml`, because `Classifier` and `Classifier_guid` are real type links on other elements (Port 920, Object 545 and 498, UseCase, Action) and `PDATA1` holds unrelated values on Requirements (12,089), Ports (3,432) and Packages (1,386). 13 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-45 · 2026-09-28 · `PDATA2`, `PDATA3`, `PDATA4` and `NType` are `archive`.** They stay in the original export only (W-35). Checked before deciding: all 11,986 Requirements hold `Medium` in both `PDATA2` and `PDATA3` and `Proposed` in `PDATA1`, so no real priority, difficulty or status is lost (the `PDATA1` value matches the status dropped in W-39). Outside Requirements, non-GUID values are defaults or codes (`Medium` on Issues and Changes, `Java` on Classes and Activities, numbers on States and Boundaries). `PDATA2` (1,909) and `PDATA3` (3,007) also hold GUID references, mostly on Ports and Parts; they wait for the Port and Object mapping. `PDATA4` (12) holds `idref` links on Note elements. `NType` has 12 distinct numeric codes. `PDATA1` stays `pending` outside Parts (W-44). 9 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-46 · 2026-09-28 · `Object_ID`, `ParentID` and `Package_ID` are `structure`.** The translator uses them and they are not copied into notes (W-35). `Object_ID` is `structure`, not `archive` as the handoff suggested, because connectors point at elements by this number: both ends resolve to an element on 21,813 of 21,822 connectors. `ParentID` (28,183 rows, all resolve) gives context such as the assembly of a Part (W-43); containment does not automatically become a relationship. `Package_ID` (all resolve to a package) places each note in the folder structure that follows the EA model (W-04). The 9 connectors with an unresolved end are for the connector fields topic. 6 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-47 · 2026-09-28 · `Object_Type` and `Stereotype` are `property`.** Both feed the temporary `eaType` property defined in W-31: the stereotype when the element has one (20,529 rows), otherwise the object type. The MDSE `type` and `subtype` come from the element mapping (W-32), which uses `eaType` as input. The raw pair stays in the export (W-35). `t_object.csv` has 35,969 rows, 72 distinct `eaType` values, and 1,386 rows with `Object_Type` Package; whether package rows become notes is for the element mapping. 4 element fields remain `pending`.

**W-48 · 2026-09-28 · `Name` is `structure`.** The name becomes the note title and file name. Where the file name has to differ from the source name, the original is kept as `- Name: <original>` in the source section below the EA GUID (W-28, W-42); a name used unchanged gets no extra line. Evidence: 1,828 names contain characters that break file names or links (`/` 1,091, `:` 591, `[` and `]` 130 each, `?` 85, `"` 43), 113 have leading or trailing spaces, 30 contain a line break, and 5,062 elements share a name with another element of the same object type (1,281 groups; 1,890 names are shared when case is ignored, which matters on macOS). 3,421 elements have no name (Parts 1,968, Ports 879, Notes 430, Objects 84); Parts take their block's name (W-43) and the rest are not decided. Not decided here: the replacement characters and the duplicate-name suffix (handoff review item 7). Element fields are now settled except `Classifier`, `Classifier_guid` and `PDATA1`, which are decided for Parts only (W-44) and wait for the Port and Object mapping for other types.

**W-49 · 2026-09-28 · Connector fields with no data are dropped.** Of the 66 connector fields, 28 have no row with a real value in the extract (0 of 21,822; empty and `0` count as blank): `DestRole`, `SourceRoleType`, `DestRoleType`, `SourceRoleNote`, `DestRoleNote`, `SourceConstraint`, `DestConstraint`, `SourceQualifier`, `DestQualifier`, `SourceIsAggregate`, `SourceIsOrdered`, `DestIsOrdered`, `SourceIsNavigable`, `HeadStyle`, `LineStyle`, `IsBold`, `VirtualInheritance`, `LinkAccess`, `IsRoot`, `IsLeaf`, `IsSpec`, `ActionFlags`, `IsSignal`, `IsStimulus`, `DispatchAction`, `SourceStereotype`, `DestStereotype` and `EventFlags`. All 28 are `drop`, the same rule as W-29 and W-40. Nothing is lost: the export keeps every column (W-35), and a fresh import (W-36) can reopen a field if a later export fills it. The handoff's "archive" and "open question" marks on several of them came from not checking for data. 38 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-50 · 2026-09-28 · Connector fields with one value on every row are dropped.** Eight connector fields hold a single value across all their rows: `SourceAccess` and `DestAccess` (`Public`, 21,795 rows each), `SourceChangeable` and `DestChangeable` (`none`, 21,795), `SourceTS` and `DestTS` (`instance`, 21,792), `SourceContainment` and `DestContainment` (`Unspecified`, 21,792). All eight are `drop`: a value that never varies is EA's default and no rule can use it. The handoff's "structure / open question" on Containment came from not checking the values. The export keeps every column (W-35). `SourceStyle` and `DestStyle` (five variants, including `Navigable` settings) and `DestIsNavigable` vary and are decided separately, with `Direction`. Assumption not checked: how the earlier translator used these eight. 30 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-51 · 2026-09-28 · Connector `Direction` and `DestIsAggregate` are `structure`.** The translator uses both and they are not copied into notes (W-35). `Direction` (21,795 rows): 20,359 are `Source -> Destination`, 1,426 are `Unspecified` (all Connector, 823, and Association, 603), and 10 are `Destination -> Source` (a Dependency `trace`, an Abstraction `allocate`, a UseCase `extend` and others), which point the wrong way if `Direction` is ignored. `DestIsAggregate` (2,288 rows) appears only on Aggregation connectors: `2` on 1,688 and `1` on 600. By EA's convention `2` is composite and `1` is shared; this is the convention, not something the data confirms, and it should be checked when the relationship rules are written. It separates composition from shared aggregation, which matters for assemblies and the Part quantities (W-43). `DestIsNavigable`, `SourceStyle` and `DestStyle` are decided separately. 28 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-52 · 2026-09-28 · `SourceStyle`, `DestStyle` and `DestIsNavigable` are `archive`.** They stay in the original export only (W-35). In the two Style strings, `Union`, `Derived`, `AllowDuplicates` and `Owned` are `0` on every row; only the `Navigable` setting varies, and it follows the connector type (for example a non-navigable source and navigable destination on Dependency, Abstraction, UseCase and Usage). The 10 rows with the source and destination swapped are the 10 `Destination -> Source` connectors kept through `Direction` (W-51). `DestIsNavigable` (20,896 rows) is `1` on almost all directed connectors and `0` on all 603 Associations; 316 rows at `0` on normally navigable connectors (Aggregation 197, Connector 48, NoteLink 27, Abstraction 21, UseCase 13, Nesting 6, Usage 3) and 2 at `-1` are unexplained and are the reason to reopen the field if the review finds they matter. No schema or rule note in the repository other than this worklist and the log mentions navigability. 25 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-53 · 2026-09-28 · The six connector label fields are `archive`.** `Top_Start_Label` (37), `Top_Mid_Label` (169), `Top_End_Label` (18), `Btm_Start_Label` (15), `Btm_Mid_Label` (10,763) and `Btm_End_Label` (4) stay in the original export only (W-35). Most are display copies of decided fields: `Btm_Mid_Label` is the stereotype as drawn (7,731 match `Stereotype` exactly, 2,795 more are the `allocate` form, 235 are Usage connectors with none); `Btm_Start_Label` matches `SourceCard` on 13 of 15 and `Btm_End_Label` matches `DestCard` on 4 of 4; `Top_Start_Label` matches `SourceRole` on 30 of 37. `Top_End_Label` holds ControlFlow guards (`True`, `No`). `Top_Mid_Label` repeats the Name on 69 Connectors, but on 79 InformationFlow connectors, which have no Name, it holds the conveyed signal (for example `«Physical Signal» ps12VAnalog`); the relationship mapping can promote it. They are `archive`, not `drop`, because the rows that differ cannot be rebuilt from other connector fields. Also confirms W-51: `SubType` is `Strong` on exactly the 1,688 Aggregation connectors where `DestIsAggregate` is `2`, so `2` is composite; the other 600 are `Weak` (27) or blank (573). 19 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-54 · 2026-09-28 · Connector `SourceCard` is `body`, `DestCard` is `archive`.** `SourceCard` (53 rows: 52 Aggregation, 1 Association) is written as the quantity line on the assembly note, `quantity of <child> is: <value>` (W-43), placed in the source section below the GUID (W-42). Where a Part and an Aggregation connector describe the same assembly and block, there is one line. Aggregation connectors mirror the Parts: 1,666 of 2,288 have a Part under the same assembly whose `PDATA1` block is the connector's other end (start is the child, end is the assembly); all 52 connectors with a `SourceCard` have such a Part, 51 of those Parts have a Multiplicity, and the values never differ (0 of 51). One connector adds a quantity no Part holds. The one Association row stays in the export. `DestCard` (7 rows: `1` on 6 Aggregations, `1..*` on one Association) is `archive`; one assembly per child is implied. For the element mapping: the translator must not emit both a Part and its Aggregation connector as separate relationships or quantity lines. 17 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-55 · 2026-09-28 · Connectors are imported where they were drawn; block-level flow connectors are flagged and fixed after import.** Flow connectors (types Connector and InformationFlow) often belong on ports; in EA 118 of 946 are attached to a block or a Part instead (94 Connectors with no end on a port, 15 Connectors and 9 InformationFlow with one end on a port). No rule can choose the port: for the 93 with both ends in the export, only 4 have exactly one port on each end, 26 have none on either end, the rest have several, and a connector's Name matches a port name on 2 of 58 named ones. Ports (4,387, parented by Parts 1,956, Classes 1,903, Objects 485) are part of the same problem. Decisions:
- **Import as drawn.** The translator keeps each connector on the object where it was drawn and does not reassign it to a port (W-30: stage 1 applies deterministic rules only).
- **Both sides.** Values kept in the body for a flow connector (Connector, InformationFlow) are written on both end notes, not only the owner side. This replaces the owner-side-only proposal. Other connector types are not decided here.
- **Flag.** For the 118 the translator writes a line `- REVIEW port needed: <Connector_Type> <Name or unnamed> to [[other end]]` in the `Source: EA` section of both end notes, and records the connector in the ledger (W-35) as a block-level flow connector. The rule: type Connector or InformationFlow, and at least one end is not a Port.
- **Fix after import.** Stage 2 moves them to ports, one reviewed commit each, with the reason in `Review Changes Log.md`. The steps are in `Post-Import Tasks.md` (`INFO-00021`). A snapshot list of the 118 is `99_System/11_Import/Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv` (74 choose a port, 38 create ports, 5 single candidate, 1 end missing from the export).
- **Release.** The vault is not released until stage 2 is complete; nobody works in it before then. This closes the open question of when stage 2 finishes relative to team rollout (W-30).
- **Not decided.** Whether ports become notes or sections of the block note (element mapping), and the dispositions of connector `Name`, `Notes` and `SourceRole`. 17 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-56 · 2026-09-28 · Connector `Name`, `Notes` and `SourceRole`.** `Name` (449 rows) and `Notes` (5 rows) are `body`, placed in the source section below the GUID (W-42). For Connector and InformationFlow they are written on both end notes (W-55); for the other types (Sequence 199, ControlFlow 31, StateFlow 10, Association 7, Abstraction 7, Dependency 5, Aggregation 4, Generalization 2) they go on the owner-side note, the one holding the forward relationship. `SourceRole` (319 rows) is `archive`: it equals the Part's name on 314 of 319 rows, which the block link already carries (W-43); the other 5 stay in the export (W-35). The 199 Sequence names come with `SeqNo` and `DiagramID` and are message labels on sequence diagrams; an owner-side line loses their order, which is decided with `SeqNo`. 14 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-57 · 2026-09-28 · Connector identity and structure fields are `structure`.** `Connector_ID`, `ea_guid`, `Connector_Type`, `Stereotype`, `Start_Object_ID` and `End_Object_ID` are used by the translator and are not copied into notes (W-35). `Connector_ID` is `structure`, not `archive` as the handoff suggested, because the 4,583 connector tag rows and all 37,955 diagram-link rows join to connectors through it and every one resolves. `ea_guid` is unique on all 21,822 connectors and keys each connector's ledger row and the flagged connectors (W-55); connectors have no note, so the GUID line of W-28 does not apply. `Connector_Type` (15 types) and `Stereotype` (10,542 rows, 12 values: `allocate` 2,795, `satisfy` 2,444, `deriveReqt` 1,417, `refine` 1,115, `trace` 775, `extend` 736, `include` 522, `verify` 333, `BindingConnector` 249, `itemFlow` 123 and others) choose the relationship rule; `Stereotype` is `structure` and not `property` because a connector has no note to carry an `eaType`. `Start_Object_ID` and `End_Object_ID` resolve the ends (21,813 of 21,822 connectors have both ends in the export). Not decided: how the translator treats the 9 connectors with an unresolved end (`invalid_connector_endpoints.csv` in the export). 8 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-58 · 2026-09-28 · Correction: the connector worklist was missing 13 export columns.** `t_connector.csv` has 79 columns; `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` listed 66, with one roll-up entry, `edge / coordinate fields`, standing for six of them. W-39 repeated the 66. The element (57) and package (23) lists match their tables exactly. The roll-up is replaced by its six columns (`Start_Edge` and `End_Edge` 2,862 rows each; `PtStartX`, `PtStartY`, `PtEndX`, `PtEndY` 2,926 each) and seven more are added as `pending`: `SourceElement` (0), `DestElement` (0), `PDATA1` (243), `PDATA2` (232), `PDATA3` (201), `PDATA4` (0), `PDATA5` (20,478) and `StyleEx` (24). The connector list now has 79 fields. What the new ones hold: `PDATA5` is layout text (`SX=0;SY=0;EX=0;EY=0;EDGE=1;`); on Sequence connectors `PDATA1`, `PDATA2` and `PDATA3` are the message kind (`Synchronous` on all 199), return value (`retval=void;`) and call type (`Call`); on StateFlow connectors they look like trigger, guard and effect (44, 27 and 2 rows, with values such as `Turn_On_A` and `Battery is Disconnected`); `StyleEx` is `Classifier=<number>;` on 24 Connectors, and none of the 24 numbers matches an element. 21 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-59 · 2026-09-28 · Connector layout and style fields.** Ten are `drop`: `RouteStyle` (21,795 rows), `LineColor` (`-1` on 21,817 of 21,822), `Start_Edge` and `End_Edge` (2,862 each), `PtStartX`, `PtStartY`, `PtEndX` and `PtEndY` (2,926 each), `PDATA5` (20,478, layout text such as `SX=0;SY=0;EX=0;EY=0;EDGE=1;`) and `StateFlags` (127, sequence-diagram display flags such as `Activation=0;`). The real layout is per diagram: `t_diagramlinks.csv` has geometry and style on all 37,955 rows, and a connector can appear on up to 78 diagrams, so a diagram import reads it there, not from these columns. `Target2` (118 numeric values on Generalizations that look like colours) and `StyleEx` (`Classifier=<number>;` on 24 Connectors, none matching an element) are `archive`, because their meaning is not confirmed. The export keeps every column (W-35). 9 connector fields remain `pending`.

**W-60 · 2026-09-28 · `SourceElement`, `DestElement`, `PDATA4` and `SubType`.** `SourceElement`, `DestElement` and `PDATA4` are `drop`: none of them has a row with a value (the W-49 rule, applied to columns found in W-58). `SubType` (2,973 rows) is `archive`: `Extends` (736) and `Includes` (522) match the UseCase `extend` and `include` stereotypes exactly, and `Strong` (1,688) matches `DestIsAggregate` = `2` exactly (W-51); `Weak` (27 rows, among the 600 shared aggregations whose other 573 are blank) is unexplained and stays available in the export (W-35). 5 connector fields remain `pending`: `SeqNo`, `DiagramID`, `PDATA1`, `PDATA2` and `PDATA3`.

**W-61 · 2026-09-28 · Sequence and state-flow connector fields; connector fields are settled.** `SeqNo` (199) and `DiagramID` (209) are `structure`: `SeqNo` is the message order within a diagram (199 Sequence connectors across 14 diagrams, no repeats), and `DiagramID` is the only link from a message to its diagram, because Sequence connectors are absent from `t_diagramlinks.csv`. This meets the condition in W-41 (diagram fields are archived unless needed for structure). `PDATA1`, `PDATA2` and `PDATA3` are `body` in the source section, real values only: StateFlow trigger (44 rows, 26 distinct), guard (27) and effect (2), and ControlFlow guard (6), labelled by EA convention and sample values, to be checked when the transition rule is written. The constant Sequence values (`Synchronous`, `retval=void;`, `Call`, 199 rows each) are placeholders and are not written (W-28). Placement follows W-56 (owner side) until the element mapping decides whether transitions are notes. All 79 connector fields are now decided. Package fields (23), diagram fields, the subordinate tables and the three Part-only element fields remain.

**W-62 · 2026-09-28 · Diagrams-only merge run (exception to W-36 and W-37).** The translator gets a new mode that pulls only diagrams, with a selection of which diagram types to pull, and merges them into an existing vault so the element notes are there to reference. This is a narrow exception to W-36 (never run over an existing vault) and W-37 (never re-import a released vault). Conditions: (1) the run only adds diagram files and never creates or edits an element note; (2) it requires the accepted run's ledger and manifest (W-35), which are kept outside the final vault, and it resolves every reference through the ledger's `uid` (a `uid` never changes and notes are retired, not deleted, W-18); (3) it is deterministic, so the same export, ledger and selection give the same files; (4) anything else that needs changing still means a fresh import. Data behind it: `t_diagram.csv` has 2,924 diagrams in 8 types (Custom 1,230, Logical 1,038, Use Case 267, CompositeStructure 151, Statechart 109, Activity 95, Sequence 24, Package 10); 42,966 diagram-object rows and 37,955 diagram-link rows hold the layout (W-59). Of the drawn objects, 5,263 (12%) are Parts, Ports, Notes, Text, Boundaries or Packages, and 9,469 (22%) are Requirements, many folded into document notes. Not decided: the output format of a diagram (canvas, Mermaid or other); whether types alone are enough or selection by package or name is also needed (Custom and Logical are 77% of all diagrams); how drawn objects with no note are shown (Ports wait for the ports question, Requirements point to the document note); and whether the run reads the Review Changes Log so diagrams reflect stage 2 changes such as connectors moved to ports (W-55), or runs after those fixes. The tool's source is not in this repository, so its current diagram option was not inspected.

**W-63 · 2026-09-28 · Package fields with no data are dropped.** Of the 23 package fields, 11 have no row with a real value in the extract (0 of 1,387; empty and `0` count as blank): `XMLPath`, `IsControlled`, `Protected`, `PkgOwner`, `UMLVersion`, `UseDTD`, `LogXML`, `CodePath`, `Namespace`, `BatchSave` and `BatchLoad`. All 11 are `drop`, the same rule as W-29, W-40 and W-49. The export keeps every column (W-35), and a fresh import (W-36) can reopen a field if a later export fills it. `Namespace` is the one the handoff hedged on ("unless a future explicit source rule needs it"); it holds nothing here. All 23 package columns in `t_package.csv` are in the worklist. 12 package fields remain `pending`.

**W-64 · 2026-09-28 · Administrative package fields are dropped.** `ModifiedDate` (1,387 rows, 2012 to 2026-09-06), `LastLoadDate` (243), `LastSaveDate` (242), `Version` (`1.0` on 1,344, `1` on 43) and `PackageFlags` (4 rows of checksums such as `CRC=a9b064fe;`) are `drop`, mirroring W-39 on elements: git tracks changes from now on, and `Version` is EA's default, not an engineering revision. The export keeps every column (W-35). `CreatedDate` is held back: on elements it builds the `uid` (W-09), and whether a package gets a `uid` depends on whether packages become notes or only folders, which the element mapping decides (1,386 package rows also sit in `t_object`). 7 package fields remain `pending`: `Package_ID`, `Name`, `Parent_ID`, `Notes`, `ea_guid`, `CreatedDate` and `TPos`.

**W-65 · 2026-09-28 · Package identity and hierarchy fields are `structure`.** `Package_ID`, `Name`, `Parent_ID` and `ea_guid` are used by the translator and are not copied into notes (W-35). They build the folder tree that follows the EA package structure (W-04): all 1,386 `Parent_ID` values resolve, there is one root, and the tree is at most 12 levels deep. No package has a blank name and no two siblings share a name, even ignoring case, so folders need no duplicate counter; 25 names are shared across different parents. 64 package names contain characters that are unsafe in folder names (`/`, `:`, `[`, `]`, `?` and others); the replacement rule is the open question from W-48 and now covers folders as well as note names. `ea_guid` keys the ledger row (W-35). Packages exist twice in the export: 1,386 of the 1,387 also have a row in `t_object` with the same GUID and name (the root has none), carrying the Author and a Note (34 rows, 30 identical to the package's own `Notes`); creation dates agree on 1,031 of the 1,386 and differ on 355. Whether packages become notes is for the element mapping. 3 package fields remain `pending`: `Notes`, `CreatedDate` and `TPos`.

**W-66 · 2026-09-28 · Package `Notes`, `CreatedDate` and `TPos`; package fields are settled.** `Notes` (34 rows) is `body`, `placement: main text`: the package description, the same content in `t_package` and `t_object` (the 4 that differ do so only in line endings and trailing whitespace), with line endings normalised. `CreatedDate` (1,387 rows) is `structure`: it builds the `uid` time if a package becomes a note (W-09), taken from the `t_object` row as for elements; the two tables differ by 1 to 2 seconds on 355 rows, always earlier in `t_package`, so there is no real conflict. `TPos` (48 rows, 14 parents) is `archive`: it is EA's manual sibling order, and folders sort by name. All 23 package fields are now decided. Spencer likes the idea of a note for each package or folder that describes what it holds and what it is for, so users understand the folder structure; he is keeping that open to think about, and it is not decided. It decides where the 34 package `Notes` go.

**W-67 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram fields added to the worklist.** `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` now also lists the three diagram tables, each field `pending` with its count of rows holding a real value: `tDiagram` (29 fields, 2,924 diagrams), `tDiagramObjects` (9 fields, 42,966 rows) and `tDiagramLinks` (7 fields, 37,955 rows). The handoff package gave no suggestions for these, so none are recorded. They matter mainly for the diagrams-only run (W-62). The subordinate tables (attributes, operations, linked documents, tags, constraints and others, one CSV each in `99_System/CSV_EA`) are not yet listed.

**W-68 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram fields that carry nothing are dropped.** Twelve of the 29 `tDiagram` fields are `drop`. Four are empty (0 of 2,924): `ShowDetails`, `Stereotype`, `HTMLPath` and `Locked`. Eight hold one value and nothing else: `Version` (`1.0`, 2,924 rows), `AttPub`, `AttPri` and `AttPro` (`1`, 2,924 each), `Scale` (`100`, 2,924), and `ShowForeign` (`1`, 499), `ShowBorder` (`1`, 1,075) and `ShowPackageContents` (`1`, 1,079), which are a display switch either on or unset. The same rule as W-50 and W-40. The export keeps every column (W-35), and the diagrams-only run (W-62) reads the export. `Orientation` (`P` 1,914, `L` 1,010) and the page size fields `cx` and `cy` vary and are kept for the next question, because the diagram output format is not decided. 17 `tDiagram` fields remain `pending`.

**W-69 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram display-setting fields are `archive`.** `Orientation` (`P` 1,914, `L` 1,010), `cx` and `cy` (page size, 6 and 5 distinct values), `PDATA` (2,818 display-option strings; few keys vary: `ShowTags=1` on 153 diagrams, `PSize=9` on 66, `HideParents=1` on 5), `Swimlanes` (2,818 appearance strings; no lane definitions or names), `StyleEx` (document and display switches) and `TPos` (26 rows, manual order) stay in the original export only (W-35). They are `archive`, not `drop` as for connector layout (W-59), because the diagram output format is not decided (W-62) and a few settings, such as `ShowTags` and `HideParents`, change what a diagram shows; they can become `drop` if the format does not need them. 10 `tDiagram` fields remain `pending`: `Diagram_ID`, `Package_ID`, `ParentID`, `Diagram_Type`, `Name`, `Author`, `Notes`, `CreatedDate`, `ModifiedDate` and `ea_guid`.

**W-70 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram identity and placement fields are `structure`.** `Diagram_ID`, `ea_guid`, `Package_ID`, `ParentID` and `Diagram_Type` are used by the translator and are not written into notes (W-35). `Diagram_ID` joins a diagram to its objects and links (2,790 diagrams have objects, 119 are empty). `ea_guid` is unique on all 2,924 and keys the ledger row. `Package_ID` resolves for all 2,924, and 1,148 packages hold diagrams; it places each diagram in the folder tree (W-04, W-65). `ParentID` (1,406 rows, all resolve) names the element a diagram belongs to (Requirement 671, UseCase 213, Activity 198, Class 185, State 124, Object 11); that element is drawn on its own diagram in 1,317 of them, and the other 1,518 diagrams belong only to a package. It is the link that lets an element point to its diagram. `Diagram_Type` has 8 values and is what the diagrams-only run selects by (W-62); it describes how EA drew the diagram, not its purpose, and Custom and Logical are 77% of all diagrams, so selection by purpose needs another key (open in W-62). 5 `tDiagram` fields remain `pending`: `Name`, `Author`, `Notes`, `CreatedDate` and `ModifiedDate`.

**W-71 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram `Name`, `Author`, `CreatedDate`, `ModifiedDate` and `Notes`; `tDiagram` is settled.** `Name` (2,924) is `structure`: the diagram title and file name, with the original kept as `- Name: <original>` below the GUID where the file name differs (W-48). Diagrams need a duplicate rule that packages did not: 172 names are shared by 380 diagrams, and 268 diagrams share a name with another in the same package (126 groups); 144 names contain unsafe characters (the replacement rule open since W-48). `Author` and `CreatedDate` (2,924 each) are `structure`: they build the `uid` if a diagram gets one (W-09); all 10 diagram authors are in the person notes' `eaNames`, so none needs `sparxeaauthor`, and creation time plus author repeats on 28 diagrams, handled by the millisecond tie-break (W-13). `ModifiedDate` is `drop` (W-39, W-64). `Notes` (8 rows, 4 with HTML markup) is `body`, `placement: main text`; one holds an EA element link (`$element://{GUID}`) that the ledger could resolve to a note link. Whether a diagram carries a `uid` and a GUID line depends on the output format (W-62); if diagrams are Obsidian canvas files they have no frontmatter or body, so the `uid` would go in the file name or a companion note. All 29 `tDiagram` fields are now decided. `tDiagramObjects` (9) and `tDiagramLinks` (7) remain, as do the subordinate tables and the three Part-only element fields.

**W-72 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram key fields are `structure`.** On `tDiagramObjects`: `Diagram_ID` and `Object_ID` (42,966 rows, all resolve, one row per element per diagram, no element twice on one diagram). On `tDiagramLinks`: `DiagramID`, `ConnectorID` and `Hidden`. `ConnectorID` resolves on all 37,955 rows, and 7 connectors appear twice on one diagram. `DiagramID` resolves on 37,937 of 37,955; the other 18 point to a diagram that is not in `t_diagram` and are for the translator to list as orphans in the ledger, a rule not decided here. `Hidden` is `1` on 222 rows: the connector is hidden on that diagram and must not be drawn. These are the join keys the diagrams-only run needs (W-62); they are used by the translator and not written into notes (W-35). 11 diagram-table fields remain `pending`: on `tDiagramObjects` `RectTop`, `RectLeft`, `RectRight`, `RectBottom`, `Sequence`, `ObjectStyle` and `Instance_ID`; on `tDiagramLinks` `Geometry`, `Style`, `Path` and `Instance_ID`. They depend on the diagram output format (W-62).

**W-73 · 2026-09-28 · Diagram output format: a canvas file plus a companion note; diagram-table fields settled.** Each diagram is an Obsidian canvas file plus a companion note. The canvas holds the drawn objects and connectors. The companion note carries what a canvas file cannot (it has no frontmatter or body): the `uid` and `id`, the description (`Notes`, main text), the original name where the file name differs, and the `Source: EA` lines (W-28, W-71). The 24 sequence diagrams are left for a separate rule (lifelines and ordered messages do not map well to a canvas; the message order from W-61 could feed another form). Evidence: all 42,966 drawn objects have a rectangle with a positive size (width 6 to 8,579, median 120; height 6 to 5,673, median 70) and `RectTop` is negative on every row, so canvas coordinates follow by a fixed sign flip; `Sequence` is unique per diagram, so it gives the drawing order; Notes (483) and Text (81) become canvas text nodes, Boundaries (50) and ActivityPartitions (28) become group nodes, Parts (836) point to their block's note (W-44), Ports (2,509) wait on the ports question; only 8 diagrams have over 100 objects (largest 214); the workspace already holds 156 canvas files. Canvas has no UML line styles, so connector types would show as labels. Fields settled: `structure` for `RectTop`, `RectLeft`, `RectRight`, `RectBottom` and `Sequence`; `archive` for `ObjectStyle` and `Instance_ID` on `tDiagramObjects` and `Geometry`, `Style`, `Path` and `Instance_ID` on `tDiagramLinks` (canvas lines route themselves). I applied these eleven as following from the format; they can be changed. All diagram-table fields are now decided. Not decided: the class and `id` prefix for the companion note (there is no Diagram template), the file naming, the sequence-diagram rule, and how Ports are drawn.

**W-74 · 2026-09-28 · Empty subordinate tables are dropped.** Fifteen export tables have 0 rows and are `drop` as whole tables, recorded as `emptyTables` in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` without listing their fields: `t_attributetag`, `t_attributeconstraints`, `t_operationparams`, `t_operationtag`, `t_connectorconstraint`, `t_objectconstraint`, `t_objecttests`, `t_objectrisks`, `t_objectproblems`, `t_objectmetrics`, `t_objecteffort`, `t_issues`, `t_tasks`, `t_glossary` and `t_files` (`t_constraint` is not in the export). The same rule as W-29, W-40, W-49 and W-63; the export keeps their structure (W-35) and a fresh import (W-36) can reopen a table. This says the model does not use them today: a later run should be checked for any of these tables having rows, and warn if so (added to the Open list). Tables with data: `t_objectproperties` (249,892 rows; the tagged values, handled in `ea-tag-dispositions.yaml`), `t_xref` (42,052), `t_document` (397, split over four CSVs), `t_connectortag` (4,583), `t_operation` (23), `t_attribute` (5) and `t_objectscenarios` (1).

**W-75 · 2026-09-28 · Linked documents, first part.** `t_document` has 397 rows of four kinds: `ModelDocument` (247, zipped binary attached to Artifact notes of subtype Document, 11 design-constraint requirements and one Class), `ExtDoc` (129 images, `ImageAsset`, `.png`, attached to Artifact notes of subtype Image, 0.3 to 1 MB each), discussion posts (`EDisc` 13 and `EPost` 2, on requirements, 11 by `montanoh`, 926 characters in all) and tool leftovers (`SSDOCSTYLE` 5 report styles, `Model_Forum` 1 empty forum). Decided: `SSDOCSTYLE` and `Model_Forum` are `drop`, and so are the two `Notes` values (both `<PGUID>` tags). The 15 discussion posts are `body`: one line each in the source section of the attached requirement (or of its document note if the requirement is folded, W-16), with author and date. They are decision rationale the requirement note does not otherwise carry. Recorded by document type as `tDocument` in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml`. `ModelDocument` and `ExtDoc` remain `pending`.

**W-76 · 2026-09-28 · Linked documents come in as attachments; licensing is decided later.** All `ModelDocument` (247) and `ExtDoc` (129) linked documents are brought in as attachment files linked from the note of the element they are attached to (`disposition: body`, `placement: attachment`, a third placement beside `main text` and `source section`, W-42). Whether third-party standards content may stay in the vault is decided after the import (task in `Post-Import Tasks.md`). Evidence: the 129 `ExtDoc` files are PNG images (13.9 MB, 126 unique), attached to Artifact notes of subtype Image; the 247 `ModelDocument` files are RTF: 9 hold text (37,105 characters; eight look like standards text, one is `Universal BMID Product Reqs`, 19,277 characters) and 238 hold no text and embed a PNG (252 pictures in all: 227 documents with one, 8 with two, 3 with three; 16.5 MB, all unique), stored in the RTF as raw bytes after a `\bin<length>` marker, so they can be extracted; 251 of the 252 pass a PNG signature and end-marker check. Names such as `Figure 7.5 Example of a Bottom-Enclosure` and `Table 7.4 Comparative Tracking Index` suggest figures and tables from standards. Attached to: Artifact/Document 235, design-constraint requirements 11, Class 1. `99_System/11_Import/Linked Document Attachments.csv` lists all 376 with element, content kind, picture count and size. Risk noted: files committed to the baseline import stay in git history even if a later review removes them, so a removal after commit would need a history rewrite. Not decided: where attachments are stored and how they are named, how the 9 text documents are attached (RTF as is, or converted to Markdown), and the licensing decision itself.

**W-77 · 2026-09-28 · Attachment storage and naming.** Attachment files sit in the same folder as the note they belong to (the note's package folder), not in a separate attachments folder. Each is named after the owning note's file name, then the word `asset`, then a counter per note: `<note file name> asset <n>` (for example `Circuit - USB-C Comm PCE25 asset 1.png`). This replaces the `<uid>-<n>` proposal. Spencer's reason: a `uid` name is not user friendly. I read "linked file name" as the owning note's file name; the `DocName` of all 129 images is `ImageAsset`, so it cannot be the document's own name. Confirmed by Spencer. Impact of mixing files with notes, checked in the repository: the `uid` and `id` snippets read only Markdown files (`getMarkdownFiles`), so they are unaffected; the `Folder Contents` Base filters on folder only, so attachments would appear as rows unless it also filters `file.ext == "md"`, which is a one-line change if the final vault keeps a Base per folder. From memory of Obsidian, to be checked in the installed version: attachments show in the file explorer, are hidden in the graph view by default, and are not searched or returned by Dataview queries. Evidence for the plan: 390 attachment files (9 text documents, 252 pictures from 238 picture documents, 129 images) would land in 108 folders; the owning note's name is 172 characters at most, so file names stay well under 255 characters; 46 of the owning Artifacts are themselves named `ImageAsset`, and 56 planned names are shared with another attachment (6 distinct names), 33 of them in the same folder; because the attachment name follows the note's final file name, the duplicate-name rule for notes (open since W-48) settles both, and embeds should use the full path where a name repeats in another folder. No strong reason to keep attachments separate was found: the licensing review (Task 2) is served by the list in `Linked Document Attachments.csv` and by the word `asset` in the name. Not decided: how the 9 text documents are attached (RTF as is, or converted to Markdown) and the licensing decision.

**W-78 · 2026-09-28 · The 9 text documents are attached unchanged.** The nine RTF text documents (`1 Scope` twice, `2 Units of Measurement`, `3 Components`, `4 Normative Refereences`, `5 Definitions`, `Table 7.1 Thickness of Cast-Metal Enclosures`, `UL 1977 Table 7.1` and `Universal BMID Product Reqs`; 18 to 62 KB as RTF, 147 to 19,277 characters of text) are attached as `.rtf` files, unchanged, named `<note file name> asset 1.rtf` (W-77), with no conversion in the import. Reasons: stage 1 applies only approved, mechanical rules (W-30) and converting RTF is a guess about structure, with two of the nine holding tables that would convert badly; a converted `.md` file would be a note to Obsidian, so it would be indexed, appear in the graph and need a `uid` and an `id` (W-06). The original is kept, and Task 2 decides what happens to standards text. Cost: Obsidian cannot preview RTF, so the text opens in an outside app and is not searchable in the vault. `Universal BMID Product Reqs` (19,277 characters, apparently your own requirements) is converted into the main text of its note after import (Task 3 in `Post-Import Tasks.md`).

**W-79 · 2026-09-28 · Cross-reference table, first part.** `t_xref` has 42,052 rows in five kinds (the `Name` column): `Stereotypes` 31,058, `CustomProperties` 8,793, `MOFProps` 1,291, `DefaultDiagram` 894 and `SMLS` 16. Decided: `Stereotypes` are `archive` (they repeat the Stereotype fields, W-47, and add the profile name: SysML1.4 19,664, Webasto MBDV Profile 9,083, EAUML 680, StandardProfileL2 344, RAAML 18; the name matches the `Stereotype` field on 29,791 rows and the rest use another text form such as `Name=include;GUID=...`, with the same name in the samples checked); `SMLS` (16, XML layout for an EA matrix view), the `DGS=` diagram grid settings (2,773), `_defaultDiagramType` (2,740) and `MOFProps` `extensionLocation` (704, UseCase extension point location) are `drop`. If two profiles ever define a stereotype with the same name, the profile in `Stereotypes` is the only way to tell them apart; none does in this model (W-31), and `archive` keeps that open. Recorded by kind and item as `tXref` in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml`. Still `pending`: `CustomProperties` other properties (3,280: `_connectorRef` 2,055, `isReference` 1,124, `kind` 57, `isConjugated` 32, and a few others), `MOFProps` other behaviours (587: `redefinedProperty` 430 on Parts, `conveyed` 79 on InformationFlow connectors, `trigger` 44 on StateFlow connectors, `event` 20 on Trigger objects, and a few others) and `DefaultDiagram` (894). The conveyed, trigger, event and redefined-property rows are real relationship data and belong with the relationship mapping.

**W-80 · 2026-09-28 · Default diagram line in the element note.** An element that has a default diagram gets a line `Default diagram: [[<companion note>]]` in the body, below the main text and above the `Source: EA` section, so a reader can go straight to the model that defines the element. It sits in a marked generated block, and it is written only if the diagram's canvas is created. That covers both a full initial import with diagrams switched on and a later diagrams-only run. This is a second, narrow extension of W-62: the diagrams-only run may write or refresh that marked block in element notes and changes nothing else in them. The line points at the diagram's companion note (W-73), not the canvas. Because the line exists only once its target does, there are no dangling links. `DefaultDiagram` (`t_xref`, 894 rows) is `structure`: it gives 893 element-to-diagram pairs, 877 of which match the diagram owner link (W-70) and 16 of which are default only; it also says which of an element's diagrams is the default (529 owner links are not the default). The element is in the same package as its default diagram for 891 of 893. Elements get no line where the diagram is empty (2), is a Sequence diagram with no canvas form yet (1), or is missing from the export (1). Not decided: the format of the marker, the handling of default diagrams whose names are shared (84 of 893, W-48 and W-71) beyond using the final companion-note name or a full path, and whether the run skips a block edited by hand.

**W-81 · 2026-09-28 · Every requirement is imported as its own note.** For this import all 13,988 Requirement elements become notes; none is folded into a document note during the import. This replaces the first proposal in the handoff (fold the unconnected standard and stakeholder requirements into their document note) and the fold rule of W-16 for the import; folding becomes a stage 2 task (Task 4 in `Post-Import Tasks.md`). Spencer's reason: import everything to avoid conflicts and lost data, and not have to repair links broken by a rigid import tool. Evidence: `requirement_connectivity_audit.csv` lists 13,988 requirements; 9,788 have no direct or child connector (`requirement` 5,221, `Regulatory Requirement` 3,155, `Webasto Requirement` 1,363, `designConstraint` 28, `functionalRequirement` 15, blank stereotype 5); 817 of those are drawn on a diagram, 7,491 have a note and 9,784 have tagged values. The handoff's figure of 10,052 unconnected standard and stakeholder requirements comes from the earlier tool's rules and is not reconciled with 9,788. Effects: diagram objects that are requirements (9,469 drawn) and the default-diagram lines for 600 Requirements (W-80) resolve straight to notes; the estimate in W-15 (about 3,936 Requirement notes after folding) no longer applies. Folding is safe to do later because notes are retired, not deleted (W-18): a retired note keeps its `id` and its links, so nothing breaks. New problem: W-17 gives an external requirement the `id` of its standard and clause, and W-26 found 79 elements that share a clause with another in the same standard, so importing them all makes 79 duplicate `id` values. The tie-break for those 79 is now needed before the import. 2,379 have no clause number (mostly the standards themselves and headings) and take `STD-#####` or `STK-#####` (W-17).

**W-82 · 2026-09-28 · Requirements that share a designator take internal ids.** When two or more requirement notes produce the same external designator (standard and clause, W-17), none of them keeps it as its `id`. Each takes an internal `STD-#####` or `STK-#####` id (W-17's fallback, numbered in EA creation order, W-14), and its designator is kept in the source section as `- Designator: <value>`. Reasons: a designator shared by two notes does not identify one section, which is what an external `id` means (W-27); letting the earliest note keep it would hand the clause id to whichever note EA created first, an arbitrary choice; nothing is lost because the designator stays in the note. Cost: those notes lose the readable `UL2594_13.1`-style id, and a search by designator has to find the body line. The number affected is not known: W-26 counted about 79, and a quick rebuild from the export could not reproduce it because the parse rule per source document, which decides what counts as the same designator (suffixes such as `.story` and `.prod`), is still open. This settles the tie-break in principle; the per-document parse rule remains open.

They are real links between elements that exist, used by the translator and not copied into notes (W-35). `redefinedProperty` (430, FlowProperty Parts) and `event` (20, Trigger objects) are `archive`: none of their 450 targets is in the export, so there is nothing to link. They are the named counterpart of the `entry` and `doActivity` relationship rows kept in W-83, and real behaviour the State note does not otherwise carry; 6 rows have a `Behaviour` value that is not resolved. `t_attribute` (5 rows, all on Classes: `Abstraction Layer` twice on `MHE Charger`, `Part Number` on `MVS_800`, `Cable OD Min` and `Cable OD Max` on `Bend Relief`, plus the profile stereotype row `Webasto MBDV Profile::Business Line(UML::Class)` on `MHE Charger`) is `body`: one line per row in the source section of the owning Class note, `- Attribute: <name> (<type>)`; the profile row is kept for the cleanup (W-27). All subordinate tables are now decided: 15 empty tables (W-74), linked documents (W-75, W-76), `t_xref` (W-79, W-83, W-84), `t_connectortag` (W-85) and these three. `t_objectproperties` (the tagged values) is decided in `ea-tag-dispositions.yaml`.

**W-87 · 2026-09-28 · The Status and Object Type tags are dropped; a new analysis follows the import.** Four tags are `drop`: `Status` (4,848 rows on Regulatory Requirement 3,363 and Webasto Requirement 1,485: `Draft` on 4,847 and `Discontinued` on 1), `Webasto MBDV Profile::Status` (2,391 rows on InformationItem 2,254 and Requirement 137: `Draft` on all), `Object Type` (4,848 rows: `Requirement` 3,362, `Heading` 1,478, `Information` 8) and `Webasto MBDV Profile::Object Type` (2,391 rows: `Information` 2,254, `Requirement` 123, `Heading` 13, `information` 1). The status values are EA's placeholder and the vault starts with its own `status` (W-39). What the drop leaves out of the notes: the `Heading` classification on 1,491 elements (which Task 4 needs when folding requirements) and the one `Discontinued` requirement. Both `Status` tags were missing from the trimmed `t_objectproperties.csv` (`tagvalue_summary.csv` marks them `FilteredOut`); the full rows are in `t_objectproperties_all.csv`. 44 of the 121 tags remain `pending`, and undecided tags default to a body line below the EA GUID (W-29).

**W-88 · 2026-09-28 · Properties every note carries.** Seven properties, on every note, translated or hand-made: `uid`, `id`, `type`, `subtype`, `status`, `eaType` and `tags`. `eaType` stays temporary and translated-only (W-31). `aliases` and `formerIds` are not properties: they are kept in the body below the notes. `control` and `boundary` are not approved and are not carried. Where they came from: `element-types.yaml` lists them in `commonProperties`, `.obsidian/types.json` types them as text, 24 templates carry them empty, and Ruleset 1.21 section 3 says only that `control` is independent of type and boundary. No Decision Log entry approves them, no value list or definition exists for either, and no EA field feeds them. Spencer did not know what they were. Consequences not yet applied: the templates, `element-types.yaml` `commonProperties`, `types.json`, Ruleset 1.21 section 3, the `id` snippet and `next-id.js` (both read `formerIds` from the properties to find the highest number) and AI_INSTRUCTIONS step 3 (same scan) all assume the old set. Not decided: the name and format of the body section that holds `aliases` and `formerIds`, whether `control` and `boundary` are removed from the schema or only left out of translated notes, and whether the property set is defined once (for example in `element-types.yaml`) for the translator to read.
