Spencer's reason: the data is unreliable because of the person who entered it. This departs from the recommendation to keep `n.a.` and the one `Low`. `Webasto MBDV Profile::Priority` is `body`: a `- Priority: <value>` line below the EA GUID (W-28), for real values only, which is 118 rows, all on Requirement: Must Have 70, Should Have 22, Could Have 16, `Could Have / Won't Have` 2 and 8 free-text values (for example `Should Have - Timestamp` followed by a `Could Have` line). `unassigned` (2,255) and blank (18) write nothing (W-29). One of the 118 is not a priority: it is the column's MoSCoW legend (254 or more characters, starting `This column categorizes requirements using the MoSCoW prioritization method`). It is written as is, so nothing is lost, and is for the cleanup. 40 of the 121 tags remain `pending`.

**W-90 · 2026-09-28 · Abstraction Layer, Domain, Origin and direction.** `Abstraction Layer` (2,293 rows: System 1,144, Multi 416, Component 297, Stakeholder 254, Module 182), `Domain` (169 real values, all `Multi` on `Module`) and `Origin` (118 real values on Requirement, 12 distinct spellings led by Field Service 45 and Customer/Market 41) are `drop`. `Abstraction Layer` is fixed by the element type on every row (16 types, no exceptions), so it adds nothing beyond `eaType`. `Origin` is dropped although its values are real; this departs from the recommendation to keep it as a body line. `direction` (763 rows, all FlowProperty: inout 496, out 171, in 96) is `structure`: not copied into notes, used by the translator when it maps flows (Spencer: for structure when we are dealing with flows). Its use belongs to the FlowProperty and flow mapping in the element mapping, and the relationship vocabulary is still open. 36 of the 121 tags remain `pending`.

**W-91 · 2026-09-28 · Comment and knowledge tags.** Eight tags are `body`, one line each below the EA GUID (W-28), real values only, written as is: `Product Management Comment` (37 rows, `- Product management comment:`), `Engineering Comment` (10), `Sales Comment` (4), `User Story` (118), `Applicability Comment` (45) and `Webasto MBDV Profile::Applicability Comment` (11), which share one `- Applicability comment:` line, `source` (2, `- Source:`) and `Webasto MBDV Profile::External Reference` (2, `- External reference:`). `In-Links` (16 rows, paths into another EA model) and `risk` (1 row, `Low`) are `drop`. **W-92 · 2026-09-28 · Part identity and procurement tags.** Twelve tags are `body`, one line each below the EA GUID (W-28), real values only: `MFG PN` (101 rows, `- Manufacturer part number:`), `PN` (7) and `PartNumber` (4), which share one `- Part number:` line (they never occur on the same element: 112 elements carry exactly one of the three part-number tags, and EA `Alias` is empty on all 112), `PN Change Level` (3), `Brand` (6), `CMF` (15), `Material` (2, the placeholder `unassigned` writes nothing), `Weight (g)` (2), `Piece Cost` (6), `Quantity` (10), `Positions` (1) and `Style` (13). Spencer's decision: keep all in the body, merge `PN` and `PartNumber` into one part-number line, keep `MFG PN` separate. This departs from the recommendation to drop `Piece Cost`. Known effects: `Piece Cost` has no currency or unit and puts cost values in notes that may be read outside engineering; `Quantity` (10, on Objects) is unexplained and does not overlap the Part quantities of W-43; the meaning of `Style` (`DV`, `PIA1` to `PIA18`) is unknown. 14 of the 121 tags remain `pending`.

**W-93 · 2026-09-28 · Body lines use the EA tag name until after the import.** Every body line taken from a tag is written as `- <EA tag name>: <value>` with the name exactly as in EA, as W-28 says. Spencer's reason: it keeps the import aligned with the EA export and avoids confusion during the import period; any renaming happens after the import. This replaces the readable line names written in W-89, W-91 and W-92. The lines are: `- Webasto MBDV Profile::Priority:`, `- Product Management Comment:`, `- Engineering Comment:`, `- Sales Comment:`, `- User Story:`, `- Applicability Comment:`, `- Webasto MBDV Profile::Applicability Comment:`, `- source:`, `- Webasto MBDV Profile::External Reference:`, `- MFG PN:`, `- Part Number:`, `- PN Change Level:`, `- Brand:`, `- CMF:`, `- Material:`, `- Weight (g):`, `- Piece Cost:`, `- Quantity:`, `- Positions:` and `- Style:`. One exception, chosen by Spencer: `PN` and `PartNumber` share one line named `Part Number` (they never occur on the same element). Two consequences: the two applicability comment tags are no longer merged, so an element with both gets two lines (I did not check whether any does), and the profile prefix stays in the line name. Definitions: only fields left in the model need a definition, not fields used for `structure` (Spencer). Meanings given by Spencer: `Quantity` is likely the number of pieces in a kit (10 rows, not confirmed for each); `Piece Cost` is in US dollars. **W-113 · 2026-09-29 · The Interface class is removed and a Port class is added.** Spencer's decision, made during the Port analysis for the element mapping. His intent, stated with it: bring the EA model into the vault as close to native as possible so no data is lost; map the steps the translator takes so the vault keeps the intent of the EA model; merge ports into the right object only as far as needed to avoid losing data, and leave what remains for the second pass. Reason from the export (`t_object`): typing and redefinition are one hierarchy. Of the 1,774 Ports that have both a type (`PDATA1`) and a redefinition parent (`PDATA2`), the redefined Port has the same interface in 1,750; 18 differ and 6 have none. Applied as consequences (my choices, to confirm): `element-types.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.6) has `Port` (prefix `PORT`, folder `20_Ports`) in the place of `Interface`, with the five subtypes of the removed class unchanged (`electrical & material`, `data`, `mechanical`, `generic physical`, `environmental`); the template `Interface.md` is renamed `Port.md` with `type: Port`; `Snippet - id` maps `Port` to `PORT`; the taxonomy note in `10_EA Native Translator/03_MDSE Elements` has the same row; `ea-element-mapping.yaml` carries a `note` on Port and Class. `oldToolRules` X-03 (emit as Interface) stays as evidence of the old tool. Not decided: where the 469 EA interface Classes go. In the export, 116 of them type Ports, 214 own 752 FlowProperty Parts (direction, typed by a Signal), and only 15 do both. Also open: the subtype of the 46 `Physical Signal` classes and the 8 `Hardware Component` classes that type Ports (neither is in the five subtypes); the merge rules for Part- and Object-owned Ports. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-114 · 2026-09-29 · Rule for merging a Part's or Object's Ports into the object's Port.** Spencer approved the rule proposed in this chat. EA models an abstract object with Ports, a specific object that inherits and may redefine or add Ports, and a Part (an instance of the specific object in an assembly) whose Ports belong to the specific object. The Part is not kept as a separate thing. For each Port owned by a Part or an Object, with the block that owner is typed by (Part: `PDATA1`; Object: `Classifier`), one hop only and never up the redefinition chain (`PDATA2`): (1) if `PDATA3` names a Port owned by that block, merge into it (1,697); (2) else if the block has exactly one Port with the same non-blank name, merge into it (59); (3) else add the Port to the block as a Port of its own (671; 617 new Ports after same-named ones on one block combine), to be cleaned up in stage 2; (4) if the owner has no block, the Port stays where it is (26: 24 Parts with no `PDATA1`, 2 with a `PDATA1` missing from the export). Connectors drawn on the assembly move to the block's Port and keep the assembly and Part as context on their line. Checked against the export: every Part-owned Port with a block resolves to a Class (1,930 of 1,930); modelled result 4,387 Ports become 2,589, 0 self-loops, 63 connectors in 18 groups end on the same pair of Ports (45 become duplicates), and in all 18 groups the members were drawn on different instance Ports, so none is a repeat of the same drawing; of the 63, 12 have a Name and 24 a stereotype. Following the redefinition chain would have created 55 false self-loops and is not used. Not checked: Part `PDATA1` also points at Signal (532), Activity (142) and State (61) for Parts that own no Ports, so the block rule is for Port owners only. The line format for a connector's assembly context is not decided. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-115 · 2026-09-29 · Requirement subtype rule, a new `engineering` subtype, and Regulatory InformationItems become standard requirements.** Spencer's decisions. (1) Requirements get their `subtype` from the EA package path, the earlier tool's rule R-01 (D-049) with one change: Engineering Requirements is a new subtype `engineering` (it was `stakeholder` in R-01), because much work will be done on these and they must be identifiable together. Rule, first match wins: `03 Product Requirement / Regulatory Requirements` standard; `03 Product Requirement / Engineering Requirements` engineering; `03 Product Requirement / Design Guides` and `05 Product Design` design; `04 Product Function` functional; any other package stakeholder; the stereotypes `functionalRequirement` (functional) and `designConstraint` (design) override the package. (2) All InformationItem elements in the package `03 Product Requirement / Regulatory Requirements` (2,383 of 2,825, in 19 packages that also hold 8,557 Requirements) become Requirement notes with subtype `standard`. They carry the same Webasto MBDV Profile tags as the regulatory Requirements; their `Object Type` tag is `Information` on 2,253 and `information` on 1, none is a Heading; 29 have a connector (61 connectors: Dependency 36, Nesting 25); 2,329 have a Note. `eaType` (W-31) keeps `InformationItem` on them, so they stay identifiable during review. The other 442 InformationItems (Market Requirements 196, Product Definition folders and others) are not decided. Counts under the rule: Requirement elements standard 8,535, engineering 2,469, stakeholder 1,744, design 742, functional 498 (13,988); with the 2,383 InformationItems, 16,371 requirement notes: standard 10,918, engineering 2,469, stakeholder 1,744, design 742, functional 498. Correction to my earlier count: 35 elements in folders the rule does not name was wrong; only 16 fall to `stakeholder` by default alone (Use Case 'Who' 7, Third Party Testing 2, and 7 others); the rest of that figure were stereotype overrides. Applied as consequences: `element-types.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.7) lists `engineering` as a fifth Requirement subtype; the taxonomy note has the same row; `ea-element-mapping.yaml` sets Requirement to `note`, `mdseType: Requirement`, `decision: W-115`, with the `subtypeRule`, and records the InformationItem part decision. Reading to confirm: the stereotype override does not apply inside Engineering Requirements, so all 2,469 elements there are `engineering`, including 83 `designConstraint`, 42 `functionalRequirement` and 1 `extendedRequirement`; their stereotype stays in `eaType`. Not decided: the `Requirement` template's default subtype (`functional`); whether the 16 default-stakeholder elements should be reviewed; the designator rule for the InformationItem names (2 are identical to a regulatory Requirement name, 2 repeat among themselves; the parse rule per source document is still open, W-82). Sits with W-81: every requirement is its own note, and folding is stage 2. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-116 · 2026-09-29 · Engineering Requirements are all `engineering`; the stereotype override does not apply there.** Spencer confirmed the reading in W-115. All 2,469 elements in `03 Product Requirement / Engineering Requirements` get subtype `engineering`, including the 83 `designConstraint`, 42 `functionalRequirement` and 1 `extendedRequirement`; their stereotype stays in `eaType`. Spencer's reason: being filed under Function or Design in EA does not make them true functional or design requirements, and they are to be sorted out in the work on the `engineering` set. Consequence: the subtype counts in W-115 stand (engineering 2,469, design 742, functional 498). The wording in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` no longer says "reading to confirm". The remaining open items for Requirement are the `Requirement` template's default subtype (`functional`) and the designator rule.

**W-117 · 2026-09-29 · The folder tree starts at the ten packages under `IPC !`.** Spencer's decision, at the start of the package-by-package walk through the requirement packages. The packages `Model` (root, `Package_ID` 1) and `IPC !` (`Package_ID` 3) are not folders in the vault; the vault tree starts with the ten packages below `IPC !` (`00 Product Abstract`, `00 Product Definition`, `01 Product Use Case`, `02 Product Context`, `03 Product Requirement`, `04 Product Function`, `05 Product Design`, `06 Product Validation`, `07 Product Assembly`, `09 Product in Progress`). Spencer's reason: no need for several parent folders above the main folders. What is set aside: both packages hold nothing of their own (no `Notes`, no diagrams, no elements except the Package rows of their children, Author `skellys` on the `IPC !` row); their rows and GUIDs stay in the export and in the ledger (W-35). It is one rule for the folder path: the package path of a note is the EA path with the first two levels removed. Where it touches earlier work: W-04 (folders follow the EA packages) now reads from the ten packages down; W-115 tests the EA package path by name (`03 Product Requirement / ...`), which is unchanged because it tests the EA path, not the vault path. Not decided: the package as a note (W-66) and the replacement of unsafe characters (W-48, W-65). (2) The requirement rules are settled folder by folder: the EA package tree is walked from the top in EA name order and, for each package, Spencer is shown the package, the rules that already apply and the element types and stereotypes inside, and then sets the rule. Each package's result is recorded in a new worklist, `99_System/03_Schemas/ea-package-rules.yaml` (entries for `Model` and `IPC !` so far); the element rules stay in `ea-element-mapping.yaml`. The rules of W-115 and W-116 still stand; the walk may refine them per folder. Correction: a first version of this entry, committed a minute earlier, said the tree starts at `IPC !` with `IPC !` as a folder; that did not match Spencer's decision and is replaced by this entry, so W-117 is one entry. The walk starts with `00 Product Abstract` (Package_ID 3982).

**W-118 · 2026-09-29 · `00 Product Abstract` is a folder only.** Spencer's decision in the package walk. The package `00 Product Abstract` (Package_ID 3982, IPC ! / 00 Product Abstract) becomes a folder in the vault and gets no note. It holds no elements, no diagrams and no `Notes` of its own, only 6 child packages (`01 System`, `02 Module`, `03 Assembly`, `04 Component`, `05 Interfaces & Signals`, `06 Properties`), so no element rule applies at this level. Scope: this settles this one package only; whether packages in general become notes stays open (W-66), and `06 Properties` (an empty package that has a `Notes` description) is not decided. Recorded in `ea-package-rules.yaml`.

**W-119 · 2026-09-29 · Back to deciding by element type; Requirements are skipped.** Spencer's decision. The package-by-package walk (W-117, W-118) is paused: the rules depend on the element type, not the package, and the same types repeat in every package (the 56 Use Cases in `01 System` are the same as in every other package), so each `eaType` is settled once, the first time it comes up. Requirements are left out of this pass because their subtype is driven by package placement, and that is already set (W-115, W-116). What stands: `Model` and `IPC !` are not folders (W-117), `00 Product Abstract` is a folder only (W-118), and `ea-package-rules.yaml` keeps those three entries; further package rules are added only where a package needs an exception. Effects: the earlier tier list (A 8,505, B 17,400, C 10,064 elements) is the working order and is a proposal only; `01 System` (Package_ID 1244) was shown and no rule was set for it. First element types: Port and Object, because the three postponed fields (`Classifier`, `Classifier_guid`, `PDATA1`) depend on them (W-96).

**W-120 · 2026-09-29 · The interface Classes become Port notes, and the typing link becomes `subtypeOf`.** Spencer's decision. The 423 Class elements with an interface stereotype (Electrical & Material Interface 205, Data Interface 112, Mechanical Interface 69, Generic Physical Interface 30, Environmental Interface 7) become notes of type Port. Their subtype is the stereotype (`electrical & material`, `data`, `mechanical`, `generic physical`, `environmental`), the five subtypes W-113 kept. A Port's `PDATA1` (typing Class) is written as `subtypeOf` between Port notes, so "this port is a kind of Contact" is one link between two Port notes and no separate Interface layer exists. This carries out W-113. Evidence from the export: of the 3,425 `PDATA1` links to a Class, 3,328 point at one of these 423 Classes (115 distinct ones); the 209 Generalization connectors between them become `subtypeOf` under the existing relationship rule; 63 other connectors touch them; 30 Ports are owned by them; 752 FlowProperty Parts are owned by 214 of them. Applied as consequences: `ea-element-mapping.yaml` records the decision on Class (partial) and a note on Port. Not decided, and what it leaves open: (1) 97 Port typing links point at a Hardware Component Class (an Object, not a Port note), 2 at a Physical Signal (a Signal element) and 5 at a GUID missing from the export, so `subtypeOf` between Port notes does not cover those 104 links; (2) the 752 FlowProperty Parts on the interface Classes (direction, typed by a Signal) have no target yet; (3) the other Class stereotypes; (4) `PDATA2` (redefinition, 1,906 links), `Classifier` on Ports (920, all to another Port), the owner link `hasPort`, and the Port stereotypes `ProxyPort` (303) and `FullPort` (13); (5) the merge of W-114 loses instance-side values on the block port (39 instance ports with a different typing Class, 89 with a different stereotype, 99 with a different name, 12 with an Alias), which need a line on the block port note; (6) W-45 archives `PDATA2` and `PDATA3`, which the Port mapping now uses. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-121 · 2026-09-29 · Differences on a merged instance Port are kept as lines on the block Port note.** Spencer's decision. When an instance Port (owned by a Part or an Object) merges into the block's Port under W-114, any real value on the instance Port that differs from the block Port is written as a line on the block Port's note, so the merge loses nothing. Only real, differing values are written (W-28, W-29); identical values and blank values add nothing. Exact scope, over the 1,756 instance Ports that merge (1,697 through `PDATA3`, 59 by exact name): name differs on 101 (a non-blank instance name that is not the block Port's name; 462 instance Ports have no name, so nothing is lost there), typing Class (`PDATA1`) differs on 39, stereotype differs on 8 (the instance is a `ProxyPort` and the block Port is plain; in the 81 opposite cases the instance has no stereotype, so nothing is lost), redefinition link (`PDATA2`) differs on 23. Alias differs on none: 12 instance Ports carry an Alias and all 12 equal the block Port's. In all, 164 instance Ports (on 117 block Ports) have at least one real difference. Correction to my earlier figures in this chat: I said 239 differences (99 names, 89 stereotypes, 39 types, 12 aliases). That counted every stereotype difference, including the 81 where the instance simply has none, and the 12 aliases that are the same on both. The real count is above. The 23 redefinition differences were not in the earlier figures. Not decided: the line format and where on the note it goes (the source section or above it), and how the assembly and Part where the value was drawn are named on the line. The same question applies to the connector context in W-114. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-122 · 2026-09-29 · A Port's redefinition link (`PDATA2`) becomes `subtypeOf`, with the odd ones flagged.** Spencer's decision. Every `PDATA2` link on a Port note that survives the W-114 merge is written as `subtypeOf` to the Port it names, exactly as EA states it, without judging it. Evidence from the export, after the merge: 2,631 Port notes survive (4,387 minus 1,756 merged), 1,257 of them carry `PDATA2`; none of the targets disappears into another note and none points at itself; 1,111 target a Port on an ancestor block (following the Generalization connectors), 143 a Port on a block with no family link to this one, and 3 a Port on the same block. The 146 links that do not follow the block family (143 and 3) get a flag line so they can be found for the stage 2 clean-up; the wording of the flag is not decided (Task 1 of `Post-Import Tasks.md` uses `REVIEW port needed` for a similar case, W-55). The `PDATA2` values of merged instance Ports are not written as links: they are blank or equal to the block Port's, except the 23 that differ, which W-121 keeps as lines. Applied as consequences: `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` gets a `portsDecision` on `PDATA1` (W-120) and on `PDATA2` (W-122); their field-level disposition for other element types is unchanged, so W-45 (archive) stands for Requirements and the rest. Not decided: the flag wording; the owner link (there is no `hasPort` in `relationships.yaml`); `Classifier` on Ports; the 104 typing links that do not point at a Port note; the Port stereotypes; the 752 FlowProperty Parts. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-123 · 2026-09-29 · `hasPort` and `portOf`: the owner link of a Port.** Spencer's decision. A new stored relationship pair, `hasPort` (forward, written on the owner's note) and `portOf` (inverse, generated on the Port note), is added to `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.3) and to `.obsidian/types.json` as `multitext`. It follows the storage rule of `relationships.yaml`: forward fields are written on the owner side and inverses are generated. Every Port note has one owner, taken from `ParentID` after the W-114 merge (so a merged instance Port belongs to the block, not to the Part). Owners of the 2,589 Port notes: a block note 2,520 (1,903 already on the block plus 617 added by the merge), another Port note 30 (the Port owned by an interface Class, now a Port note, W-120), an ActivityPartition 42, a Part with no block 26, an Actor 1. Ports per block: median 3, maximum 38 (`Circuit - LPC1788FBD208,551 PCE25`), 4 blocks over 20. Consequences: the 42 Ports owned by an ActivityPartition get their `hasPort` link when that element has a mapping (undecided, Tier C), and until then the Port note carries a flag line, wording not decided (W-122 leaves the flag wording open too); the 26 Ports owned by a Part with no block are owned by the Object note that Part becomes (W-54), not by a block. The alternative not chosen was a table on the block note that lists its Ports through Dataview, which stores no link. Not decided: `Classifier` on Ports; the 104 typing links that do not reach a Port note; the Port stereotypes (`ProxyPort` 303, `FullPort` 13); the 752 FlowProperty Parts; the flag wording; the line format of W-121. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-124 · 2026-09-29 · `copyOf` and `hasCopy`: a general link from a copy to its original.** Spencer's decision. A new stored relationship pair, `copyOf` (forward, written on the copy) and `hasCopy` (inverse, generated on the original), is added to `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.4) and to `.obsidian/types.json` as `multitext`. Spencer's reasons: it can be used wherever an element is a copy of another element for any reason, and it lets a reader trace back to the original note. It replaces `instanceOf`, which I proposed for the `Classifier` link on Ports. It is used first for the merged Ports whose `Classifier` names a Port other than the one the merge uses (W-114, W-121). Exact scope, checked against the export after the merge: of the 479 merged instance Ports that carry `Classifier`, 274 name the same Port the merge already uses (nothing to write), and 205 name another Port. Correction to my last message, which called the 205 lost: for 203 of them the other Port also merges into the same block Port, so the link would join a note to itself and there is nothing to write; only 2 point at a different note (`Vehicle Comm` on `Circuitry - Wired Comms`, from unnamed Ports of the blocks `Circuitry - Wired Comms Interface Ramprunner` and `... PVS`), and those 2 become `copyOf`. Reading to confirm: for the 203 nothing is written, since a note cannot be a copy of itself; the EA link stays in the export. Not decided: `Classifier` on the 441 Ports that survive as notes (387 added to a block as their own Port, 12 on a block, 42 on an ActivityPartition); the placement and line format for the 2 (W-121 family); the flag wording; `copyOf` for other element types (Part, Object and others have their own decisions). Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-125 · 2026-09-29 · A temporary property pair, `hasClassifier` and `classifierOf`, keeps the Port `Classifier` link during the import.** Spencer's decision, to keep the import clean. The EA `Classifier` field on a Port is written on the Port note as `hasClassifier`, pointing at the Port it names; `classifierOf` is the generated inverse. The pair is temporary: it is resolved in a later stage into `copyOf`/`hasCopy` (W-124) or another relationship, and then removed. It is recorded in `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.5) under `temporaryPairs`, and in `.obsidian/types.json` as `multitext`. This changes the plan of W-124 for `Classifier`: `copyOf` is no longer written by the import for these links. Scope, from the export after the merge: 441 Port notes that survive carry `Classifier` (387 added to a block as their own Port, 42 on an ActivityPartition, 12 on a block); the 2 merged instance Ports whose `Classifier` names a Port in a different note (W-124) also get `hasClassifier`, on the block Port they merged into (reading to confirm: the same rule for the whole field, one line of logic instead of two). The 274 that repeat the merge target and the 203 that would join a note to itself write nothing; their EA link stays in the export. Not decided: what the later stage turns `hasClassifier` into for each case; whether `hasClassifier` is listed with `eaType` as a translated-only property (`element-types.yaml` lists only `eaType`); a property definition note for it (W-19, `Definitions/Properties`); the placement of relationship fields in the properties. The last one is a conflict to settle: W-97 and `Note Layout.md` say only `tags` holds several values, while `hasPort` (W-123), `subtypeOf` (W-120, W-122) and `hasClassifier` hold lists. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-126 · 2026-09-29 · Relationship fields sit in the properties after `tags`, and may hold lists.** Spencer's decision. W-97 is amended: the properties keep their order (`type`, `subtype`, `id`, `uid`, `status`, `eaType`, `tags`), and the relationship fields follow `tags`, in the order they appear in `relationships.yaml` from top to bottom. `tags` and the relationship fields may hold more than one value; the other properties hold one each. Reason: it matches the storage rule in `relationships.yaml` (forward fields written on the owner side, inverses generated and kept in the YAML), and the fields in use so far are lists: `hasPort` (up to 38 entries on one block, W-123), `subtypeOf` (W-120, W-122), `hasClassifier` (W-125) and `copyOf` (W-124). Applied as consequences: `Definitions/Note Layout.md` (rule and example), `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md` step 1, `element-types.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.8, a `relationshipFields` line) and the handoff. The W-97 entry is not edited (history); this entry replaces its multi-value rule. Not decided: whether a relationship field with no value is written (the templates carry `tags: []` empty, W-88, but a note has few of the many relationship fields); the order inside the block for the pairs and the temporary pair; whether the templates list any relationship fields; I did not check how the Nodian plugin reads them. Not applied: Ruleset 1.21 section 3, the translator and existing notes.

**W-127 · 2026-09-29 · The 104 Port typing links that do not reach a Port note.** Spencer's decision. Of the 3,432 `PDATA1` links on Ports, 104 do not point at an interface Class (W-120): 97 point at 8 Hardware Component Classes (`Bus Bar` 46 Ports, `Connector - PCBA 30 position` 12, `Connector - USB` 10, and 5 other connectors), 2 point at a Physical Signal (`psRS232`, on 2 Ports named `RS232`), and 5 name a GUID that is not in the export (`EV Connector`, `PCBA:Suspension` and three `Bend Relief` Ports). Decided: the 99 whose target exists are written as the temporary `hasClassifier` (W-125) on the Port note, pointing at that element's note once it is mapped (Hardware Component is an Object note, W-32 mapping open for the Signal); a later stage decides what they become. The 5 with a missing target keep their GUID as a line in the `Source: EA` section, so the reference is not lost; there is no note to link to. Reason: a `subtypeOf` from a Port to a Bus Bar would say the Port is a kind of bus bar, which is not what EA says; the port is typed by a physical part. Applied as consequences: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.6) now describes `hasClassifier` as covering the Port `Classifier` field and these `PDATA1` links; `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` records it on `PDATA1`. Worth knowing: `hasClassifier` now carries two EA fields, so a later stage cannot tell from the property which field a link came from, only from the EA export (which keeps both). Not decided: the wording of the source-section line for the 5 (it follows the `- <EA name>: <value>` pattern of W-28 with the EA field name `PDATA1`); the mapping of Signal (Physical Signal 46 and the rest); what `hasClassifier` turns into later. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-128 · 2026-09-29 · Port stereotypes become the subtypes `proxy` and `full`.** Spencer's decision. The Port subtype list gains `proxy` and `full`, so it is now `electrical & material`, `data`, `mechanical`, `generic physical`, `environmental`, `proxy`, `full`. A Port element with the stereotype `ProxyPort` gets subtype `proxy`, `FullPort` gets `full`, and a Port with no stereotype gets a blank subtype. Where several instance Ports combine into one new Port on a block (W-114), the note takes the stereotype if any of them has one; a plain Port has no value, so nothing is lost; this affects 2 of the 617 new Ports (one plain copy and one `ProxyPort` copy each). `eaType` keeps the EA stereotype while it exists (W-31). From the export, after the merge: 2,631 Port elements survive (none 2,401, `ProxyPort` 217, `FullPort` 13); 62 of the 617 new Ports on blocks are `ProxyPort`; the 8 merged instance Ports that are a `ProxyPort` on a block Port that is plain are kept as lines under W-121. A note comes from one EA element, so it never needs two values: a note made from an interface Class (W-120) gets an interface subtype, a note made from a Port gets a port kind or none. The cost is that the list now holds two ideas, the kind of interface and the kind of port, and one note cannot say both. Applied as consequences: `element-types.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.9), the taxonomy note and `ea-element-mapping.yaml`. Not changed: the `Port.md` template still defaults to `electrical & material`. Found while checking, not decided: `isConjugated` is held in `t_xref` for 31 Ports (24 true, 7 false, all with no stereotype); 21 of them survive as their own note and 10 merge into a block Port, where W-121 does not cover it. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-129 · 2026-09-29 · `isConjugated` is kept as a line in the source section of Port notes.** Spencer's decision. The EA custom property `isConjugated` (`t_xref`, kind CustomProperties, disposition `structure` in W-84, now `body`) is written as `- isConjugated: true` or `- isConjugated: false` in the `Source: EA` section of the Port note, real recorded values only, following the `- <EA name>: <value>` pattern of W-28. Values are written as true and false, converted from EA's -1 and 0 (my reading, confirmed by the yes to the proposal). From the export: 32 rows on 31 Ports (24 rows -1, 8 rows 0; one Port, `3.3VDC Out`, has the same 0 row twice), so 24 true and 7 false Ports. After the W-114 merge, 21 Port notes carry a value (15 true, 6 false); the other 10 merge into block Ports that already hold the same value, so W-121 needs nothing for them. The other 4,356 Ports have no recorded value and write nothing; they are the EA default (false), which is not written. None of the 31 is a `ProxyPort` or `FullPort`. Applied as consequences: the `isConjugated` entry in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` (kind CustomProperties) changes from `structure` to `body`, placement source section. Not decided: the order of this line against the other `Source: EA` lines (open since W-107); what a later stage does with it; how direction is modelled. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-130 · 2026-09-29 · Four fixed review lines for Port cases.** Spencer approved the four flags. The import writes each as one line in the `Source: EA` section of the Port note, worded exactly as below so one search finds every case (the form follows `REVIEW port needed`, W-55): `REVIEW subtypeOf: target port is not on an ancestor block` on the 146 Ports whose redefinition link (W-122) does not follow the block family (143 to a Port on an unrelated block, 3 on the same block); `REVIEW port: added from an assembly, no matching port on the block` on the Port notes made by the third step of the W-114 rule (617 new Ports from 659 instance Ports); `REVIEW owner: ActivityPartition has no mapping yet` on the 42 Ports owned by an ActivityPartition (W-123); `REVIEW block: owner Part has no block` on the 26 Ports owned by a Part with no block (24 with no `PDATA1`, 2 with a `PDATA1` missing from the export, W-114). 831 lines in all. Applied as a consequence, listed as required: `Post-Import Tasks.md` has a new Task 6, "Clear the Port review flags", with how to find each line, how to resolve one and when it is done, following the instruction in that file to add new tasks at the bottom. The vault is not released until it is finished (W-55). Not decided: the order of these lines against the other `Source: EA` lines (open since W-107); whether the ActivityPartition line goes when that element is mapped by the translator itself. Counts will change if the Port rules change. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-131 · 2026-09-29 · Signal elements become Item Flow notes.** Spencer's decision. All 623 Signal elements (Logical Signal 565, Physical Signal 46, Environmental Effect 8, Software Parameter 4) become notes of type Item Flow with a blank `subtype`; the EA stereotype stays in `eaType`. This follows the translator principle in the Item Flow definition (`MDSE Element - Item Flow.md`): a stereotype does not create a subtype on its own, and the subtype stays blank until a review. Evidence from the export: the Signals are linked by 579 Generalization connectors (a taxonomy), only 118 of them are named by a FlowProperty, 505 are used by nothing yet, and 3 have a Note. Consequences to know: 505 Item Flow notes have no user until the flows are modelled; `eaType` is temporary (W-31), so after review the Logical, Physical and Environmental distinction survives only in the export unless a subtype carries it; the 2 Port typing links to the Physical Signal `psRS232` (W-127) now point at an Item Flow note. The old tool's rule SG-01 suppressed all Signals, so the old rule is no guide. Applied as a consequence: the Signal entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` is `note`, Item Flow, blank subtype. Found while checking, not decided: `99_System/10_EA Native Translator/03_MDSE Elements/MDSE Element - Interface.md` still defines the removed Interface type (W-113) and needs a Port definition, as does `CANVAS - Interface Translation.canvas`. Not decided: the 752 FlowProperty Parts (how a pin, its direction and its Signal are written on the Port note; the word `hasflow` in Spencer's example is a candidate relationship); the 82 unnamed FlowProperty; the 8 typed by a Class. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-132 · 2026-09-29 · Each FlowProperty becomes its own Item Flow note.** Spencer's decision (option A). Each of the 760 Part elements with the stereotype `FlowProperty` (a pin on an interface: name, direction, sometimes the Signal that moves) becomes a note of type Item Flow, one per element, in stage 1; folding is left to stage 2, as for requirements (W-81). Direction stays structure (W-90) and is carried by the shape of the model with the existing `source` and `target` relationships: an `out` pin has its Port as `source`, an `in` pin has its Port as `target`, an `inout` pin has the Port as both `source` and `target`. A pin typed by a Signal (516) or a Class (8) links to that element's note through the temporary `hasClassifier` (W-125), from its `PDATA1`; the 236 untyped pins have no such link. These 760 Parts are not merged into blocks: W-44 (`PDATA1` holds the block GUID) is for the Parts that are instances of a block, and here `PDATA1` is the Signal or Class. From the export: 752 pins are owned by 214 interface Classes (Port notes, W-120), 1 by a Package (`Delta arc`), 7 by nothing; direction is exactly one value each (inout 496, out 171, in 96; 763 rows, 3 of them duplicates of one pin); 678 named, 82 unnamed, 2 names repeated inside one owner; no Notes, 2 Aliases; 19 Sequence connectors touch them. Item Flow now has 1,383 notes (623 Signals, W-131, plus these 760). Applied as a consequence: `ea-element-mapping.yaml` records it as a partial decision on Part. Not decided: how a Port note points at its pins (the relationship name; `carries` and `flowsOn` exist in `relationships.yaml` without a definition, and `hasFlow` is Spencer's word from his example); the note names for the 82 unnamed pins and the 2 repeated names (open duplicate-name rule, W-48); what owns the 1 Package-owned and 7 ownerless pins; how `inout` reads with one Port at both ends; the 19 Sequence connectors; the 2 Aliases (`Alias` is a `- Alias:` line, W-42); the other 2,377 Parts. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-133 · 2026-09-29 · `hasFlow` and `flowOf`: a Port note points at its pin notes.** Spencer's decision. A new stored relationship pair, `hasFlow` (forward, written on the Port note) and `flowOf` (inverse, generated on the Item Flow note made from a pin, W-132), is added to `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.7) directly after `hasPort`/`portOf`, so it also sits there in a note's properties (order of the file, W-126), and to `.obsidian/types.json` as `multitext`. It is ownership only; direction stays with `source` and `target` on the Item Flow note (W-90, W-132). Recommended over reusing `carries`/`flowsOn`, which have no definition and could be read as either ownership or direction. A guidance line in `relationships.yaml` records the meaning. Scope: 752 pins are owned by 214 Port notes (median 2, maximum 49), so 752 `hasFlow` entries. The 8 pins with no interface owner (1 in the Package `Delta arc`, 7 with no owner) get no `hasFlow`. Not decided: whether those 8 get a review line (W-130 has none for them); the other flow items left open in W-132. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-134 · 2026-09-29 · Names of Port notes and pin notes.** Spencer's decision. A Port note is named `<owner name> - <port name>` and a pin note (the Item Flow note made from a FlowProperty, W-132) is named `<Port note name> - <pin name>`. The owner already identifies the note, so the only clashes left are repeats inside one owner. Repeats and blank names take a counter in EA creation order (W-14). Reading to confirm: for a repeated name the first keeps the plain form and later ones add ` 2`, ` 3` and so on; a blank name becomes `port 1`, `port 2` (pins: `pin 1`, `pin 2`) counted inside the owner. From the export, after the merge (W-114): 2,589 Port notes, of which 426 have no name (376 added to a block, 42 owned by an ActivityPartition, 8 on a block) and 130 (54 groups) repeat a name inside one owner; 23 names hold characters unsafe in a file name; 446 of 903 distinct Port names are also used on another owner, which is why the owner goes in the name. Pins: 82 have no name, 2 repeat a name inside one owner, and 143 of 229 distinct pin names occur on more than one owner. Consequences: (1) the original EA name is kept as `- Name: <original>` in the source section (W-48, W-99) on every Port and pin note that has an EA name, since the file name always differs from it now, about 2,163 Port notes and 678 pin notes; a blank name writes no line. (2) Names get long (an owner name such as `Circuit - LPC1788FBD208,551 PCE25` is repeated in each of its 38 Port note names); I did not check file-name or path length limits. (3) The replacement of unsafe characters and the duplicate-name suffix for other element types stay open (W-48, W-65). (4) An owner with no note name yet (the 42 ActivityPartition owners, the 26 Parts with no block, and any owner whose own name is blank) has no rule for its name part: the owner's EA name is used, and a blank one waits for the rule for that element type. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-135 · 2026-09-29 · Each EA Object becomes its own Object note.** Spencer's decision. All 545 Object elements (no stereotype on any) become notes of type Object with a blank `subtype`; none is folded into its blueprint in stage 1, and folding is a stage 2 task (as for requirements, W-81). Reason: an Object carries its own data, unlike a Part: 46 have a Note, 360 tag rows (`MFG PN` 111, `CMF` 27, `Style` 25, `Quantity` 24, `PN` 18), 588 connectors touch 367 of them (Aggregation 248, Sequence 90, Abstraction 77, Association 58), and 189 are nested under another Object. Fields: `Classifier` (the blueprint Class) is written as the temporary `hasClassifier` (W-125) for the 498 that resolve to a Class (Hardware Component 214, Physical Component 126, System Partner 47, Software Component 45, Physical System Variant 27, Module 27, block 6). The 47 whose blueprint ID is not an element in the export keep that value as a line in the `Source: EA` section (as W-127 does for missing GUIDs). `Classifier_guid` is the same reference (545 non-blank, 498 resolve) and is not written separately. `PDATA1` (166 non-blank) repeats the `Classifier` blueprint in 160, so it writes nothing there; the other 6 (3 that name a different Class or a GUID not in the export, and 3 whose `Classifier` does not resolve) keep a source-section line. This settles the three set-aside fields (W-96) for Ports (W-120, W-125, W-127) and Objects; `Classifier` on UseCase (23), ActivityPartition (22), Action (18) and Sequence (1), and `PDATA1` on Requirement (12,089), Issue (264), State (253), Activity (118) and others, are still open. Applied as consequences: the Object entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` is `note`, Object, blank subtype; `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` carries an `objectsDecision` on `Classifier`, `Classifier_guid` and `PDATA1`. Not decided: the 84 unnamed Objects (W-134 covers Ports and pins only); the link for nesting under another Object (189), a Class (54), a UseCase (36) or an ActivityPartition (3); the Object subtype list has no `part`, while W-54 says unlinked Parts become Object notes with subtype Part, a conflict for the Part decision; the 263 Objects with no owner. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-136 · 2026-09-29 · Parts that represent a Class fold into the assembly as a `hasPart` entry, and keep their own data as a line.** Spencer's decision, on the picture of a car (the assembly) with sticky notes (the Parts) that point at blueprint cards (the blocks). Of the 2,377 Parts that are not FlowProperty, 2,055 point at a Class through `PDATA1` (1,075 different blocks). For each of them the assembly note gets a `hasPart` entry pointing at the block note (`hasPart`/`partOf`, W-43, W-44). Each Part with something of its own also gets one line in the assembly's `Source: EA` section carrying it: 469 have a role name that differs from the block name (for example `Charger` for `MHE Charger`), 70 a quantity (`Multiplicity`), 9 tag values (`Piece Cost`, `PN`, `Weight (g)`, `Dimensions` and others), 3 a `Style`; 515 Parts in all, of which 512 are under a Class or Part assembly now and 3 wait. The other 1,540 Parts write the link only. This keeps the role names and tag values that W-43 ("the child is named by its block") would have dropped. Reading to confirm: one `hasPart` entry per Part, so a block drawn 4 times in one assembly appears 4 times; the alternative of one entry per block would lose that count silently, because 1,850 Parts under a Class or Part assembly make only 1,544 distinct (assembly, block) pairs and 514 Parts repeat a pair. Assembly: the parent Class (1,581), or for a Part nested under a Part, that Part's block (269), as W-43 says; that gives 1,850 Parts with an assembly note. 205 Parts sit under an Object (177) or an ActivityPartition (28) and wait until those owners are mapped (3 of them have data). One Part has the same note as assembly and block, which would be a link from a note to itself; it needs a flag. Applied as a consequence: the Part entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` records it. Not decided: the wording of the source-section line (the quantity keeps the W-43 wording `quantity of <block or part> is: <value>`; the rest is open); the other 322 Parts (Activity 142, State 61, Signal 16, `PDATA1` missing from the export 98, none 5), the choice of block when `PDATA1`, `Classifier` and `Classifier_guid` differ (W-43), the 107 connectors that touch 90 Parts (98 Connector, 9 InformationFlow; Task 1 covers the block-level flow connectors), and the conflict between W-54 (`Object` subtype Part) and the Object subtype list. I did not check whether the tools that read the properties merge duplicate list entries. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-137 · 2026-09-29 · Parts that point at an Activity, a State or a Signal become `hasPart` entries on their owner.** Spencer's decision. Of the 322 Parts not typed by a Class, 219 are pure references that carry no data (no name, quantity, connector, diagram, tag or Note): 142 sit inside an Activity and point at another Function (Hardware Function 73, Software Function 33, System Function 29), 61 sit inside a State and point at a System State, 16 sit inside a Port and point at a Physical Signal. For each, the owner's note gets one `hasPart` entry pointing at the note of the element the Part references, one entry per Part as in W-136; no line is written. Dependence on other mappings: the 16 Signal ones are ready (the owner is a Port note, W-120, the target an Item Flow note, W-131). The 142 and 61 need the Activity and State mappings, which are not decided (the Activity owners may be any Activity, including those with no stereotype or `testCase`), so those entries are written once the two elements have a mapping, and until then they are not written. Applied as a consequence: the Part entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` records it. Not decided: the other 103 Parts (`PDATA1` missing from the export 98, none 5; W-43 says Object note with subtype Part, which conflicts with the Object subtype list); whether a Port's Signal Part should use `hasFlow` instead of `hasPart` (left as `hasPart`). Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-138 · 2026-09-29 · Parts with no usable blueprint become Object notes with subtype `part`, named with a counter.** Spencer's decision. The 103 Parts that have no usable block (98 whose `PDATA1` names a GUID that is not in the export, 5 with no `PDATA1`) become notes of type Object with subtype `part`, as W-43 said. `part` is added to the Object subtype list (`element-types.yaml` schemaVersion 1.10, and the taxonomy note), which removes the conflict between W-43 (subtype Part) and the list. Naming, Spencer's rule: an unnamed Part is called `part` plus a counter, `part 1`, `part 2` and so on. Reading to confirm: the counter runs across all of the 89 unnamed Parts in EA creation order (W-14), not inside each owner, and the 14 named ones (9 and 5) keep their EA name. The owner note gets a `hasPart` entry pointing at the new note (W-136); the missing blueprint GUID (98) is kept as a line in the `Source: EA` section (as W-127 and W-135); the 3 quantities are quantity lines on the owner (W-43) and the 3 tag sets stay on the new note. From the export: owners Class 70 and 4, Part 13 and 1, Object 12, Activity 3; 9 named, 3 with a quantity, 2 with a connector, 9 drawn (4 of the 5 unlinked ones drawn); 24 of the 98 have an old `Classifier` that points at another Part with no block. Applied as consequences: `element-types.yaml`, the taxonomy note and the Part entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml`. Not decided: the 205 Parts under an Object or ActivityPartition and the one self-assembly Part (W-136); whether the counter is zero-padded (`part 1` is used, reading to confirm); the naming of the other unnamed Objects (84) and of unnamed elements of other types; the choice of block when `PDATA1`, `Classifier` and `Classifier_guid` differ (W-43); the 107 connectors that touch 90 Parts. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-139 · 2026-09-29 · Class becomes Object, mechanically.** Spencer's decision. Every Class element that is not an interface Class (W-120) becomes a note of type Object with a blank `subtype`, and keeps its EA stereotype in `eaType`; the correction of type and subtype is a stage 2 job after the import. Spencer's reason: keep the translation mechanical. This covers 2,314 elements: Hardware Component 912, block 407, Physical Component 293, System Partner 182, Module 169, Software Component 146, Physical System Variant 123, Physical Context 43, no stereotype 14, Platform 13, Business Line 9, Software Unit 3. Reading to confirm: Physical Context (43, which I had proposed as a Context note) and System Partner (182, which the old tool made an Actor) are not split out; they become Object notes too, and the split is made from `eaType` after the import. With the 423 interface Classes that become Port notes, all 2,737 Class elements now have a mapping. Evidence from the export: these Classes own 1,873 Ports; 1,064 of them are the block of 2,038 Parts and 187 are the class of 498 Objects; and 39 sit inside another Class (the first version of this entry said 1,124 Parts and 200 Objects, which did not match the export and is corrected here); 124 Class names repeat; none has a blank name; tag rows sit on all but the 14 with no stereotype; 111 have a Note. Object notes now come from the 2,314 Classes, the 545 Objects and the 103 Parts with no usable block (W-138): 2,962 in all. Applied as a consequence: the Class entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` is `note`, Object, blank subtype (interface Classes to Port, W-120). Consequences to know: the Context class receives no note from the import, since Physical Context becomes Object; the 124 repeated names need the duplicate-name rule (W-48, open), and Port and pin note names (W-134) start with the owner's name, so they depend on it. Not decided: the subtype values the stage 2 review will set (`electrical`, `circuit`, `mechanical`, `software`, `firmware`, `part` are the only ones the Object list has); the 39 Classes inside another Class (nesting link); the 1,653 Parts and 54 Objects that sit under these Classes are covered by W-136 and W-135. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-140 · 2026-09-29 · UseCase becomes Use Case, with the subtype taken from the folder; `who` is added.** Spencer's decision. All 1,667 UseCase elements (no stereotype on any) become notes of type Use Case. The `subtype` comes from the EA package they sit in under `01 Product Use Case`, the same idea as the Requirement rule (W-115): `01 What` gives `what` (983), `03 When` gives `when` (21), `04 Where` gives `where` (74), `00 Who` gives `who` (232). Every other UseCase has a blank subtype: `Mixed Use Digital` 18, directly in `01 Product Use Case` 12, and 327 in packages outside it (`00 Product Abstract / 02 Module` 95, `00 Product Definition / BMID` 68, `00 Product Abstract / 01 System` 59 and others), 357 in all. So 1,310 have a subtype and 357 do not. There is no `why` folder, so nothing takes `why`. `who` is added to the Use Case subtype list (`element-types.yaml` schemaVersion 1.11 and the taxonomy note), which had `what`, `where`, `why`, `when`. Connectors are imported where drawn (W-55). From the export: 171 have a Note, 1 has a tag row, 7 have no name, 41 names repeat; 3,008 connectors touch them (`extend` 735, `include` 522, Association 500, `refine` 412, Generalization 384, Usage 217, NoteLink 160, `allocate` 30); 1,426 are nested under another UseCase, and under UseCases sit 115 Notes, 62 Issues, 36 Objects, 24 Activities and 14 Requirements; 213 own a diagram; 24 have a `Classifier`. Applied as consequences: `element-types.yaml`, the taxonomy note and the UseCase entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` (with the subtype rule). Not decided: the link for nesting under another UseCase (1,426) and the things nested under UseCases; the relationship vocabulary for `extend`, `include` and `refine` (W-83 and the relationship matrix cover part); `Classifier` on the 24 UseCases (still one of the set-aside fields, W-96); the 7 unnamed UseCases (naming rule for unnamed elements, W-134, W-138); the `why` subtype has no source. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-141 · 2026-09-29 · Activity becomes Function, or Verification for a test case.** Spencer's decision. The 1,668 Activity elements map by `eaType`: `System Function` (252), `Hardware Function` (157), `Software Function` (108) and `Module Function` (13) become Function notes with subtype `system`, `hardware`, `software` and `module`; an Activity with no stereotype (611) becomes a Function note with a blank subtype; `testCase` (527) becomes a Verification note with subtype `test`. That is 1,141 Function notes and 527 Verification notes. `eaType` keeps the stereotype, so a wrong call can be corrected from it in stage 2. From the export: the 530 with a Function stereotype and the 611 with none sit mostly in `04 Product Function` (the 611 also include 90 in `00 Product Definition / GSE`); the `testCase` elements are all in `06 Product Validation` (Import 259, ProCore Edge DV 93, BMID Validation 43, SkyLink Validation 35), carry a `Verdict` tag (527), have a Note (332) and 326 `verify` connectors, and 346 sit inside another Activity. 507 of the 611 with no stereotype have a connector (`satisfy` 714, `allocate` 580, Generalization 380, ControlFlow 213) and 213 Requirements nested under them. The old tool chose Function, Step, Verification, Procedure or Plan from context (rules E-03 to E-05); this is a mechanical reading. Consequences: the 142 Parts that point at an Activity (W-137) can now be written, since the owner Activities and the referenced Functions all have a mapping; the 611 with no stereotype are Function by placement only, and a few in the GSE definition folder may be something else; a `testCase` as Verification is a reading (it could be a Procedure); the Function stereotypes are the four subtype names. Applied as a consequence: the Activity entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` lists the six mappings. Not decided: the link for nesting of Activities under Activities (the 360 under test cases and the Function tree); `PDATA1` on Activity (118, set aside, W-96; no Activity has a `Classifier`, the 18 belong to Actions); the Action, Decision, StateNode and other behaviour elements inside them. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-142 · 2026-09-29 · State: in the Design package it becomes Design, elsewhere State.** Spencer's decision, replacing my proposal to make every State a State note. A State element that sits in the package `05 Product Design` or below it becomes a Design note; every other State element becomes a State note. Counts from the export: 954 in `05 Product Design` (System State 752, no stereotype 202) and 158 elsewhere (System State 17, no stereotype 139, Fault State 2), 1,112 in all; the 158 are in `00 Product Definition` 94, `00 Product Abstract` 37, `02 Product Context` 7, `07 Product Assembly` 7, `04 Product Function` 6, `01 Product Use Case` 5, `03 Product Requirement` 2. The Design States sit in 15 Design packages (Compatibility 268, Electrical Design 115, Software Design 98, Environmental Protection 78, Mechanical Design 77, Communications Design 48 and others) and their names read as design characteristics (`MacOS Compatibility`, `IP 66K`, `Discontinuous Conducted Emissions`). Reading to confirm: both notes have a blank `subtype`; `characteristic` fits the Design ones by name but is not applied mechanically, and the stage 2 review sets it from `eaType` and the folder. The 2 `Fault State` elements become State notes (the old tool made them Failure Modes), which stage 2 can change from `eaType`. Evidence that the split is clean: no nesting crosses it (851 Design States are owned by a Design State, 57 non-Design States by a non-Design State, none across); only 11 Design States are touched by a `StateFlow` connector (11 connectors). Consequences: (1) the 61 Parts that point at a System State (W-137) can now be written, the owners and targets being Design or State notes; (2) Design notes end some `StateFlow` connectors (11), so a Design note can be a transition end, which is odd and is left for the connector rules; (3) 401 Requirements and 60 Parts are nested under Design States. Applied as a consequence: the State entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` lists the two package rules. Not decided: the link for nesting of States under States (851 and 57); the StateFlow and StateNode elements (73 StateNodes, other element types, later); `PDATA1` on State (253, set aside, W-96); the subtype of the Design notes. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-143 · 2026-09-29 · Artifact stays Artifact; the stereotype gives the subtype.** Spencer's decision. The 459 Artifact elements become Artifact notes: `Document` (329) with subtype `document`, `Image` (129) with subtype `image`, and `CustomDocument` (1, `Req Test` in `00 Product Definition / MHE`) with a blank subtype, since its stereotype is not one of the two subtype names. `eaType` keeps the stereotype. The binary attachments of Documents and Images are already settled (W-75 to W-78). From the export: the Documents sit in `03 Product Requirement / Regulatory Requirements` 311, Engineering Requirements 13 and 5 elsewhere, with names such as `GSE_SW Table 2-4 J1772 Signal Levels`; 44 have a Note and 238 a connector (`Dependency` 175, `Nesting` 48, `trace` 26, `refine` 14); the Images sit in `09 Product in Progress / Platform Controller` 111, `00 Product Abstract / 02 Module` 6 and 12 elsewhere, 95 with a connector; 56 Artifact names repeat. Applied as a consequence: the Artifact entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` lists the three mappings. Not decided: the duplicate-name rule for the 56 repeated names (W-48); whether some of these should be Document notes instead (the MDSE Document type has `standard`, `specification`, `report`, `drawing`), which is a stage 2 review from `eaType`; the link for the 48 `Nesting` connectors. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-144 · 2026-09-29 · Issue, Actor and StateMachine map straight to their MDSE classes.** Spencer's decision. The 264 Issue elements become Issue notes with a blank `subtype` (the subtypes `engineering issue` and `lifecycle risk` cannot be told from any field; stage 2 sets them). The 44 Actor elements become Actor notes and the 2 StateMachine elements (`Package Status`, `Circuit - Relay_STM`) become State Machine notes; neither class has subtypes. `eaType` keeps the EA type. From the export: Issues have no stereotype, 222 have a Note, all have EA's default status `Proposed` (dropped, W-39), 239 have a connector (`allocate` 259, `trace` 52, ControlFlow 17), 62 are nested under a UseCase, 24 under a Requirement, 165 have no owner; 4 carry FMEA-style scores. Actors: 249 connectors touch them (Association 218, Aggregation 13), 25 are nested under another Actor, 1 owns a Port, 12 names repeat (some are person names), 2 have a Note. StateMachines hold 12 States and have no connectors. Applied as consequences: the three entries in `ea-element-mapping.yaml`. Not decided: the nesting links (Actors under Actors, Issues under UseCases and Requirements, States under the two StateMachines); the duplicate-name rule for the 12 repeated Actor names (W-48); the Issue subtype. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-145 · 2026-09-29 · Note, Text, Constraint and the other InformationItems become Info notes; one rule names every unnamed element.** Spencer's decision. (1) The 431 Note elements, the 61 Text elements, the 1 Constraint element and the 442 InformationItem elements outside `03 Product Requirement / Regulatory Requirements` (the 2,383 inside became standard requirements, W-115) become notes of type Info with a blank `subtype`, 935 in all; `eaType` keeps the EA type (Note, Text, `NavigationCell`, Constraint, InformationItem). Connectors are imported where drawn (W-55). Nothing is folded into the linked element in stage 1, unlike the old tool (N-01, N-02, I-01 to I-04); folding is stage 2. (2) One naming rule for every unnamed element: its lowercase EA object type and a counter across the vault, in EA creation order (W-14), for example `note 1`, `object 1`, `text 1`. Reading to confirm: the counter runs per EA object type, and a compound type is written as one lowercase word (`activitypartition`, `statenode`). It applies to the unnamed Notes (430), Objects (84), ActivityPartitions (21), Actions (9), Boundaries (9), Synchronizations (7), UseCases (7), Texts (4), StateNodes (2) and the Constraint (1); Ports and pins keep W-134 and the Parts with no block keep W-138 (`part 1`, one counter, the same rule). From the export: 430 of 431 Notes have no name; 419 carry text (median 90 characters, longest 1,152); 377 are drawn on a diagram; 348 `NoteLink` connectors join Notes to UseCases (159), Requirements (78), Classes (59), Activities (35) and others. The 61 Texts are mostly diagram labels (57 hold the text in the name). Of the 442 InformationItems, 427 have a Note and 312 a connector, mostly to Requirements and Classes; 135 of the 196 in `Market Requirements` are nested under a requirement. Applied as consequences: the four entries and the InformationItem two-way mapping in `ea-element-mapping.yaml`, and an `unnamedRule` line on the `Name` field in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml`. Not decided: turning `NoteLink` into `describes`/`describedBy` (a link decision); how a diagram canvas shows a Note or Text that is now also a note (W-73 made them canvas text nodes); names such as `note 214` say nothing, so a search finds them by `eaType` or the linked element; whether a name that already exists (a real element called `note 1`) needs a rule. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-146 · 2026-09-29 · Thirteen small element types become `modelCheck` notes, with the EA type as the subtype.** Spencer's decision. The 632 elements of these types have no matching MDSE class and become notes of type `modelCheck` with `subtype` set to their `eaType`, so each note shows what it was and that it still has to be updated: Action 323, StateNode 73, Change 65, Boundary 44, Decision 34, ActivityPartition 28, Trigger 27, Synchronization 14, Sequence 11, ActionPin 6, ProxyConnector 5, Event 1, ActivityParameter 1. None has a stereotype, so the `eaType` is the EA object type. This amends W-100, which left the `subtype` of a `modelCheck` note empty; the subtype now carries the EA type, and the same holds for any element with no approved mapping. Unnamed ones are named by the W-145 rule (Action 9, Boundary 9, Synchronization 7, ActivityPartition 21, StateNode 2). Reading to confirm: "take the type and add a counter" is read as the W-145 rule, the lowercase EA type and a counter (`action 1`), not the word `modelcheck` with a counter, because the subtype already carries the EA type. From the export: the Actions are steps inside Activities (37 have a `kind` in `t_xref`, W-84); ActivityPartitions own 42 Ports (W-123), which keep pointing at `modelCheck` notes, so the W-130 flag `REVIEW owner: ActivityPartition has no mapping yet` stays until stage 2; Boundaries and ActivityPartitions are canvas group nodes on diagrams (W-73). The old tool folded most of these into the owning behaviour (rule B-01), which stage 1 no longer does. Applied as a consequence: the 13 entries in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` are `note`, `modelCheck`, subtype = the EA type. Not decided (still open from W-100): the `id` prefix of `modelCheck` notes, and whether `modelCheck` is a class in `element-types.yaml`, the templates and Breadcrumbs, with its subtype list; I did not add it to `element-types.yaml` because it needs a prefix. Found while checking: the `folder` values in `element-types.yaml` (`10_Objects`, `20_Ports` and the rest) predate W-04 (folders follow the EA packages) and are not used for placement; they need a decision or removal. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-147 · 2026-09-29 · A package is a folder; only a package with a description also gets a note.** Spencer's decision. Every package becomes a folder in the tree that follows the EA packages (W-04, W-65, W-117). The 34 packages that have a `Notes` description (W-66) also get one note, named like the package and placed inside its folder, of type `modelCheck` with subtype `Package` (W-146: MDSE has no Package class), carrying the description as its main text and the usual `Source: EA` lines. The other 1,352 packages become a folder only, as W-118 decided for `00 Product Abstract`, so their Package row (Author, creation date, GUID) stays in the export and the ledger (W-35) and is not in the vault. From the export: 1,387 packages (the root has no `t_object` row), 1,386 Package elements with no stereotype and no Alias; the 34 descriptions have a median of 12 characters and a maximum of 193; 1,148 packages hold diagrams, 1,114 hold elements, 38 hold only sub-packages, 21 are empty; 63 names hold characters unsafe in a folder name. This answers the question W-66 left open (whether every package gets a describing note): it does not, and only a described package does; it also decides where the 34 descriptions go. Not decided: the replacement of unsafe characters in folder names (W-48, W-65); the `uid` of the 34 notes (from the Package element's creation date, W-09, W-66). Applied as a consequence: the Package entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml`. With this, all 72 `eaType` values have a mapping. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-148 · 2026-09-29 · Dry run of the element mapping.** A check, not a decision, in the manner of the sweep in W-108. Every decided rule (W-113 to W-147) was applied to all 35,969 rows of `t_object.csv`. Result: each row lands in exactly one outcome and none is unmapped. Outcomes: note 30,587 (29,928 plus 659 instance Ports added to a block), merged into a block Port 1,756 (W-114), folded into the owner as a `hasPart` entry 2,274 (2,055 typed by a Class, 219 typed by an Activity, State or Signal; W-136, W-137), Package as a folder only 1,352 (W-147). The 659 added instance Ports combine into 617 new Ports, so the vault has 30,545 notes from elements. By type: Requirement 16,371, Port 3,012 (423 interface Classes plus 2,589), Object 2,962 (2,314 Classes, 545 Objects, 103 Parts with no block), Use Case 1,667, Item Flow 1,383 (623 Signals, 760 FlowProperty), Function 1,141, Design 954, Info 935, `modelCheck` 666 (632 small types and 34 Package notes), Verification 527, Artifact 459, Issue 264, State 158, Actor 44, State Machine 2. All match the figures in the log entries. The 2,924 diagrams (canvas files with a companion note, W-73) are not in these counts. Not covered by the check: links, connectors, folder placement, names and the properties of each note; the rules for those are the open items (containment, connectors, naming, templates, the `modelCheck` prefix). The per-element sheet (35,969 rows) was made outside the repository and is not committed. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-149 · 2026-09-29 · `hasPart`/`partOf` is for Objects only; a generic `hasChild`/`childOf` carries every other nesting.** Spencer's decision. The end state is that `hasPart`/`partOf` is an object relationship (the assembly and its block), and a generic parent and child link serves everything else. A new stored pair, `hasChild` (forward, written on the owner) and `childOf` (inverse, generated on the child), is added to `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.8) directly after `hasFlow`/`flowOf`, so it also sits there in a note's properties (W-126), and to `.obsidian/types.json` as `multitext`. It is permanent, not temporary like `hasClassifier`; Spencer wrote that a generic parent and child link "might work" for everything else, so it stays until a pair needs something more specific. The guidance line `composition` in `relationships.yaml` now says so. The rule for the open containment (`ParentID`) of W-46: for an element that is not a Port or a Part, the owner is written as `hasChild` on the owner note; when both notes are Objects it is `hasPart` instead. From the export: 20,666 open children on 4,564 owners; 282 pairs have an Object at both ends (Object under Object 189, Object under Class 54, Class under Class 39) and take `hasPart`; the other 20,384 take `hasChild`, among them Requirement under Requirement 14,566 (70.5% of all), Use Case under Use Case 1,426, Design under Design 851, Function under Function 826, Requirement under Function 493, Requirement under Design 401, Verification under Verification 346, Artifact under Requirement 309. The median owner has 3 children, the maximum is 117, 9 owners have more than 50. Consequences applied, listed as required: (1) W-46 said containment does not become a relationship; the `ParentID` entry in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` now records W-149 as the change. (2) W-137 used `hasPart` for the 219 Parts that point at an Activity (142), a State (61) or a Signal (16); none of those pairs is Object to Object, so under the new rule they use `hasChild` (the Part entry in `ea-element-mapping.yaml` is updated); this is my reading of the rule and reverses the wording of W-137 for those 219, and the W-137 entry itself is not edited. (3) `hasPart` stays for the Parts that fold into an assembly (W-136, Object to Object) and the 103 Parts that become Object notes when the owner is an Object; a Part with no block whose owner is not an Object (an Activity owner, 3 of them) takes `hasChild`. Not decided: whether stage 2 later replaces `hasChild` on a specific pair with a more precise link (for example a requirement decomposition or a comment link); the `Nesting` connectors (836, of which 584 repeat an owner link) remain imported where drawn under W-55 and Rule 33; the `modelCheck` pairs (about 490) carry `hasChild` until those notes are sorted. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-150 · 2026-09-29 · Vault review before the change of chat.** A check and clean-up, not a decision, in the manner of W-108 and W-148. Checked and found consistent: the log runs W-01 to W-149 in order with no gap or duplicate; every W number cited in the schemas and notes exists; all 8 YAML files and `types.json` parse; the taxonomy note matches `element-types.yaml` on prefix, folder and subtype for all 24 classes; the `id` snippet matches the prefixes; every class has a template and every template default `type` and `subtype` is valid; every relationship field is in `types.json`; the remote has no commit I did not make. Found and fixed: (1) `ea-element-mapping.yaml` still showed Port and Part as `pending`; both are decided (W-113 to W-134, W-132 to W-138) and now list their rules and decisions, so all 31 object types are `note`. (2) The Open list carried resolved items; removed as decided: the package note (W-147), ports as notes or sections, the element mapping as next topic (W-148), the 469 interface Classes (W-120), the Port merge rules (W-114), the Object details (W-138, W-145, W-149) and a duplicate naming item; narrowed: the three set-aside fields (now only the element types other than Port, Object and Part), the Port, FlowProperty and Part leftovers, containment. (3) `Handoff - Continue Here.md` was rewritten: it still said the Port findings were undecided, the element mapping all pending and the package walk paused.
