Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-151 · 2026-09-29 · Nesting connectors add no notes or links of their own; requirement nesting is bidirectional.** Spencer's decision. (1) The 836 drawn `Nesting` connectors carry no name, notes, stereotype, role or PDATA (checked in `t_connector.csv`), so their only own data is the connector GUID and where they are drawn. All 836 run start to end as child to parent, except 2 of the 584 that repeat a `ParentID` link (582 child to parent, 2 parent to child). The 584 add no link, because `hasChild`/`childOf` from `ParentID` already covers them (W-149). The 252 independent ones (200 whose child has no `ParentID`, 52 whose child has a different `ParentID` and so gets a second `childOf` value; 210 involve a Requirement, mostly Requirement to Requirement 156, Requirement to Class 32, Artifact to InformationItem 24) are written as `hasChild` on the end note and `childOf` on the start note, following the direction of 582 of the 584 that can be checked; the pair rule of W-149 still applies, so `hasPart`/`partOf` replaces it when both notes are Objects (how many of the 252 that affects was not counted). No endpoint is a Package, Port or Part, so every endpoint is a note. (2) The 252 also get one `REVIEW nesting direction` line on the parent note, because the direction is inferred and not recorded in EA; stage 2 checks them (Task 7). (3) Each of the 836 connector GUIDs is kept as a source-section line on the parent note, one line per connector, so nothing is lost; the provisional wording is `- Nesting connector: {GUID}`, and the final format belongs to the connector line format still open (W-55, W-99). (4) Confirmed for requirements: every nested element gets `childOf` to the element it is placed under, and the owner gets `hasChild` to each child, whatever the child type, including the requirements under a Function or a Design and the Artifacts under a requirement. It restates W-149; the export has 14,939 children under Requirements: Requirement 12,178, InformationItem 2,395, Artifact 309 and 57 others. An Artifact element is a note and carries `childOf` itself; only its attached files (W-76) cannot hold relationships. The `Artifact.md` template lists no relationship fields yet, which belongs to the templates step. Consequences applied: `hasChild` under W-149 is about 20,636 links before the Object-to-Object exception (20,384 plus the 252), the `ParentID` entry in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` and the `composition` guidance in `relationships.yaml` now name W-151, Task 7 has a new item for the nesting review lines, and the Open item for Nesting is removed. Not decided: the final wording of the connector GUID line and of the `REVIEW nesting direction` line. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-152 · 2026-09-29 · Generalization connectors become `subtypeOf`/`supertypeOf`; the Nesting GUID moves into the review line.** Spencer's decision, the first connector type settled by type (Generalization, 3,974 connectors, one rule: matrix Rule 1). (1) Rule: every Generalization is drawn source to destination with no stereotype and no Notes; the source is the specific element. `subtypeOf` is written on the source note and `supertypeOf` is generated on the destination note. Both are list properties (`multitext` in `.obsidian/types.json`, W-126); 401 of the 3,500 source notes have more than one parent, and 1,027 notes have children (median 2, most 127 on `lsWarning`, then 57 on `Wire` and 56 on `lsCanSystem`). (2) Counts: 3,973 connectors resolve to two notes; 6 pairs are drawn twice and are written once, so 3,967 unique links; 1 connector (`{DCD0BC02-C22E-4fe2-91FD-01EC342FFECC}`) has both ends at object 0, which is not in the export, so it writes no link and is recorded as an exception in the ledger (matrix Rule 71, W-57). (3) Names: the 2 connectors with a `Name` (`AC` and `DC`, both on `lsErrorControlPilot`, to `lsErrorAutomaticError` and `lsErrorUserInteractionError`) go in the source section of the source note (W-56) as `- Connector name (to [[<target>]]): <name>`; the wording is provisional and belongs to the connector line format still open. No GUID line is written for a Generalization, because the ledger keys every connector by GUID (W-35, W-57). (4) Mixed types: under the element mapping 88 connectors (87 unique links, on 81 source notes) join notes of different types: Port and Object 39 (Port to Object 22, Object to Port 17, from W-120), Function to Object 30, Function to Design 8, State to Object 5, Function to State 2, and one each of State to Design, Design to Object, Design to Function and Actor to Object. They are written like the others, and each source note that has one also gets the fixed line `REVIEW subtypeOf: the two notes have different types` in its `Source: EA` section, so one search finds them, in the manner of W-130. The other 3,885 pairs have the same type at both ends (Object 1,627, Function 757, Item Flow 579, Use Case 384, Design 288, Port 209, Requirement 26, Actor 5, State 5, Issue 2, modelCheck 2, Verification 1). (5) Amendment to W-151: point (3) of W-151 (a `- Nesting connector: {GUID}` line for each of the 836 Nesting connectors) is replaced. The 584 that repeat a `ParentID` link get no line. The connector GUID of each of the 252 independent ones goes into the `REVIEW nesting direction` line on the parent note, because the reviewer needs it to find the connector in EA to check its direction; provisional wording `REVIEW nesting direction: connector {GUID}`. That is 252 GUID lines, not 836. The rest of W-151 stands, and the W-151 entry itself is not edited. Spencer asked about a mix-up between the two: Generalization is not used for nested requirements, which stay `hasChild`/`childOf` (W-149, W-151); only 26 Generalization connectors join two requirements, and they are true \"kind of\" links. Consequences applied, listed as required: a new file `99_System/03_Schemas/ea-connector-mapping.yaml` records the rules by connector type, starting with Generalization and Nesting; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 has a new item for the `REVIEW subtypeOf` lines and the Nesting item now names the GUID in the review line; the handoff and the Open list are updated. The Generalization rule replaces the r12 matrix status only for the new mapping; the matrix notes in `10_EA Native Translator` are not edited. Not decided: the final wording of the connector name line and of the two review lines. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-153 · 2026-09-29 · Connector vocabulary: `interfaces` and a temporary `equals`; `equals` is updated after the import.** Spencer's decisions on the EA `Connector` type (823 connectors: 574 with no stereotype and 249 `BindingConnector`; `Direction` is `Unspecified` on all of them). (1) The 574 plain connectors become `interfaces`, a new symmetric field written on both end notes (matrix Rule 41). Spencer named it. (2) The 249 `BindingConnector` connectors become `equals`, also symmetric and written on both end notes (matrix Rule 40), and `equals` is kept for stage 1 on purpose, to stay mechanical (W-30). Spencer's intent for the relationship is that one port is also exposed at a higher layer (a circuit's antenna port, the PCBA's and the product's are the same antenna at different levels), which is a direction, so stage 2 replaces `equals` with a directional pair; the proposed names are `exposes` (written on the outer Port) and `exposedBy` (on the inner Port), and `extends` is ruled out because it already means UML extend and inheritance. The name is decided when the task is done. (3) New Post-Import Task 8, \"Update the `equals` relationships\", records this. From the export: 185 of the 249 have a clear inner and outer end (a Port of an assembly block and a Port of a Part inside it; 99 are drawn inner to outer and 86 outer to inner, so the connector's order gives no direction); 64 do not (Part to Part in different assemblies 17, Part and Object 29, Object to Object 10, Object and Class 7, Part to Part in one assembly 1); after the W-114 merge 17 of the 154 inner block Ports connect to more than one outer Port. (4) The matrix rule notes for Connector are not edited. Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.9) lists `interfaces` and `equals` under `symmetric`, `equals` marked temporary, with a guidance line; `.obsidian/types.json` has both as `multitext`; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a partial entry for Connector; `Post-Import Tasks.md` has Task 8. Not decided for the Connector type: one link per pair when several connectors end on the same pair of notes after the Port merge (70 connectors in 22 groups, 4 plain pairs drawn both ways, 2 groups with different names); the line format for a connector Name on both end notes (174 named, 165 distinct names); the assembly and Part context line (W-114); the `REVIEW port needed` line for the 109 connectors with an end that is not a Port, including the 87 Part-to-Part cases whose Parts fold away. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-154 · 2026-09-29 · One link per pair of notes for Connector-type connectors; every distinct name is kept.** Spencer's decision. After the Port merge (W-114), connectors of type Connector that end on the same pair of notes are written as one entry, in `interfaces` for the plain ones and in `equals` for the BindingConnectors (W-153), on both end notes. The pair has no order: a connector drawn A to B and one drawn B to A are one link (4 plain pairs). Every distinct non-empty `Name` in a group is kept as its own line; identical names are written once, and unnamed connectors add no line. The ledger keeps every connector GUID and records that it was folded into the link (W-35). From the export: 22 groups hold 70 connectors (16 plain, 6 binding; sizes 2 to 6), which become 22 links, so 822 connectors that resolve become 774 links, 48 fewer; no pair is joined by both a plain and a binding connector, so no group mixes the two fields. 17 groups are all unnamed, 3 share one name and 2 hold different names. 2 groups contain a repeat of the same drawing (identical original endpoints); the other 20 join different instance Ports, so they are the same link seen from different assemblies, and what a collapse loses is which assembly each connector was drawn in; that is kept only by the assembly and Part context line, whose format is still open (W-114). The rule follows W-114, which found the same pattern for all connector types (63 connectors in 18 groups). Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` records the rule for the Connector type and drops it from the not-decided list; the Open item for the Connector type is narrowed. Not decided for the Connector type: the Name line format on both end notes (174 named, 165 distinct names); the assembly and Part context line; the `REVIEW port needed` line for the 109 connectors with an end that is not a Port, including the 87 Part-to-Part cases. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-155 · 2026-09-29 · Connector type: no assembly context line; block-level ends keep the W-55 flag; every connector is an interface.** Spencer's decisions, after the examples in this chat. (1) The assembly and Part context line of W-114 is dropped: if a Port exists the connection exists, and where a connection was drawn is not written into the notes (the diagram canvases still show it, W-73). This changes the sentence in W-114 that says a moved connector keeps the assembly and Part as context; the W-114 entry itself is not edited. It is my reading of Spencer's words that he is not concerned with which diagrams a relationship was drawn in. (2) A Connector-type connector with an end that is not a Port is written as drawn and flagged with the W-55 line on both end notes, `- REVIEW port needed: Connector <name or unnamed> to [[other end]]`, in the `Source: EA` section, and identical lines on one note are written once. In the export that is 109 connectors (94 with no Port end, 15 with one); 108 resolve (1 has both ends missing from the export and writes nothing), giving 101 distinct flag entries. Task 1 in `Post-Import Tasks.md` stands. (3) A Part end resolves to the block note the Part is typed by (`PDATA1`, a Class): 182 Part ends do; the 3 with no block resolve to the Object note with subtype `part` (W-138). This is my reading of W-114, W-136 and W-138 for Connector-type ends, since a Part folds into its block and is not a note, and Spencer approved it with the rest. No connector becomes a self-loop. (4) Every connector produces an interface: none is dropped, and connectors on the same pair of notes are one link (W-154), with each connector GUID in the ledger. Because Part ends now resolve to blocks, the 822 connectors that resolve give 766 links, not the 774 of W-154 (which counted a Part as its own end). (5) Ports and links that are named differently stay separate: Ports through the W-114 rule, and each distinct connector Name as its own line (W-154). Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` records the flag, the Part-end rule and the count for the Connector type and drops the context line and the flag from its not-decided list; the Open items for the Connector type and W-114 are narrowed. Not decided for the Connector type: only the Name line format on both end notes (174 named connectors, 165 distinct names). Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-156 · 2026-09-29 · Every review item must be resolvable from the vault alone; the tool writes review tables keyed by GUID.** Spencer's decision, on the question of what the tool can do mechanically so a person or an AI can find missing information without opening EA. The trigger: after the Port merge (W-114) and W-155, 813 of the 822 Connector-type connectors have an end on a Part or Object instance that becomes its block (84 in an assembly with two or more Parts of the same block, 88 with an Object end), and the ledger (W-35) holds no endpoints, so the review tasks depended on the EA export. (1) Rule: every `REVIEW` line the tool writes must be resolvable from the vault alone, with no need to open EA or the export. (2) Review tables: for each review task the tool writes a table (CSV, viewable through a Base) in `99_System/11_Import`, regenerated on every run like the ledger and removed when stage 2 is complete, one row per review item: GUID, EA type, name, the ends as drawn (element, owner, assembly), the notes the ends resolved to, the candidate notes (for example the Ports on each block end), any direction or answer the tool derived, and the category. The hand-made snapshot `Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv` is the first example and is replaced by the generated table. (3) In the note, the review line stays short: a fixed phrase that one search finds (W-130), a one-phrase reason and the GUID, which is the key to the table row; the wording of each line is set per task. The evidence is in the table, not in the note. (4) The tool derives what it can and records it as evidence, not as a change to the stage-1 rule: for the `equals` connectors (W-153) it follows block containment over several levels (a block holds a Part typed by another block, which holds another Part, and Objects nested in Objects). From the export 60 of the 64 connectors that had no one-level answer have a containment path (end contains start 31, start contains end 29), so a direction is derived for 245 of the 249 and 4 have none. `equals` stays in stage 1 as decided (W-153); Task 8 becomes confirming 245 derived directions and deciding 4. The multi-level rule is mine and has not been spot-checked; how Objects nested in Objects were counted needs a few examples before the 60 are trusted. Consequences applied, listed as required: `Post-Import Tasks.md` Tasks 1 and 8 point at their review tables and Task 8 carries the new counts; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` records the rule for the Connector type; the Open list has the audit and the spot-check. Not decided: the audit of the review lines already decided against the rule (W-55, the four of W-130, W-151, W-152; at least the W-130 line `REVIEW port: added from an assembly, no matching port on the block` does not say which assembly); the file names and exact columns of each table; whether the ledger keeps the ends as drawn after stage 2. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-157 · 2026-09-29 · Audit of the review lines against W-156: three get a review table and a GUID, four pass.** Spencer's decision. Each review line already decided was checked against the rule that it must be resolvable from the vault alone (W-156); the verdicts are my reading of the rules and were not checked against sample notes. (1) Three fail and are fixed. `REVIEW port needed` (W-55, 108 Connector-type connectors that resolve, plus the flow connectors of other types when those are mapped): the instance, assembly and candidate Ports are lost by the Port merge; the line becomes `- REVIEW port needed: Connector <name or unnamed> to [[other end]]: connector {GUID}`. `REVIEW port: added from an assembly, no matching port on the block` (W-130, 617 Port notes from 659 instance Ports): the line does not say which Part or assembly the Port came from; it becomes `REVIEW port: added from an assembly, no matching port on the block: port {GUID}`, where the GUID is the instance Port in the note's `EA GUID` line, and the table lists every instance Port folded into the note (my reading of how to key a note made from several). `REVIEW nesting direction: connector {GUID}` (W-151, W-152, 252): the GUID alone points into EA; the line is unchanged and gets a table with both ends as drawn. Each has a review table in `99_System/11_Import` (W-156), provisional names `Review - Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv` (replacing the hand-made `Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv`), `Review - Added Ports.csv` and `Review - Nesting Direction.csv`; the `equals` table of Task 8 is `Review - Equals Direction.csv`. The fixed phrase of each line stays at its start, so a search for it still works. (2) Four pass and stay as they are: `REVIEW subtypeOf: target port is not on an ancestor block` (W-130, 146: the link and both blocks' `hasPort` are in the vault), `REVIEW owner: ActivityPartition has no mapping yet` (W-130, 42: the owner is a `modelCheck` note that links to the Port), `REVIEW block: owner Part has no block` (W-130, 26: the Part becomes an Object note of subtype `part`, W-138), `REVIEW subtypeOf: the two notes have different types` (W-152, 88: both notes and their `eaType` are in the vault). Consequences applied, listed as required: `Post-Import Tasks.md` Tasks 1, 6, 7 and 8 name the tables and the new line forms; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has the new `REVIEW port needed` form; the Open item for review lines is narrowed. Not decided: the exact columns of each table; whether the ledger keeps the ends as drawn after stage 2. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-158 · 2026-09-29 · One Name line format for connectors of every type.** Spencer's decision. A connector's `Name` is written in the `Source: EA` section as `- Connector name: <name> (to [[other end]])`, one line per distinct name. Which note gets it stays as decided: both end notes for Connector and InformationFlow (W-55, W-56), the owner-side note (the one holding the forward relationship) for the other types (W-56). Identical names on the same link are written once and unnamed connectors write nothing (W-154). The `(to [[other end]])` part says which link the name belongs to, since a note can hold several named links. It replaces the provisional Generalization form of W-152 (`- Connector name (to [[<target>]]): <name>`), so there is one pattern for all types; the W-152 entry itself is not edited. From the export: the Connector type has 174 named connectors with 165 distinct names, so up to 330 lines; Generalization 2 lines. It also settles the part of W-99 that left open how a connector's values go on an end note, for `Name` only: the line sits with the other `- ` lines below `EA GUID`, not in the `Name` line above it, which stays the element's own original name. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` uses the new form for Generalization and Connector, and the Connector type is marked settled; the Open item for the Connector type is narrowed. Not decided: where the connector name lines sit against the tag lines and the review lines in the `Source: EA` section (the line order, open since W-107); the other connector values that may go on an end note (`Notes` 5 rows, StateFlow trigger, guard and effect, W-61), which are decided with their types. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-159 · 2026-09-29 · Aggregation: composite Object pairs are `hasPart`, everything else `hasChild`; an existing link wins.** Spencer's decision on the Aggregation type (2,288 connectors, all drawn part to whole, no stereotype; matrix Rules 2, 3, 4 and 51). Spencer approved the rule for shared aggregations after asking what they are; I read his approval as covering the rest of the proposal made just before, which that answer only changed for shared ones. (1) Composite (`DestIsAggregate` 2, 1,688): Object to Object becomes `hasPart` on the whole and `partOf` on the part; every other pair becomes `hasChild`/`childOf` (W-149; matrix Rule 3's `parent`/`child` fields do not exist). (2) Shared (`DestIsAggregate` 1, 600): every pair becomes `hasChild`/`childOf` on the whole, meaning grouped under, with no claim of ownership. In SysML a shared part is used by the whole but not owned; in this model most are not physical parts: 278 are countries grouped into market regions (`block` to `System Partner`, for example `Brazil` to `South America`), 57 are things placed in a Physical Context, and about 30 are real parts (Hardware Component pairs 17, Module to Physical System Variant 15) that stay `hasChild` until stage 2. The flag is used unevenly (parts with more than one whole: 38 of 538 shared, 194 of 1,325 composite), so it is kept as the only signal the data gives rather than trusted as ownership. (3) Precedence: where the pair already has a link, from a folded Part (`hasPart`, W-136) or from `ParentID` placement (W-149), that link wins and the connector writes nothing: 1,837 connectors (1,551 composite, 286 shared). The Part element is the stronger evidence that the thing is an instance in the assembly. (4) Result: 451 connectors add 450 new links, 77 `hasPart` and 373 `hasChild`; no pair has both a composite and a shared connector; no self-loops and no pair drawn both ways. (5) Physical Context: its wholes are Objects since W-139, so matrix Rule 4 (`participants`) has no target; they are written by the rules above, and when stage 2 makes a Physical Context a Context, its `hasPart` and `hasChild` entries become `hasParticipant` (Task 7). (6) Mixed types: a new link between notes of different types gets `REVIEW hasChild: the two notes have different types` (or `REVIEW hasPart: ...` for the Object-only field, which cannot occur here) on the whole's note; 23 links on 15 notes. Both notes are in the vault, so it passes W-156 without a table. (7) Other fields: `SourceCard` is the quantity line on the assembly (W-43, W-54; 52 connectors); `SourceRole` is archived (W-56); the 4 names use the W-158 line on the whole's note; `DestIsAggregate` is used by this rule and stays in the export. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has an Aggregation entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 has items for shared `hasChild` links, Physical Context participation and the new review line. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-160 · 2026-09-29 · Realisation: a requirement applies to an Object, a Function realizes a Use Case, everything else keeps realization with a model check flag; only a Function or a Design satisfies a requirement.** Spencer's decisions. (1) Principle for the relationship mapping: a Function or a Design satisfies a requirement (`satisfies`/`satisfiedBy`); an Object does not, it is what a requirement applies to. It is applied when the `satisfy` Dependencies are mapped. (2) Spencer confirmed the reading of W-159: composite is `hasPart`, shared aggregation is `hasChild`, and a pair that is not Object to Object is `hasChild`. (3) Realisation (950 connectors, all drawn source to destination, none named, no self-loops; matrix Rules 18, 19, 48, 55): Object to Requirement becomes `appliesTo` on the requirement pointing at the Object, with `applies` generated on the Object (Rule 18); a Part end resolves to its block (W-155). That is 910 connectors and 909 unique links. Function to Use Case becomes `realizedBy` on the Use Case and `realizes` on the Function (Rule 19); the current export has none, so the rule is there for later exports. Every other pattern keeps EA's meaning as `realizedBy` on the target and `realizes` on the source, and the target note gets one line per link in its `Source: EA` section, `REVIEW modelCheck: realization <source type> to <target type>`: 40 connectors, 40 links on 34 notes (Requirement to Object 8, drawn the other way round from the applicability rule and so not converted, which is my reading of \"everything else\"; Port to Requirement 5; Function to modelCheck 4; Function to Item Flow 4; Object to Design 3; Package to Requirement 7; and single ones). Both notes are in the vault, so the line passes W-156 without a table. (4) The 7 connectors that start at a Package come from 6 packages with no description (`SW PCE25 Logs`, `SW PCE25`, `PosiGuard MRD`, `NOM`, `IEC`, `LAX`), which are folders with no note (W-147), so there is nothing to link: the requirement note gets the line `REVIEW modelCheck: realization from folder <vault folder path>` and no field. This is my reading; the folder is in the vault, so it passes W-156. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a Realisation entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 has an item for the realization flags. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-161 · 2026-09-29 · NoteLink: a note linked to one element is written into that element's body; a note linked to several stays an Info note with `describes`.** Spencer's decision on the NoteLink type (349 connectors; matrix Rule 45). Every NoteLink has an EA Note, Text or Constraint at one end (347) or at both (2); every end resolves. (1) A note linked to exactly one element is written into the body of that element and gets no note of its own: 250 notes, on 177 elements (up to 16 on one). A Part or Port end resolves as in W-155 and W-114. This amends W-145 for those 250, which were to be Info notes; the W-145 entry itself is not edited. The note's own GUID stays in the ledger (W-35). The 1 with empty text writes nothing. (2) A note linked to two or more elements stays an Info note (W-145) and gets `describes` pointing at each element, with `describedBy` generated on the element: 38 notes, 94 links (up to 6 on one note). The drawn direction does not matter; the field is always on the note. (3) The 2 NoteLinks between two notes are written as `describes` in the drawn direction between the two Info notes, which is my reading; it is only possible if neither folds, and how a fold into another note behaves is not decided. (4) The 205 notes with no NoteLink stay Info notes (W-145). Counts that change: Info notes from Note, Text and Constraint fall from 493 to 243, and the notes of W-148 from 30,545 to 30,295. Consequences not applied, and open: where the folded text goes in the body and its label (`Definitions/Note Layout.md` has no place for it yet); 225 of the 250 are drawn on a diagram, so the canvas (W-73) has to show them as a text card instead of a link to a note; 40 of the 250 hold EA rich-text tags, handled like any other EA Note text. Applied: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a NoteLink entry. The matrix rule note is not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-162 · 2026-09-29 · Where folded EA notes go in the body.** Spencer's decision, after an example built from the export (the System Function `Communicate with external device`, which receives 16 notes, all by Herman Montano between 2025-01-29 and 2025-03-20). The notes of W-161 that fold into one element go after the comment texts and before the user section, under one label `**EA notes:**`, one bullet per note in EA creation order (ties by GUID, W-14): `- <date>, <author>: <text>`. The date is the note's EA creation date (yyyy-MM-dd). The author is the person's name from their person note, found through `eaNames` (W-25; `HermanMontano` gives `Herman Montano`), else the EA author as written, else `unknown`; the reading of `unknown` for a blank author is mine. A note of several lines continues as an indented block under its bullet. A note with no text is left out (1). Rich-text tags in the text are handled like any other EA Note text. Chosen over one `**EA note:**` label per note because it is shorter and keeps who wrote each note and when, which the ledger (W-35) does not show in the note. Consequences applied, listed as required: `Definitions/Note Layout.md` has the new item 5 (the items after it renumbered) and the example; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` records it for NoteLink. Not decided: the canvas text card for the 225 folded notes drawn on diagrams (W-73). Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-163 · 2026-09-29 · Dependency `satisfy`: a Function or Design satisfies a requirement; everything else keeps `satisfies` with a model check flag.** Spencer's decision, the first Dependency stereotype (Dependency is taken one stereotype at a time: `satisfy` 2,444, `deriveReqt` 1,416, `refine` 1,114, `trace` 770, none 667, `verify` 333, `RecoveryRequirement` 14, `ASILDecompose` 4). All 2,444 are drawn source to destination; matrix Rules 15, 16, 17, 54 and 71. (1) Function or Design to Requirement becomes `satisfies` on the Function or Design, with `satisfiedBy` generated on the requirement (the principle of W-160): 2,328 connectors, 2,328 links (Function 1,372, Design 956). The matrix deferred 1,048 of these as State satisfaction (Rule 17); the element mapping makes the System States under `05 Product Design` Design notes (W-142), so most now fit directly. (2) Every other pair keeps `satisfies` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets one line per link in its `Source: EA` section, `REVIEW modelCheck: satisfy <source type> to <target type>`, the pattern of W-160: 112 connectors, 112 links on 89 notes (State to Requirement 92, modelCheck to Requirement 6, Object to Requirement 3, Requirement to Requirement 3, and 8 in pairs of one or two). Both notes are in the vault, so the line passes W-156 without a table. (3) The 4 connectors with an end missing from the export write nothing and are recorded in the ledger (Rule 71, W-57). No self-loops; Part ends resolve to their block (W-155). Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a Dependency entry with `satisfy`; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 has an item for the satisfy flags. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-164 · 2026-09-29 · Dependency `deriveReqt`: `derivedFrom` between requirements; everything else keeps it with a model check flag.** Spencer's decision (1,416 connectors, all drawn source to destination, no end missing, no self-loops, no pair drawn both ways; matrix Rules 23 and 57). (1) Requirement to Requirement becomes `derivedFrom` on the source (the derived, more specific requirement) pointing at the target it comes from, with `derivedBy` generated on the target (Rule 23): 1,410 connectors, 1,408 links. 1,102 of them run from a functional or design-constraint requirement to a plain `requirement`. (2) The other 6 connectors (Requirement to Artifact 3, Function to Requirement 1, Design to Requirement 1, Requirement to Info 1) keep `derivedFrom` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: deriveReqt <source type> to <target type>` (the pattern of W-160 and W-163): 6 links on 6 notes; both notes are in the vault, so it passes W-156. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` adds `deriveReqt` to the Dependency entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 now covers the `deriveReqt` flags too. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-165 · 2026-09-29 · Dependency `refine`: `refines` between requirements, a Use Case `drives` a requirement, Info and Artifact `describes`; a new `drives`/`drivenBy` pair.** Spencer's decision (1,114 connectors, all drawn source to destination, no end missing, no self-loops, no pair drawn both ways; matrix Rules 20, 21, 22, 56, 65). (1) Requirement to Requirement becomes `refines` on the source, which adds precision to the target, with `refinedBy` generated on the target (Rule 22): 541 connectors, 539 links. `refines`/`refinedBy` is a new pair. (2) Use Case to Requirement becomes `drives` on the Use Case, with `drivenBy` generated on the requirement: 412 connectors, 412 links. This replaces Rule 20 (`describes`). `drives`/`drivenBy` is a new pair, and Spencer intends to use it for other relationships to requirements later. (3) Info or Artifact to Requirement becomes `describes` on the source, with `describedBy` on the requirement (Rule 65): 144 connectors (Info 130, most of them Market Requirements InformationItems, and Artifact 14), 144 links. The field is shared with W-161 (EA notes that comment on several elements); both mean that the note explains the element. (4) The other 17 connectors (Info to Object 7, Info to Function 4, Info to Design 3, Info to modelCheck 2, Requirement to Design 1) keep `refines` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: refine <source type> to <target type>` (the pattern of W-160): 17 links on 16 notes; it passes W-156. Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.10) adds `refines`/`refinedBy` and `drives`/`drivenBy` after `derivedFrom`/`derivedBy`, with guidance lines, so they sit there in a note's properties (W-126); `.obsidian/types.json` has the four fields as `multitext`; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` adds `refine`; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 covers the `refine` flags. Not decided: the `Definitions/Properties` notes for the new fields. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-166 · 2026-09-29 · Dependency `trace`: `describes`, `affects` and `appliesTo` by endpoint, regardless of drawn direction where the meaning is clear; Requirement to Requirement pending.** Spencer's decision (770 connectors, no end missing, no self-loops; 1 marked Destination to Source, for which the tool follows `Direction`, W-51; matrix Rules 24, 25, 26, 50). (1) Info or Artifact to any element becomes `describes` on the Info or Artifact, `describedBy` generated (Rule 24): 224 connectors, 224 links. (2) Object to Info, drawn the other way round, is turned around: `describes` on the Info note pointing at the Object, no flag (Spencer): 35 connectors, 35 links; 259 `describes` links in all. (3) Issue to any element becomes `affects` on the Issue, `affectedBy` generated (Rule 25): 49 connectors, 49 links. (4) Object to Requirement and Requirement to Object both become `appliesTo` on the requirement pointing at the Object, `applies` generated, no flag (Rule 26 and Spencer for the reversed ones): 15 and 8 connectors, 23 links. (5) Requirement to Requirement (336 connectors, 336 links) is usually one requirement mentioning another; Spencer drew them as trace for want of a better relationship. Its field is not decided yet (a proposal is under discussion); until then it is `tracesTo`. (6) Everything else keeps `tracesTo` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: trace <source type> to <target type>` (the pattern of W-160): 103 connectors, 102 links on 82 notes; it passes W-156. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` adds `trace`; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 covers the `trace` flags. Not decided: the field for Requirement to Requirement; whether the 8 Realisation connectors from a Requirement to an Object, flagged in W-160, should also become `appliesTo` without a flag, to match (4). The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-167 · 2026-09-29 · Requirement to Requirement trace becomes `references`; Realisation from a Requirement to an Object becomes `appliesTo`.** Spencer's decision. (1) A new pair `references`/`referencedBy`: `references` is written on the note that mentions another, `referencedBy` is generated on the one mentioned. The 336 `trace` connectors between two requirements (W-166; usually one requirement mentioning another) become `references` on the source: 336 links, no flag. The pair is general enough for a later hand-written link from a requirement to a standard clause or a document. Chosen over `tracesTo` (a migration escape hatch in `relationships.yaml`), `derivedFrom` and `refines` (they claim more than a mention) and `drives` (kept for what gives rise to a requirement, W-165). (2) To match W-166, the 8 Realisation connectors from a Requirement to an Object, flagged in W-160, become `appliesTo` on the requirement pointing at the Object, with no flag; this amends W-160, and the W-160 entry itself is not edited. Realisation now has 917 `appliesTo` links, and the flagged ones drop from 40 to 32 links on 29 notes (7 of them from Package folders). Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.11) adds `references`/`referencedBy` after `drives`/`drivenBy`, with a guidance line; `.obsidian/types.json` has both as `multitext`; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` updates `trace` (settled) and Realisation; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 10 has the new count; the Open item for trace is removed. Not decided: the `Definitions/Properties` notes for `references` and `referencedBy`. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-168 · 2026-09-29 · Dependency `verify`, `RecoveryRequirement` and `ASILDecompose`.** Spencer's decision. None has an end missing from the export or a name; all are drawn source to destination. (1) `verify` (333; matrix Rules 63, 64): Verification to Requirement becomes `verifies` on the Verification, `verifiedBy` generated: 325 connectors, 325 links. The other 8 (Requirement to Requirement 6, Verification to Function 1, Requirement to Function 1) keep `verifies` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: verify <source type> to <target type>`: 8 links on 7 notes. (2) `RecoveryRequirement` (14, RAAML; matrix Rule 68): Design to Requirement 13 and Function to Requirement 1 become `satisfies` on the source (the principle of W-160), and the target note also gets `REVIEW modelCheck: RecoveryRequirement <source type> to <target type>`, because `satisfies` alone would hide that the link came from a recovery analysis: 14 links, lines on 13 notes. (3) `ASILDecompose` (4, RAAML; matrix Rule 69): Requirement to Requirement becomes `derivedFrom` on the source, since an ASIL decomposition splits a requirement into lower ones, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: ASILDecompose Requirement to Requirement`, because the data does not show which end is the decomposed one: 4 links on 4 notes. All three lines pass W-156 (both notes are in the vault and the stereotype is named). Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` adds the three stereotypes; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 covers the new lines. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-169 · 2026-09-29 · Dependency with no stereotype; Dependency is settled.** Spencer's decision (667 connectors, no end missing, no self-loops, no pair drawn both ways, 664 unique links; matrix Rules 27 and 58). (1) Function to Function and Function to Design become `dependsOn` on the source, `dependencyOf` generated, with no flag: 195 and 122 connectors, 317 links. Spencer chose `dependsOn` for Function to Design over `hasDesign`. (2) Pairs covered by rules already set: Artifact to Requirement becomes `describes` (173; W-165, W-166; the Artifacts are tables and formulas of the same standard clause), Requirement to Requirement becomes `references` (38; W-167), a Requirement and an Object in either direction become `appliesTo` on the requirement (13; W-167), Issue to any becomes `affects` (7; W-166), Object to Info becomes `describes` turned around (1; W-166). (3) Everything else keeps `dependsOn` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: dependency <source type> to <target type>` (the pattern of W-160): 118 connectors, 115 links on 95 notes (Design to Design 25, Object to Object 21, Function to Object 8, Design to Object 8, Port to Requirement 5 and small groups); it passes W-156. With this, all 6,762 Dependency connectors have a rule (W-163 to W-169). **W-170 · 2026-09-29 · Abstraction: an Object performs a Function and has a Design; the rest by earlier rules; Abstraction is settled.** Spencer's decision (2,811 connectors: `allocate` 2,795, `Derive` 8, `trace` 5, `Refine` 3; 2 with an end missing from the export write nothing and are in the ledger, Rule 71; 1 is marked Destination to Source and the tool follows `Direction`, W-51; matrix Rules 10 to 14, 53, 66, 67, 72 to 79). (1) `allocate`, Function to Object: turned around, `performs` on the Object pointing at the Function, `performedBy` generated (Rule 10): 1,362 connectors, 1,348 links. (2) `allocate`, Design to Object: `hasDesign` on the Object pointing at the Design, `designOf` generated (Rule 11): 968 connectors, 956 links. (3) `allocate` covered by rules already set: Issue to any becomes `affects` (213; Rule 12, W-166), Info or Artifact to any becomes `describes` (44; Rule 13, W-166), a Requirement and an Object in either direction become `appliesTo` on the requirement (25; W-167). (4) Every other `allocate` (modelCheck to Issue 44, State to Object 26, Object to Object 11, modelCheck to Object 11 and small groups) becomes `tracesTo` in the drawn direction, because the vault has no allocate field, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: allocate <source type> to <target type>`, which keeps the EA meaning visible: 181 connectors, 180 links on 123 notes; it passes W-156. (5) The other stereotypes follow earlier rules: `Refine` Use Case to Requirement becomes `drives` (3; W-165); `Derive` Requirement to Requirement becomes `derivedFrom` (7; W-164) and the 1 Requirement to Issue keeps `derivedFrom` with `REVIEW modelCheck: Derive Requirement to Issue`; `trace` Requirement to Requirement becomes `references` (3; W-167), Object to Requirement `appliesTo` (1; W-167), and Object to Artifact is turned around to `describes` on the Artifact (1), which applies Spencer's Object to Info rule of W-166 to an Artifact, my reading since Artifacts are handled like Info elsewhere. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled Abstraction entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 covers the `allocate` and `Derive` lines. The matrix rule notes (including the package-context Rules 72 and 73) are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-171 · 2026-09-29 · UseCase connectors: `extend` is `optionOf`, `include` is `hasChild`.** Spencer's decision (1,258 connectors: `extend` 736, `include` 522; 1 with an end missing from the export writes nothing and is in the ledger, Rule 71; 8 are marked Destination to Source and the tool follows `Direction`, W-51; matrix Rules 30, 31, 60, 61). (1) `extend`, Use Case to Use Case: `optionOf` on the extending (source) Use Case pointing at the base, `hasOption` generated (Rule 30): 733 connectors, 733 links. (2) `extend`, Issue to Use Case: `affects` on the Issue (W-166): 2. (3) `include`, Use Case to Use Case: `hasChild` on the including (source) Use Case and `childOf` on the included one, because an include means the base scenario contains the included one (Rule 31's parent/child, which is `hasChild` since W-149). 47 includes repeat a placement link (W-149) and add nothing, so 473 new `hasChild` links. (4) `include`, Use Case to Object (1): `hasChild` with `REVIEW hasChild: the two notes have different types` (W-159). Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled UseCase entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 9 covers the one new line. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-172 · 2026-09-29 · Association: participation is written only on the Use Case; Issue `affects`, Info `describes`, everything else `tracesTo` with a model check flag.** Spencer's decision (603 connectors, no stereotype, all `Unspecified` in direction, no end missing, 603 unique pairs; matrix Rules 5, 6, 7, 8, 34, 52). (1) A Use Case associated with an Object is written only on the Use Case; Spencer's reason: a two-way link would overload the Object's properties. It uses a new one-way field `participants` (the name in Rules 5 and 34), listed under `oneWay` in `relationships.yaml`, with no inverse. I applied the same to the Use Case's Actors, Functions and Ports, as Rules 5 and 34 do; that part is my reading. 494 connectors (Object 273, Actor 215, Function 5, Port 1), 494 links on 330 Use Cases, at most 6 on one. `hasParticipant`/`participatesIn` stays the paired field planned for a Context (Task 7 item 8). (2) An Issue with any element becomes `affects` on the Issue (3; W-166). (3) An Info or Artifact with an Object becomes `describes` on the Info or Artifact (4; W-166). (4) Everything else becomes `tracesTo` from the drawn start to the drawn end, and the end note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: association <start type> to <end type>`: 102 connectors, 102 links on 65 notes (Object to Object 72, Design to Object 12, Object to Port 9, Use Case to Use Case 6, Actor to Function 2, Port to Port 1); the line records that EA associated them without a direction. It passes W-156. The 7 names use the W-158 line on the note holding the field. Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.12) lists `participants` under `oneWay` with a guidance line; `.obsidian/types.json` has it as `multitext`; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled Association entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 covers the new line. Not decided: the `Definitions/Properties` note for `participants`. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-173 · 2026-09-29 · Usage: a Function or a Design realizes a Use Case; requirements depend on requirements.** Spencer's decision (239 connectors: 235 with no stereotype, 4 `Responsibility`; all drawn source to destination, no end missing, 238 unique links; matrix Rules 28, 29, 49, 59, 70). (1) Use Case to Function and Use Case to Design become `realizedBy` on the Use Case, with `realizes` generated on the Function or Design (Rule 28; W-160 for Functions, and Spencer extends it to Designs): 214 connectors (Function 157, Design 57), 213 links. (2) Requirement to Requirement becomes `dependsOn` on the source, `dependencyOf` generated, with no flag: 15 connectors, 15 links. Spencer chose `dependsOn` here rather than `references` (W-167), so a Usage between requirements means one relies on the other. (3) Issue to State becomes `affects` on the Issue (2; W-166). (4) Everything else keeps `dependsOn` in the drawn direction, and the target note gets `REVIEW modelCheck: usage <source type> to <target type>`: 8 connectors, 8 links on 6 notes (the `Responsibility` stereotype Object to Object 4, Function to Use Case 2, Function to Function 1, Use Case to State 1); it passes W-156. Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled Usage entry; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 11 covers the new line. The matrix rule notes are not edited. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-174 · 2026-09-29 · ControlFlow becomes `precedes`/`follows`; the flow elements stay `modelCheck` notes.** Spencer's decision (407 connectors, no stereotype, all drawn source to destination, no end missing, 407 unique links; matrix Rule 44, which wrote no relationship, is replaced). (1) A new pair `precedes`/`follows`: `precedes` is written on the step before, pointing at the step after, and `follows` is generated. Every ControlFlow becomes one `precedes` link, whatever its ends (modelCheck to modelCheck 138, Function to Function 124, modelCheck to Function 51, Function to modelCheck 38, Object to modelCheck 13, modelCheck to Issue 10, others 33), with no review line, because the chains run through Function and `modelCheck` notes that Task 7 already reviews. 5 connectors loop back to the same step and are kept as a link to itself; 2 pairs are drawn both ways and give two links. (2) A guard is written on the step before as `- Guard: <value> (to [[next step]])`, the pattern of the W-158 name line: 24 connectors, 6 from `PDATA2` (W-61) and 18 from `Top_End_Label` (`True`, `Yes`, `No` and others), no connector has both. Using `Top_End_Label` for ControlFlow guards changes its disposition from `archive` (W-53) to `body` for those 18 rows. The 31 names use the W-158 line on the step before. (3) Spencer asked whether the translator should create the final note types for the flow elements instead, since many will not be kept. My answer: for the tool the two are equally simple (one rule per EA type either way); the cost of mapping now is on the vault side: a class, template and `id` prefix per type for notes that will mostly be deleted, and since `id` numbers are never reused (W-15, W-18), each deleted note would retire its number. So the flow elements (Action 249 ends, Decision 78, StateNode 44, Synchronization 39 in ControlFlow) stay `modelCheck` notes (W-146), a single holding type, and stage 2 decides what to keep. Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.13) adds `precedes`/`follows` after `references`/`referencedBy`, with a guidance line; `.obsidian/types.json` has both as `multitext`; `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` records the change to `Top_End_Label`; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled ControlFlow entry. Not decided: the `Definitions/Properties` notes for the new fields. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-175 · 2026-09-29 · StateFlow becomes `precedes`/`follows`, with trigger, guard and effect lines.** Spencer's decision (200 connectors, no stereotype, all drawn source to destination, no end missing, no self-loops, 200 unique links; matrix Rule 47, which kept them as evidence for Transition notes, is replaced). (1) Every StateFlow becomes `precedes` on the state before, pointing at the next state, with `follows` generated (W-174), with no review line: State to State 115, modelCheck to State 45 (initial and final nodes), State to modelCheck 29, Design to Design 5, others 6. The 18 pairs drawn both ways give two links each, which is right for transitions. No Transition notes are made. (2) On the state before, one source-section line per value, in the pattern of the W-158 name line: `- Trigger: <value> (to [[next state]])` from `PDATA1` (42), `- Guard: <value> (to [[next state]])` from `PDATA2` (27), `- Effect: <value> (to [[next state]])` from `PDATA3` (2) (W-61). The 10 names use the W-158 line. The `trigger` (44) and `effect` (2) rows in `t_xref` (W-83) are not written separately; they name the same trigger and effect as the text, and they stay in the export. If stage 2 wants Transition notes, the links and lines hold what is needed to build them. Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` guidance for `precedes` names StateFlow; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled StateFlow entry. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-176 · 2026-09-29 · Sequence messages are an ordered list on the diagram's companion note, not relationships.** Spencer's decision (199 connectors, all named, on 14 diagrams, the largest `Charge Function GSE Mk3mod CAN` with 40; 48 lifelines, one in 38 messages; matrix Rule 43, which wrote no relationship). No relationship field is written. The companion note of each diagram (W-73) gets a labelled list `**Messages:**` in `SeqNo` order (W-61), one line per message, `<SeqNo>. [[sender]] → [[receiver]]: <message name>`; a message a lifeline sends to itself names it twice. The 4 messages with Notes get the text as an indented line under the message. Reasons: order and wording only mean something inside one diagram, and as a link field a lifeline would collect up to 38 unordered entries; the wikilinks still show the diagram in each lifeline's backlinks without adding properties. `DiagramID` places the message on its diagram (W-61). This is the first rule for the companion note's body; the rest of that note is still open (W-73). Consequences applied, listed as required: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled Sequence entry. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-177 · 2026-09-29 · `transmits`, `receives` and `exchanges` say what data a Port carries; InformationFlow and FlowProperty pins use them.** Spencer's decision. The Port already connects to something (`interfaces`); these fields define what is exchanged with it, and point at the Item Flow note for that data, so the data note can be linked to and queried (Spencer confirmed this reading over one where the fields link two Ports). (1) Three new fields on a Port, each pointing at an Item Flow note: `transmits` for the source or `out` end, `receives` for the target or `in` end, `exchanges` for an `inout` flow, which both Ports carry (Spencer calls it symmetric in that sense). They are listed under `oneWay`: nothing is written back on the Item Flow note, which shows the Ports in its backlinks; generating inverses (`transmittedBy` and so on) was proposed and not taken up, which is my reading of the answer. (2) InformationFlow (123 connectors, stereotype `itemFlow`, no end missing, 123 unique links, none drawn both ways; matrix Rule 42, which wrote nothing, is replaced): every connector becomes `interfaces` between its two Ports, as for the Connector type (W-153, W-154), and the 79 that convey an item (`conveyed` in `t_xref`, W-83; always one Item Flow note, 25 distinct, at most 9 flows on one) also write `transmits` on the source Port and `receives` on the target Port, pointing at that item. None is `exchanges` in this export. The 44 with no item write only `interfaces`. The 9 with an end that is not a Port keep `REVIEW port needed` with the GUID and the review table (W-55, W-157); their `transmits` or `receives` goes on the end as drawn. The 10 names use the W-158 line on both ends. (3) FlowProperty pins (W-132): the direction of a pin (W-90) now becomes a field on the Port that owns it, pointing at the pin's Item Flow note: `out` gives `transmits` (171), `in` gives `receives` (96), `inout` gives `exchanges` (485). This replaces the `source`/`target` fields of W-132 for pins and settles its open question of how `inout` reads with one Port. The 8 pins with no Port owner (all `inout`) write no direction field. `hasFlow` (W-133, ownership) stays as decided and now repeats the pin link; whether to keep both is not decided. Consequences applied, listed as required: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.14) lists the three fields under `oneWay` with a guidance line; `.obsidian/types.json` has them as `multitext`; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` has a settled InformationFlow entry; `ea-element-mapping.yaml` notes the change on Part. With this, every one of the 15 connector types has a rule. Not decided: `Definitions/Properties` notes for the new fields; whether `hasFlow` stays next to the direction fields. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.

**W-178 · 2026-09-29 · Consistency pass over the connector rules; a placement that repeats a generalization is not written, and a Port that both transmits and receives an item exchanges it.** A check of W-151 to W-177 by rebuilding all 21,822 connectors from the export with the logged rules, plus the placement links of W-149, and two fixes Spencer approved. Checked and consistent: every connector lands in exactly one rule (9 are the exceptions with an end missing from the export, W-57); the review-line counts match each entry, 1,187 lines in 18 kinds (largest: nesting direction 252, allocate 181, dependency 118, port needed 117, satisfy 112, trace 103, association 102); links per field from connectors: `subtypeOf` 3,967, `satisfies` 2,454, `derivedFrom` 1,426, `performs` 1,348, `hasChild` 1,094, `appliesTo` 979, `hasDesign` 956, `optionOf` 733, `describes` 722, `interfaces` 662, `precedes` 607, `refines` 556, `participants` 494, `dependsOn` 455, `drives` 415, `tracesTo` 384, `references` 377, `verifies` 333, `affects` 276, `realizedBy` 238, `equals` 231, `hasPart` 81 and the data fields. Expected and left as they are: 18 `precedes` pairs both ways (transitions back and forth), 3 `references` pairs both ways, 5 `precedes` self-loops. (A) 799 placement links (W-149) repeat a generalization: the child is filed inside its general element and is also `subtypeOf` it (Function 507, Design 159, Use Case 110, Object 18, Actor 5); written as placement they would read `Relay` `hasPart` `Relay - DPDT`. Rule: where a placement child is `subtypeOf` its owner, no placement link is written, because `subtypeOf` already says it. That removes 781 `hasChild` and 18 `hasPart` links from W-149's counts (hasChild about 20,384 to about 19,603 before the connectors). No case has the owner as a subtype of the child. (B) 18 Ports both transmit and receive the same item through different InformationFlows. Rule: where a Port would get both `transmits` and `receives` for one item, it gets `exchanges` once instead (Spencer's definition, W-177). The InformationFlow data fields become `transmits` 51, `receives` 60, `exchanges` 18. Raised and not decided: some inverse lists are long (`SW PCE25 CTRL` gets 133 `applies` entries and 178 links in all; `lsWarning` 127 `supertypeOf`), the property overload Spencer named for Use Cases (W-172). Consequences applied, listed as required: the `ParentID` entry in `ea-field-dispositions.yaml` and the `composition` guidance in `relationships.yaml` state rule A; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` states rule B for InformationFlow; `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 7 item 4 has the new `hasChild` count. Existing notes and the translator are not updated.
