**W-196 · 2026-09-29 · Which characters count as unsafe in names (W-48, W-65).** Spencer's decision: only the characters the system does not allow. My reading, applied: the set is what Windows rejects in a file or folder name (the strictest common system, so a vault opens on macOS and Linux too): `\ / : * ? " < > |`, control characters and line breaks, a trailing space or period, and reserved device names (`COM1`, `COM2`). `#`, `^`, `[`, `]` are not replaced. Figures from `t_object.csv` (32,548 named elements): 1,694 names contain a disallowed character (`/` 1,091, `:` 591, `?` 85, `"` 43, `>` 15, `*` 4, `<` 4; none has `|` or `\`); 810 end in a space or period, 3 start with a space, 30 hold a line break; `COM1` and `COM2` are Port names, which W-134 prefixes with the owner, so they do not clash. `t_package.csv` has 64 package names with a disallowed character (`/` 24, `:` 42, `*` 1), which matches W-65; the handoff's 63 was wrong. Problem this creates, not solved: about 133 names contain `[`, `]`, `#` or `^` (Class 113, Requirement 17, UseCase 3); Obsidian probably accepts them in a file name but they cannot be written inside a `[[link]]`, so every generated link to them would break. I have not tested this in Obsidian. Still open: the replacement for each character and the duplicate-name suffix. Applied as consequences: the Open naming item is narrowed and the handoff Next step 2 is updated.

**W-197 · 2026-09-29 · Replacement of unsafe characters in note and folder names.** Spencer approved my proposal (W-196). Applied to every note file name and every package folder name, in this order: (1) `/` becomes `-`; (2) `:` becomes ` -`; (3) `?` and `*` are removed; (4) `"` becomes `''` (two apostrophes); (5) `<` becomes `(` and `>` becomes `)`; (6) `|` and `\`, which no name has today, become `-`; (7) each run of line breaks, control characters and spaces becomes one space; (8) leading spaces and trailing spaces and periods are trimmed. `#`, `^`, `[` and `]` are left as they are (W-196). The original name is kept as `- Name: <original>` when the file name differs (W-48). Tested on `t_object.csv` (32,548 named elements): the rules create one new same-type duplicate name, from a double space, not from a replaced character. Not decided by this entry: the duplicate-name suffix, and what to do with the about 133 names holding `[`, `]`, `#` or `^`. Applied as consequences: `Definitions/Note Layout.md` has a File names section; the Open naming item and the handoff are narrowed.

**W-198 · 2026-09-29 · Duplicate names: the note's `id` is the suffix.** Spencer's decision: "add the id as suffix". My reading, applied: where two or more notes would get the same file name, case ignored, across the whole vault, every note except the oldest in EA creation order (W-14) gets its `id` after the name, separated by one space, for example `Start charge ACT-00456`; the oldest keeps the plain name. This replaces my proposed type marker and counter. The `id` is the vault `id` of W-14 (`<prefix>-<5 digits>`, for example `REQ-00012`), not the EA `Object_ID`. It needs no extra step: ids are numbered in EA creation order after the translator has decided which elements become notes, so they exist before names are fixed, and the same data gives the same names. The prefix also tells mixed-type clashes apart. Ports and pins (W-134), unnamed Parts (W-138) and unnamed elements (W-145) keep their own rules, which are unchanged. The original name stays in `- Name: <original>` (W-48). Figures, after W-197 and without Ports and Parts: 656 groups hold 1,582 of 26,485 named elements, so 926 notes get an id suffix; 299 groups have two or more in one folder. Checked: 1 real name ends like an id, `PDD-01064`; `PDD` is not one of the 25 id prefixes, so it cannot clash. (My first check in this chat said none; the script found this one after the text was written.) Problems, not decided: (1) if stage 2 changes a note's class (`modelCheck` in Task 7 is the main case), its `id` prefix may change while the file name keeps the old prefix, until someone renames it; (2) the repeated structural names (`ImageAsset` 46, `1.0 Purpose` 16) become `ImageAsset ART-00312` and are still not self-explaining; (3) diagrams: a diagram has no `id` until its companion note's class and prefix are decided (Diagram layout gaps), so the diagram duplicates (126 same-package groups, W-71) still have no suffix. Not decided: whether the oldest note should also get its id. Applied as consequences: the File names section of `Definitions/Note Layout.md`, the Open naming item and the handoff.

**W-199 · 2026-09-29 · Every note in a duplicate-name group gets its id, the oldest too.** Spencer's answer "sure" to my question (W-198). Changes W-198: no note in a clash group keeps the plain name; all of them add their `id` after the name, for example `Start charge ACT-00456` and `Start charge REQ-00012`. Figures (W-198, without Ports and Parts): 656 groups, so 1,582 notes get the suffix, not 926. A note with a unique name keeps it. Consequence: no plain-name note is left in a clash group, so `[[Start charge]]` resolves to nothing for those names; every link the tool writes must use the full name with the id. Applied as consequences: the File names section of `Definitions/Note Layout.md`, the Open naming item and the handoff.

**W-200 · 2026-09-29 · Correction to W-198 and W-199: not every `id` is `<prefix>-<5 digits>`.** I wrote that the suffix is an id of the form `REQ-00012` and that it needs no extra step because ids are numbered before names are fixed. That holds only for internal ids. W-17 and W-27 give external Requirement notes an id made from the source's designator (for example `UL2594_13.1`), and W-82 gives a note that shares a designator an internal `STD-#####` or `STK-#####` instead. Which Requirement gets which id therefore depends on the per-document designator parse rule (W-81, W-82), which is still open. Effect on the suffix: the suffix is whatever `id` the note ends up with, so a Requirement in a clash group may be suffixed `Name UL2594_13.1`, and its file name cannot be fixed before the parse rule is decided. Size: 447 of the 1582 notes in clash groups are Requirements (103 are Regulatory or Webasto Requirements, the two stereotypes that take designators). `.` and `_` are safe in file names. No decision changes; the W-198 and W-199 rule stands with this dependency. Applied as consequences: the File names section, the Open naming item and the handoff name the dependency.

**W-201 · 2026-09-29 · Designator parse rule for Regulatory and Webasto Requirements (W-17, W-81, W-82).** Spencer approved two rules. (1) Regulatory Requirement (3,363): the last word of the name is the clause and everything before it is the standard; the designator is the standard with spaces removed, an underscore, and the clause, for example `UL 2594 24.1.0-01` gives `UL2594_24.1.0-01`. (2) Webasto Requirement (1,485): the leading number in the name is the clause and the standard comes from the parent package folder (the standard number in the folder name, for example `UL 2251 - 2017`), same designator form. My reading, not confirmed: the folder's standard is taken the same way as in rule 1 (name of the standard without the year), and comparison ignores case. Notes that do not parse (1 Regulatory, 61 Webasto) take the internal `STD-#####` or `STK-#####` id (W-17). Notes that share a designator take internal ids too (W-82). Tested on the export: regulatory 3,362 parse and give 3,362 designators, none shared; Webasto 1,424 parse and give 1,418 designators, 6 shared by 12 notes (for example `06 General` and `06 INSTRUCTIONS` in `UL 2594 - 2016`). So the tested count of shared designators is 12, not W-26's 79; I have not found the source of the difference, which may lie in stereotypes I did not test. Not tested: the `requirement` stereotype (7,648), `designConstraint`, `functionalRequirement`, and the stakeholder documents (GSE_SW alone 1,877), so those parse rules are still open. Problem, not decided: 214 regulatory designators contain `/` (`GB/T18487_11.6.1.0-1`); a `/` is fine in the `id` value but is replaced by `-` wherever the id becomes part of a file name (W-197), so the id and the file name would differ; whether the slash is kept in the id is open. Applied as consequences: the Open designator item is narrowed and the handoff is updated.

**W-202 · 2026-09-29 · A designator keeps `/` in the `id`.** Spencer's decision: keep it true to the original name. The 214 GB/T designators are written as they are, `GB/T18487_11.6.1.0-1`. Where an `id` becomes part of a file name (only the duplicate-name suffix, W-198), W-197 still turns `/` into `-`, so the two can differ; none of the 214 notes is in a duplicate-name group today (checked: the group members are Requirements of other stereotypes). Applied as consequences: the Open item and the handoff no longer ask about the slash.

**W-203 · 2026-09-29 · Standard requirements take an internal `STD-#####` id; no designator parsing.** Spencer's decision: the id for standards may simply be `STD-00000`, and the import will not rely on a simple tool to parse designators. This withdraws the parse rules of W-201 and the external-id form of W-17 for standards. My reading, applied: every Requirement note with subtype `standard` (8,535 Requirement elements plus the 2,383 InformationItems of W-115, 10,918 notes) takes `STD-#####`, numbered in EA creation order with ties by EA GUID (W-14), five digits (W-15). The designator stays only where it already is, in the note's name (for example `UL 2594 24.1.0-01`); no `- Designator:` line is written, since it needed the parse. Reading to confirm: subtype `stakeholder` (1,744) takes `STK-#####` the same way, because W-17's stakeholder ids also needed a parse of the source document name; other Requirement subtypes keep `REQ-#####`. Consequences: (1) W-201 is superseded and W-202 is moot (no id holds a `/`); (2) W-82's tie-break and its `Designator` line are no longer needed (12 shared designators among the tested standard notes, 25 notes, cause no problem now); (3) W-200's dependency is removed: every suffix under W-198 and W-199 is now an internal id of the form `<prefix>-<5 digits>`, so a Requirement clash reads `Start charge STD-00456`; (4) W-27's external form (`UL2594_13.1`) is not used for notes; (5) the Open items on the designator parse rule, W-26's 79 shared clauses and second editions of a standard are closed as not needed; (6) not applied yet: `element-types.yaml` records only the class prefix `REQ` for Requirement, so the subtype prefixes `STD` and `STK` are not in the schema; whether they go there, and the effect on Note Layout's id line and the Requirement template, is for the templates and layout step; (7) the search by clause is by name only. Applied as consequences: the Open items, the File names section, the `Designator` line in `Definitions/EA Source Section.md`, and the handoff.

**W-204 · 2026-09-29 · Stakeholder requirements take `STK-#####`.** Spencer confirmed my reading of W-203: the 1,744 Requirement notes with subtype `stakeholder` take `STK-#####`, numbered in EA creation order with ties by EA GUID. Other Requirement subtypes keep `REQ-#####`. Still open from W-203: recording `STD` and `STK` as subtype prefixes in `element-types.yaml` (templates and layout step). Applied as consequences: the Open item on `STK` is narrowed and the handoff is updated.

**W-205 · 2026-09-29 · `[`, `]`, `#` and `^` are also replaced in file names.** Spencer approved my proposal, an exception to the "only what the system rejects" rule of W-196 because these characters cannot be used inside a `[[link]]` (my belief, not tested in Obsidian). Added to the W-197 rules, after step 6: `[` becomes `(`, `]` becomes `)`, `#` becomes `no.`, `^` is removed; repeated spaces are then collapsed as in step 7. The original name is kept as `- Name: <original>` (W-48). Applies to 137 element names (`[` and `]` 130 each, `#` 5, `^` 2; Class 113, Requirement 17, UseCase 3, Artifact 3, Text 1) and to no package name. Tested on the export: no new duplicate name of the same type. Applied as consequences: the File names section of `Definitions/Note Layout.md`, and the Open and handoff items about these names are removed. Naming (handoff item 2) is settled except diagram duplicates, which wait for the companion note's id prefix (item 4).

**W-206 · 2026-09-29 · The user section is an empty `## Notes` heading.** Spencer approved my proposal (layout gap 3, first point). The translator writes one heading line, `## Notes`, on every translated note, with nothing under it, so the place for people's additions exists and the aliases block (W-110, W-111) has something to follow. It matches the `## Notes` heading in the templates. Problems named, accepted: about 31,000 notes get an empty heading; the name can be confused with the EA note text (normal text, no heading) and with the `**EA notes:**` label; the alternative `## User notes` was not chosen. Applied as consequences: part 6 of `Definitions/Note Layout.md` and its Not decided list, and the handoff.

**W-207 · 2026-09-29 · Format of aliases and `formerIds`.** Spencer approved my proposal (layout gap 3, second point). The translator writes neither. When a note has an entry, the block reads `## Aliases` with one bullet per other name (`- <other name>`), then `## Former ids` with one bullet per earlier id as plain text (`- REQ-00042`, not a link), in that order, directly below the user section (W-110, W-111, W-206). Problems named, accepted: nothing marks the place before the first entry, so the hand-made template carries both headings and a translated note gets them when first needed; Obsidian offers alias names in link suggestions only from an `aliases` property, so body aliases are not found that way (W-110); the `id` snippet (W-20) and AI_INSTRUCTIONS step 3 still read `formerIds` from the properties and must read `## Former ids` instead, not yet changed. Applied as consequences: part 7 of `Definitions/Note Layout.md` and its Not decided list, and the Open item below and the handoff.

**W-208 · 2026-09-29 · Order of the lines in `Source: EA`.** Spencer approved my proposal (layout gap 3, third point). Below the `EA GUID` line, in this order: (1) the element's own EA values: the `Alias` line, then the tag lines in Note Layout order (W-101, W-104); (2) values from EA structure on the element: `quantity of`, then the State action lines (`Entry action:`, `Do action:`, `Exit action:`) and the Class `Attribute:` lines, then the link lines (`Represents:`, `Target pin:` and the inverse forms, W-194); (3) connector values; (4) discussion posts; (5) `REVIEW port needed`, always the last line of the section. Reasons: grouped by origin, the same on every note, and the temporary `REVIEW` line is deleted alone when a connector moves to a port. Not checked: how often each kind occurs. Not settled by this entry: the format of the `Name` line (`Name: <original>` before the GUID in the layout, `- Name: <original>` in the rules list; W-48, W-99). Applied as consequences: `Definitions/EA Source Section.md` gets an Order section, the Not decided list of `Definitions/Note Layout.md` loses this point, and the handoff is updated.

**W-209 · 2026-09-29 · Definitions of the kept body lines: one table in `Definitions/EA Source Section.md`.** Spencer chose option A of three (a table in the section document; one note per line in `Definitions/Properties`; a separate `EA Body Lines` note). Written as a table with the columns line, meaning, rows and example value, for the 16 inline lines and the four labeled comment texts (`Part Number` covers `PN` and `PartNumber`). Rows are counted from `t_objectproperties_raw.csv` with a real value only (see the table's own note). The meaning column holds only what Spencer has said (`Quantity`, `Piece Cost`, and the MoSCoW reading of `Priority` from W-106); every other meaning is blank, not guessed, and is his to fill. Problems named, accepted: a table cannot be searched per line the way notes can, and the section document is longer; the table goes stale as the review cleanup deletes lines, so it is removed with them. Applied as consequences: the new section in `Definitions/EA Source Section.md`, the Not decided point removed from `Definitions/Note Layout.md`, and the handoff.

**W-210 · 2026-09-29 · Attachment counter: on every file, in RTF order.** Spencer chose option A. Every attachment is named `<owning note's final file name> asset <n>`, including a note with only one file (`asset 1`), as W-77 says. For a note whose RTF holds several pictures, the counter follows the order of the pictures inside the RTF, starting at 1 (my reading, applied). Because the owning note's file name includes its `id` where names clash (W-198, W-199), attachment names of different notes are unique; the counter only tells apart files of the same note. Figures from `Linked Document Attachments.csv` and W-76, W-77: 376 linked documents, each on a different note; 25 of the 390 files belong to the 11 notes that hold 2 or 3 pictures (8 with two, 3 with three); the other 365 are the only file on their note. Problems named, accepted: the `1` on single files tells nothing; not checked: whether the byte order of the pictures in an RTF matches their order on the page. Applied as consequences: the Attachments line of `Definitions/Note Layout.md` and the handoff (item 3 loses the attachment counter).

**W-211 · 2026-09-29 · Line format for values kept on a merged Port (W-121).** Spencer approved my proposal. On the block Port's note, in `Source: EA`, in group 2 of the W-208 order (values from EA structure, after the link lines), one line per differing instance Port and field, in EA creation order: `- Instance name in [[<assembly>]]: <value>`, `- Instance type in [[<assembly>]]: [[<Port note>]]`, `- Instance stereotype in [[<assembly>]]: <value>`, `- Instance redefinition in [[<assembly>]]: [[<Port note>]]`. When the owning Part has a role name, it follows the assembly link in brackets, `(part <name>)`; a Part with no name adds nothing (63 of 130 in a quick check). `<assembly>` is the note of the Class or Object that owns the Part (for example `[[PCBA PT4]]`). Problems named, accepted: one block Port can get many lines from one assembly (up to 10 differing names in a quick check); the assembly link is a real link, so the assembly note is linked from the Port note, which was not confirmed as wanted. Not checked: my quick count found 130 differing instance names against W-121's 101 (looser filter, cause not found). Not covered by this entry: the connector context of W-114, which stays open. Applied as consequences: `Definitions/EA Source Section.md` (order and line kinds) and the handoff (item 3 is done).

**W-212 · 2026-09-29 · A new class `Diagram` (prefix `DIA`) for the diagram companion note.** Spencer approved option A of three (a new class; `Info` with subtype `diagram`; `Artifact` with subtype `diagram`). The class is `Diagram`, prefix `DIA`, subtype the EA diagram type in lower case: `custom`, `logical`, `use case`, `composite structure`, `statechart`, `activity`, `sequence`, `package` (2,924 diagrams: Custom 1,230, Logical 1,038, Use Case 267, CompositeStructure 151, Statechart 109, Activity 95, Sequence 24, Package 10). The `id` is `DIA-#####`, numbered in EA creation order (W-14). This settles the duplicate names of diagrams (W-71, 126 same-package groups): a clash takes the id suffix of W-198 and W-199, `<name> DIA-00012`. My choices, not confirmed: the folder `72_Diagrams`, and the subtype spelling `composite structure`. Applied as consequences: `element-types.yaml` (class added, `schemaVersion` 1.12 to 1.13, 26 classes), `Snippet - id` (prefix map; the snippets have not been run inside Obsidian), a new template `99_System/05_Templates/Diagram.md` in the form of the Artifact template, `Definitions/Note Layout.md` and the handoff. Not applied: the Folder Map canvas, `00 - Views and Bases.md` and the Folder Contents base (no entry for `72_Diagrams`), `types.json` (it types properties, not classes; no change expected, not checked), and the body of the note beyond the message list. Correction to any earlier text in this file that says 25 classes: the schema now holds 26 including `modelCheck`; the old entries are not edited.

**W-213 · 2026-09-29 · Body of the diagram companion note: a `Canvas:` link line.** Spencer approved my proposal. After the description (`Notes`, main text) and before the `**Messages:**` list (W-176), the note has one line, `Canvas: [[<canvas file name>.canvas]]`. It is a link, not an embed. It is written only when the canvas exists, as W-80 does for the `Default diagram` line, so no dangling link: 134 of the 2,924 diagrams have no drawn object (Statechart 56, Logical 50, Custom 19, Use Case 3, Activity 3, Package 2, Sequence 1) and the 23 Sequence diagrams with objects have no canvas form yet (W-73), 157 notes in all without the line. The canvas has the same base name as the note, including the `DIA` id suffix where names clash (W-212), so note and canvas names do not differ. No other line is added: the objects are links inside the canvas, and the diagram type is the subtype. Problems named, accepted: 157 notes hold only description and metadata, some of which stage 2 may delete; not checked: whether Obsidian in the installed version shows a backlink from a canvas file to the notes it references. My positions, not confirmed: the line's place before the Messages list. Applied as consequences: a Diagram section in `Definitions/Note Layout.md` and the handoff.

**W-214 · 2026-09-29 · Canvas text card for a folded EA note: the note's text.** Spencer approved option A of three (the text; a link to the element; text and link). A folded note (W-161, W-162) that is drawn on a diagram becomes a canvas text card holding its text as EA drew it, a snapshot at import, handled like other EA Note text; the copy in the element body under `**EA notes:**` stays the record (W-162), and the ledger keeps the original (W-35). Figures from the export, my count: 247 folded notes, 223 drawn on a diagram (the log said 225; the 2 difference is not explained), drawn 297 times in all, 47 on two or more diagrams; text length median 103 characters, longest 911, 31 over 300. Problems named, accepted: the text exists in up to 298 places (297 cards and the element body), an edit to one does not reach the others; long cards take room; not checked: how the `NoteLink` line from the card to the element is drawn under the general connector rule. Applied as consequences: the Diagram section of `Definitions/Note Layout.md` and the handoff (item 4 is done).

**W-215 · 2026-09-29 · Layout of the ledger and the run manifest (W-35).** Spencer approved my proposal. Both files are generated on every run into `99_System/11_Import`. `Ledger.csv`: one row per element, connector, diagram and package (about 62,000: 35,969 elements, 21,822 connectors, 2,924 diagrams, 1,387 packages), sorted by GUID so a rerun makes small diffs; columns `ea_guid, source_kind, ea_type, ea_name, outcome, rule, uid, id, folded_into_uid`; `outcome` is one of `note`, `folded`, `link`, `canvas`, `not carried`; `rule` is the decision number (for example `W-136`); `uid`, `id` and `folded_into_uid` are blank where they do not apply. `Run Manifest.md`: a short readable page with the date, the tool version, the input identity (the export's file names and sizes) and counts by outcome. Not confirmed by Spencer: the outcome names and the `folded_into_uid` column are mine (W-35 only says "disposition"). Problem named: a ledger of 62,000 rows regenerated on each run lands in git history at every commit, which is why only validated runs are committed (W-35). Not decided: what happens to the four header-only files in the same folder (`Identity Registry.csv`, `Model Checks.csv`, `Pending Relationships.csv`, `Transformation Log.csv`), which overlap with the ledger at least in the case of `Transformation Log.csv`. No file is created now; the tool writes them. Applied as consequences: the handoff (item 5 loses the ledger point) and the Open list.

**W-216 · 2026-09-29 · Review tables: final names, read-only, shared first columns.** Spencer approved my proposal. (1) Names, now final and all in `99_System/11_Import`: `Review - Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv` (Task 1, 118 connectors), `Review - Added Ports.csv` (Task 6, one row per instance Port folded into an added Port note: 659 Ports in 617 notes), `Review - Nesting Direction.csv` (252 connectors) and `Review - Equals Direction.csv` (Task 8, 249 connectors). (2) Each table is read-only evidence, regenerated on every run, with no status or decision column: a column filled in by hand would be overwritten by the next run. Decisions go in `Review Changes Log.md` keyed by the same GUID (W-38). (3) Every table starts with the columns `ea_guid, ea_type, ea_name, category`; the other columns are set one table at a time (W-156 lists ends as drawn, resolved notes, candidate notes and a derived answer). Problem named, accepted: there is no status column, so open rows are found by comparing the table with the log by GUID; no Base view can filter on "done". Applied as consequences: `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 1 loses "provisional name", and the handoff and Open items say the names are final and the other columns are open.

**W-217 · 2026-09-29 · Columns of `Review - Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv`.** Spencer approved my proposal. After `ea_guid, ea_type, ea_name, category` (W-216), for each end (`start` and `end`): `<end>_element` (the element as drawn, `type | name`), `<end>_owner` (the owner as drawn, `type | name`), `<end>_assembly` (the assembly above the owner, `type | name`, blank if none), `<end>_note` (the file name of the note the end resolved to, blank if the end is missing from the export) and `<end>_candidate_ports` (the Port notes on that block end, separated by `;`); then `connector_values` (the connector's Name and Notes joined by ` | `). The snapshot's `Port_Ends` and the two port-count columns are dropped: the category says which case it is and the candidate list gives the count. Problems named, accepted: the candidate list can be long on a block with many Ports and I have not counted the longest; I did not count how many of the 118 have an assembly. Correction: in my message I wrote that the assembly was blank for 4 of the 5 rows I had looked at; I had looked at 4 rows, and 3 of them had none. Applied as consequences: `Post-Import Tasks.md` Task 1 now lists the columns, and the handoff and Open items are updated.

**W-218 · 2026-09-29 · Columns of `Review - Added Ports.csv`.** Spencer approved my proposal. One row per instance Port folded into an added Port note (W-114 step 3, W-130). `ea_guid`, `ea_type` (`Port`) and `ea_name` are the instance Port's own, so `ea_name` is blank for the unnamed ones; then `note` (the file name of the added Port note; rows with the same value were folded together, at most 4), `owner` (the Part or Object that owns the instance Port, as drawn, `type | name`), `assembly` (the owner's parent object, `type | name`, blank if none: 92 of 659), `block_note` (the block note the Port was added to) and `block_ports` (the Port notes already on that block, `;` between them, the merge candidates). `category` has four values, with counts reproduced from the export: unnamed instance Port 367; named and the block has no Ports 148; named and the block has Ports but none matches 132; named and several Ports on the block have that name 12 (too many matches for rule 2). The W-114 rule reproduces as 1,697 merged by `PDATA3`, 59 by name, 659 added (617 new Port notes) and 26 with no block. Problems named, accepted: the 367 unnamed Ports have no `ea_name` to search for, so `note` is the name to use; W-114's own text says 671 added, W-130 says 659, and I reproduced 659; the difference of 12 is probably the same-named Ports, not confirmed. Applied as consequences: Task 6 of `Post-Import Tasks.md` lists the columns and categories, and the handoff and Open items are updated.

**W-219 · 2026-09-29 · Columns of `Review - Nesting Direction.csv`.** Spencer approved my proposal. One row per independent `Nesting` connector (W-151, W-157). After `ea_guid, ea_type, ea_name, category` (`ea_type` is `Nesting`, `ea_name` blank): `start_element` and `end_element` (the ends as drawn, `type | name`; start is the child, end the parent), `start_note` and `end_note` (the file names of the notes they resolved to), `link_written` (`hasChild`/`childOf`, or `hasPart`/`partOf` when both notes are Objects, W-149), `child_other_parent` (the note of the child's existing `ParentID` owner, blank if none) and `diagrams` (the names of the diagrams where the connector is drawn, `;` between them). `category` has two values: child has no other parent (200) and child already has a different parent (52), as in W-151. No Port, Part or assembly columns: every endpoint is a note (W-151). Count: W-151 has 252; my quick check on the export gave 254 (201 and 53); the likely cause is the 2 connectors drawn parent to child, which W-151 treats as repeats, not confirmed. In my check 189 connectors are on one diagram, 44 on two, a few on up to 13, and 3 on none. Problems named, accepted: the long `diagrams` cells; the start-is-the-child direction is an inference from 582 of 584 checkable cases, so the table shows evidence and cannot confirm it. Applied as consequences: Task 7 of `Post-Import Tasks.md` lists the columns, and the handoff and Open items are updated.

**W-220 · 2026-09-29 · Columns of `Review - Equals Direction.csv`.** Spencer approved my proposal. One row per `BindingConnector` (249, all of EA type `Connector`; 2 have a name, none has Notes). After `ea_guid, ea_type, ea_name, category`: `start_element`, `start_owner`, `start_assembly` and `end_element`, `end_owner`, `end_assembly` (the ends as drawn, `type | name`, as in `Review - Block-Level Flow Connectors.csv`), `start_note` and `end_note` (the Port notes they resolved to after the Port merge, W-114), `derived_outer_note` and `derived_inner_note` (the direction the tool derived, blank where none), `containment_path` (the chain of blocks from the outer block to the inner one, `>` between names; for a one-level answer just the assembly), `connector_values` (the Name, with the Notes if any). `category` has three values: one level of containment 185, several levels of containment 60, no containment path 4. Real rows checked: `+5VDC1` and `Current1` on `PCBA PT4` (outer end the block's Port, inner end the Port of an unnamed Part) and `L1 Grid` on `PCBA - PCE25 Power` (Part `AC In`). Problems named, accepted: the 185, 60 and 4 come from W-156 and were not reproduced in this chat; the multi-level rule behind the 60 has not been spot-checked, and `containment_path` is what lets a person check each of them. Correction to my wording: 64 connectors have no one-level answer (W-153); 60 of them get a multi-level path and 4 get none, so the `derived_*` columns are blank only for the 4. All four review tables now have their columns (W-217 to W-220). Applied as consequences: Task 8 of `Post-Import Tasks.md` lists the columns, and the handoff and Open items are updated.

**W-221 · 2026-09-29 · Open-list clean-up before the change of chat.** A check and clean-up, not a decision, in the manner of W-183. **W-222 · 2026-09-30 · The Requirement template lists no relationship fields.** Spencer's decision (answer "b"), the first class of the W-182 template list. Of three options (A `derivedFrom` only, B none, C both), Spencer chose B. Basis, my counts and not re-run against the export: of 13,988 Requirements, 3,584 have any connector other than Nesting or NoteLink (from the last chat); `derivedFrom` is under 11%; `appliesTo` is written by about 980 connectors in `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` (918 + 23 + 13 + 25 + 1; 917 unique links for the first rule), at most about 7% of Requirements, and the count of unique notes was not made. Neither field meets W-182's "nearly always". My recommendation changed from A to B once `appliesTo` was counted. Applied as consequences: none in `Requirement.md`, which already holds no relationship field; the handoff's proposed list now shows Requirement as none. A Requirement still takes `derivedFrom` or `appliesTo` when a link exists (both are in `relationships.yaml`). Not decided: the other classes of the W-182 list, one at a time.

My basis for B, not verified: about 30% of the 4,387 raw Port objects (1,308) are an end of a `Connector`-type connector, before the Port merge (W-114) reduces the notes, and no Generalization connector touches a Port (other sources such as `t_xref` were not checked). **W-226 · 2026-09-30 · The Use Case template lists `participants`.** Spencer's decision, the fifth class of the W-182 template list. **W-248 · 2026-09-30 · The import goes into a fresh vault with MDSE Bootstrap and the plugins installed; that vault is set up for testing once the tool works.** Spencer's statement, recorded as a decision. Open: what the fresh vault holds before a run. The translator needs the person notes (the EA author name is looked up in `eaNames`, `Note Layout` item 5), `authors.yaml` and the templates; Bootstrap creates only the installing person's note. Not decided: where the tool reads them from, and how the test vault is set up. Not answered in this turn: Spencer's approval of W-246 step 3 and of checks 2 to 5 in section 10 of the file; both stay pending.

**W-249 · 2026-09-30 · The path to a testable vault: a script builds a base vault, the tool fills a copy of it, the first runs use a slice of packages.** Spencer's answer "yes" to my proposal; my reading is that it covers all three parts. (1) A script run once builds a clean base vault from this workspace, in a new empty git repo. (2) The translator does not create the vault, the Obsidian settings or the plugins (Bootstrap's job); it fills a copy of the base vault and reads the person notes (`eaNames`), `authors.yaml` and the templates from it, which is option A of my last question. (3) Each test run is a disposable copy, checked by the checks in section 10 of the Translator Definition and then discarded; the first runs cover one or two packages, so the tool needs a package filter. Applied as consequences: `Translator Definition.md` (section 1, section 12; current through W-249); the handoff (step 8) and the Open list. Contents proposed for the base vault, not approved: `.obsidian` without `workspace.json`, plugin `data.json` and `author-code.txt`; `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `AGENTS.md`; `Definitions`; in `99_System`: `02_AI`, `04_People`, `05_Templates`, `08_Scripts`, `09_Tools`, the `Enabled Plugin Stack` note, the Bootstrap spec, Ruleset 1.21, and in `03_Schemas` only `authors.yaml`, `element-types.yaml` and `relationships.yaml` (`AI_INSTRUCTIONS` and Nodian read these three). Left out: `CSV_EA`, `10_EA Native Translator`, the Decision Log, handoff and Translator Definition, the mapping and disposition YAML files (tool inputs), the import outputs, `MDSE Workbench`, the README and the cross-vault files. Not decided: whether that list is right, and `.vault.yaml` (the README says never to reuse a populated one). Not checked: what Bootstrap installs beyond its spec, how Obsidian behaves on a vault of about 30,000 notes, and whether the tool can apply a package filter without changing id numbering (W-14 numbers notes in EA creation order across the whole model). Noticed, not changed: 14 commits by Spencer's account (pull request 1, 2026-09-30) added a `MDSE Workbench` folder at the repository root; I merged it (fast-forward) and did not edit it. Its README says it is not authoritative for model semantics. Still pending: W-246 step 3 and checks 2 to 5 in section 10.

**W-251 · 2026-09-30 · No package filter: every test run is a full run.** Spencer's decision (answer "c" to options A number from the whole model and write a slice, B number the slice on its own, C no filter). It replaces the third part of W-249 (first runs on a slice of packages); W-249 stays as history. My recommendation was A. Problems named, not resolved: each test cycle costs a full run of about 30,300 notes, plus the ledger of about 62,000 rows, so the speed of the tool and of Obsidian on a vault that size decides how fast we can test, and neither is measured; a fault found late costs a full rerun. **W-252 · 2026-09-30 · The tool takes a package filter and numbers from the whole model; W-251 is replaced.** Spencer's answer "yes" to changing W-251 to option A. Decision: the tool opens the `.qeax` read-only, numbers every note from the whole model (W-14) and writes only the selected packages; a slice keeps its final ids and has gaps; a full run is the same tool with no filter. W-251 stays as history. Applied as consequences: the Translator Definition (section 1, section 10 check 3, section 12; current through W-252), the handoff and the Open list. Not decided: how packages are selected; what happens to links to notes outside the slice (check 3 of section 10 cannot hold as written); folds that cross the slice edge (a Part folded into an assembly, or an instance Port merged into a block, in a package that is not selected); what the ledger and run manifest hold for a slice. Not measured: the time to read and number the whole model on every run. Still pending: W-246 step 3 and checks 2 to 5 in section 10.

**W-266 · 2026-09-30 · Package paths use `>` and EA names.** Spencer's answer "b" to options A `/` as the separator with `\/` for a slash inside a name, B `>` as the separator with no escaping, C the vault folder path with `/`. My recommendation was B. Decision: a path is written `Name > Name > Name`; `>` separates names, with optional spaces around it; each name is matched on the EA package name after trimming leading and trailing spaces, case kept; nothing needs escaping. Checked in the CSV bundle, not the `.qeax`: no package name contains `>`; 24 contain `/` and 42 contain `:`; 6 have a leading or trailing space (for example `Gen IV - Diagnostics `), and trimming makes no two sibling packages equal, nor does ignoring case, so either rule would give the same matches there. Applied as consequences: section 1 of the Translator Definition (current through W-266), the handoff and the Open list. My reading, not confirmed: a path starts at one of the ten packages under `IPC !`, because `Model` and `IPC !` are not folders (W-117); case is kept when matching; how several paths are given to the tool (one per line, or a repeated setting) is not decided.
