**W-271 · 2026-09-30 · The native importer is a developer-only local HTML tool; v0.1 is the read-only QEAX preflight.** Spencer asked to use the HTML approach that worked well for the earlier translator and clarified that only the developer uses this tool, so diagnostic controls do not need to be hidden. Applied: `99_System/09_Tools/EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.1.html`. It reuses the proven offline streaming SQLite page reader from the earlier `qeax-extract.html`, so a large `.qea`/`.qeax` is read through browser `File.slice()` without loading the complete repository into memory and without a CDN/WASM dependency. v0.1 is intentionally preflight only and writes no MDSE notes. It reports the SQLite schema; checks the W-257/W-268 expected row counts for the 11 translator-used tables; checks all 31 EA object-type counts, all 15 connector-type counts and all 8 diagram-type counts; scans the five tag properties previously seen at the 254–255-character boundary; warns on SQLite WAL mode; and exports diagnostic JSON plus counts, schema and text-length CSV files. The interface keeps source, counts, schema, issues and the developer run log visible. No Stage-1 mapping rule changed. Next implementation step after running v0.1 on the actual source is the translation-planning build; the first note-producing acceptance slice remains `02 Product Context` (W-267).

**W-272 · 2026-09-30 · Relationship endpoint rules live in `relationships.yaml`, on each relationship.** Spencer's answer "yes" to option A (A: allowed source and target classes on each relationship in `relationships.yaml`; B: allowed relationships per class in `element-types.yaml`; C: a separate class-by-class matrix file), on the condition that Nodian is checked first. My recommendation was A. Raised by the MDSE Workbench review: `relationships.yaml` names relationships and their inverses but not which classes each may connect, so Workbench cannot offer only valid relationships (Workbench WB-053) without inventing rules, which WB-071 forbids. Decided: each relationship states the classes it may connect, owner side first; the inverse needs no rule of its own (it is the forward rule reversed); per-class views (what a Function can connect to) are generated from this file, not kept separately; the template field lists (W-222 to W-241) are checked against it. Nodian check (source of Nodian 1.4.14, the version pinned in `.obsidian/plugin-lock.yaml`): Nodian never reads `relationships.yaml`. Its pairs live only in its own `data.json`, so extra keys in `relationships.yaml` cannot break it; W-272 is safe for Nodian. No code in this repository parses `relationships.yaml` today; the native importer (W-271) is preflight only. Readers written later must ignore keys they do not know. Found in the check, not decided (see Open): Nodian needs a tag on both notes of a pair and syncs only when the note's `tags` match, but MDSE notes carry `tags: []` and are classified by `type` (W-97); its pairs sit in `data.json`, which is local per machine (`.gitignore`, W-250 base vault list), so a new machine has no pairs and nothing ties them to `relationships.yaml`. My reading: Nodian as configured by the files in this repository generates no inverse fields. Not checked: the `data.json` on Spencer's machines. Not yet done: the key format and the rules themselves (to be proposed from the template field lists and the translated data, data before governance); `oneWay` entries are plain names today, so giving them rules changes them into small maps. Applied as consequences: the handoff (step 9) and the Open list; the Workbench Decision Log (WB-053, WB-085).


**W-273 · 2026-09-30 · The actual QEAX is the verified Stage-1 source baseline; the 255-character values are source truncation; native importer v0.2 adds read-only whole-model planning.** Spencer approved proceeding after the v0.1 preflight of `EA_2026_09_06_endgame.qeax`. The direct read of the actual QEAX matched the W-268 baseline exactly for all 11 translator-used table counts, all 31 EA object-type counts, all 15 connector-type counts and all 8 diagram-type counts; the SQLite file was not in WAL mode. This closes the question of whether the CSV bundle represented the source counts correctly: it did for the Stage-1 count baseline. The direct QEAX also showed that `User Story`, `Product Management Comment`, `Sales Comment` and `Engineering Comment` themselves stop at 254/255 characters, with no value over 255 and no continuation in the tagged-value Notes field. Therefore the old extractor did not create this truncation; it already exists in the source repository. Stage 1 preserves those values exactly, does not infer missing text, and reports the condition as a source-data advisory rather than an import failure. The inherited SQLite DDL parser is corrected to recognize EA column names quoted with single quotes (for example `'Constraint'`, `'Partition'` and `'Default'`), so physical columns are not silently unnamed. Applied: `99_System/09_Tools/EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.2.html`. v0.2 remains read-only and writes no vault. After preflight it reads `t_package`, `t_object`, `t_connector`, `t_diagram` and `t_xref`; constructs the package tree; applies the approved mechanical element rules including package-sensitive classification, NoteLink folding, Part folding and W-114 Port merging/grouping; resolves connector endpoints through folds and Port merges/groups; applies the settled connector rule families; fails on unmapped element/connector rows or source-row coverage gaps; shows rule-coverage and Port-merge summaries; and exports whole-model plan JSON plus a ledger-style CSV. It does not yet render Markdown, Canvas, attachments or a vault. W-114 combines some newly-added instance Ports with the same non-blank name on one block into one future Port entity but never selected which source Port supplies that note's canonical identity. v0.2 therefore groups and resolves those Ports without inventing that identity, and reports multi-source groups as an explicit implementation item. The first note-producing acceptance slice remains `02 Product Context` (W-267).


**W-274 · 2026-09-30 · Native importer v0.2 whole-model planning passed on the actual QEAX and is the accepted planning baseline.** Spencer ran `EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.2.html` against `EA_2026_09_06_endgame.qeax` and supplied `EA_MDSE_Translation_Plan_PASS.json`. Result: `PASS` in 1,948 ms, with 35,969 elements, 21,822 connectors, 1,387 packages, 2,924 diagrams and 42,052 xref rows planned. The plan produces 30,298 element-derived MDSE entities (including the 34 package notes) and 2,924 diagram companion notes. Port planning is consistent with W-114: 4,387 source Ports become 1,972 source-Port notes, 1,756 merged Ports and 659 source Ports grouped into 617 newly-added Port entities; 27 of those added-Port groups contain more than one source Port. There are no planner failures or warnings. The only planner issue is informational: W-114 never chose which source Port supplies the canonical note identity for those 27 multi-source added-Port groups; this remains open and must be settled before final whole-model uid/id assignment and note rendering. The accepted plan also corrects a prior bookkeeping error in W-161/W-148 without changing the NoteLink rule: 250 folded NoteLink connector rows represent 247 unique EA Note elements, not 250 unique Notes. Therefore Note/Text/Constraint leave 246 Info notes, total Info notes are 688, and the correct element-derived total is 30,298 rather than the earlier 30,295/30,545 figures. Applied as consequences: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml`, `ea-element-mapping.yaml`, the handoff and Translator Definition are updated. v0.2 itself is not modified after this acceptance run; it remains the tested read-only planning baseline. The next importer step is to settle the 27-group canonical identity rule, then add the `02 Product Context` note-producing slice.

**W-275 · 2026-09-30 · Inverse relationship fields are written by the translator and Workbench, not Nodian (trial).** Spencer's answer "We can try B" to three options raised in W-272 (A: tag every note with its class and keep Nodian; B: the translator and Workbench write inverses; C: do not store inverses and compute them). My recommendation was B. My reading of "try": B is the direction now and is judged after the first test slice (`02 Product Context`, W-267); it can be reversed then. Decided: inverse fields stay persisted in YAML (unchanged), and the component that writes a forward field writes its inverse in the same step: for a paired field the inverse field on the other note, for a symmetric field the same field on both notes, for a one-way field nothing. The forward field is the authority; when forward and inverse disagree, the inverse is regenerated from the forward. The translator writes inverses in the same run. Workbench writes them in the same step as its own edits, about a second after a hand edit made on the same machine, and reports inverses missing after a pull or an outside AI edit as a Review finding with a fix and a "Regenerate inverses" command, rather than rewriting them on every machine. AI tools that edit notes write the inverse themselves. Until Workbench exists, a regenerate script does the same job. Nodian stays pinned but is not relied on during the trial; its removal is decided after it. Applied: `relationships.yaml` (storage line, schemaVersion 1.24); `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md`; `Translator Definition.md` section 6; `Enabled Plugin Stack.md` (Nodian row); the handoff (step 9); the Workbench notes (WB-085 and the build, architecture and risk text). Not changed: Ruleset 1.22 says inverses are generated and synchronized before handoff, which still holds; the property definitions say the inverse is generated, which still holds. Not decided: the regenerate script (language, where it lives, when it runs); whether a translator check that every forward field has its inverse is added to section 10.


**W-276 · 2026-09-30 · Canonical identity for a W-114 added-Port group is the earliest-created source Port.** Spencer approved resolving the 27 multi-source groups found by the accepted v0.2 plan so v0.3 can assign stable whole-model identities. For every W-114.3 added-Port group, sort its source Ports by EA `CreatedDate` oldest first, breaking an exact time tie by EA GUID in ascending lexical order. The first source is the canonical source. This follows the same chronological/tie-break principle used for translated note numbering (W-14) and avoids using EA `Object_ID` as engineering identity. The canonical source supplies the emitted Port note's creation time and author for `uid`, its primary `EA GUID` traceability line, and its position in whole-model Port `id` numbering. The entity name and owner still come from W-114. Its subtype still follows W-128 across the whole group: a real Port stereotype on any grouped source is preserved rather than lost because the canonical source happens to be plain. Every non-canonical source Port remains a separate ledger row pointing to the same emitted Port entity, and its real differing values remain evidence under the existing merged-Port preservation rules. This changes no grouping or connector resolution, only the stable identity of the one Port entity emitted for the group. The rule also applies trivially to the 590 one-source W-114.3 groups. With W-276, all 617 added-Port entities have deterministic canonical source identities and the W-273/W-274 identity blocker is closed. Applied: `99_System/09_Tools/EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.3.html` implements W-276 before whole-model uid/id assignment. It keeps the accepted v0.2 preflight/planning path and adds a deliberately bounded structural acceptance renderer for `02 Product Context`: whole-model deterministic id/uid/file-name assignment, folder creation, Markdown notes, ParentID/Part/Port structural links, planned connector relationships, W-275 paired/symmetric inverse persistence and validation, outbound slice links, `Ledger.csv` and `Run Manifest.md`, written into a user-selected fresh unpacked base-vault folder through the browser File System Access API. v0.3 does not yet render diagrams/canvases, `t_document` attachments, the four final review tables, or every retained EA source-evidence line; its manifest marks the output as structural acceptance only and not releasable.

**W-277 · 2026-09-30 · Endpoint rules: principle, format and family 1 (structure); new pair `includes`/`includedIn` for shared aggregation.** Spencer's answer "yes" to my proposal as revised by him: `hasPort` from any class to Port ("I'd like to use ports as a defining feature for other elements, including ports themselves"), and his question whether aggregation was missing ("I would think includes/includedIn would be the relationship there"). Principle: the endpoint rules say what a person or AI may create; the translator stays mechanical and writes links as drawn, an off-rule link being a Review finding (it already carries a REVIEW line), not an error. Format (W-272): on each entry in `relationships.yaml`, `from` (class of the note holding the field), `to`, `any`, `sameClass`, `excludePairs`; one-way entries become small maps and symmetric entries take `between` when their rules are written; a relationship with no rule is not restricted yet. Family 1: `hasPart` Object to Object; `hasChild` any to any except Object to Object (uses `hasPart`); `includes` any to any; `hasPort` any to Port; `hasFlow` Port to Item Flow; `subtypeOf` and `copyOf` same class only (same class for `copyOf` is my reading of W-124, approved). New pair: `includes`/`includedIn`, membership without ownership, written on the whole, placed after `hasChild` (so its property order follows W-126). W-159 is amended: EA shared aggregation writes `includes` instead of `hasChild`; composite aggregation is unchanged; an existing link still wins. The shared/composite split of the 373 `hasChild` links counted in W-159 is to be recounted by the planner. Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.25, family 1 rules, the new pair, guidance `endpoints` and `includes`); `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` (Aggregation shared rule); `Translator Definition.md` (section 6 table, current through W-277); `Post-Import Tasks.md` (task 7); `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md` (create only within the rules); new `Definitions/Properties/includes.md` (INFO-00065); the handoff; the Workbench Decision Log (WB-053). Not changed: the native importer v0.2/v0.3 code, which another session maintains; it needs the shared-aggregation change before a note-producing run.

**W-278 · 2026-09-30 · EA Use Case include stays `hasChild`; both `hasChild` and `includes` are allowed between Use Cases until the post-import review.** Spencer's answers: "let's leave both, this difference wasn't thought through when modeling previously", then, to my question which of two readings he meant: "aggregation is includes, use case can use both relationships until review after import". My recommendation had been to move Use Case include to `includes`. Decided: W-277 stands (EA shared aggregation writes `includes`); the translator still writes EA Use Case include as `hasChild` (W-171 unchanged); both relationships may be used between Use Cases (family 1 already allows both, W-277); after the import each include link is reviewed, `hasChild` where the base owns the included Use Case, `includes` where the included Use Case is shared. My reading: "use both" means both are allowed, not that the translator writes both. Applied: `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` (note on the include rule); `Post-Import Tasks.md` (Task 7, item 13); the Open list; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed, so `Translator Definition.md` is unchanged.

**W-279 · 2026-09-30 · Endpoint rules, family 2 (requirements).** Spencer's answer "appliesTo is requirement to any, otherwise good" to my proposal. Written on each entry in `relationships.yaml`: `satisfies` Function or Design to Requirement (W-163); `derivedFrom` Requirement to Requirement (W-164); `refines` Requirement to Requirement (W-165); `verifies` Verification to Requirement (W-229); `appliesTo` Requirement to any class (Spencer widened my proposal of Object only; the import still writes it only to Objects, W-160, W-167); `references` Requirement to Requirement or Document (Document is my reading of the W-167 guidance, approved); `drives` any class to Requirement (W-165 guidance). Off-rule links written by the import stay Review findings (W-277 principle), among them the 112 `satisfies` links from other classes, 92 of them from States (Post-Import Task 7, item 11). Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.26); the Open list; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-280 · 2026-09-30 · Endpoint rules, family 3 (behavior and allocation).** Spencer's answer "let's add document to hasDesign alongside object in 'to'; dependsOn is any to any; participants add document to the 'to' list", and, to my question on `hasDesign`, "A Document can have a Design, like an Object can: Object, Document → Design". Written in `relationships.yaml`: `performs` Object to Function (allocate, turned around); `hasDesign` Object or Document to Design (Document added by Spencer); `realizedBy` Use Case to Function or Design (Usage); `dependsOn` any to any (Spencer widened my proposed list of pairs); `precedes` any to any, same class only (my reading, approved; the end classes of ControlFlow and StateFlow were not checked in the data); `optionOf` Use Case to Use Case (extend); `participants` (one-way) Use Case to Object, Actor, Function, Port or Document (Document added by Spencer). Because `dependsOn` is any to any, the `pairs` key I proposed is not added to the format. As W-277 set out, the `oneWay` entries are now small maps (`{field: ...}`); only `participants` has a rule so far. No code in this repository reads `oneWay`. Off-rule links written by the import stay Review findings (W-277), among them the 32 Realisation links (Post-Import Task 7, item 10). Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.27); the Open list; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-281 · 2026-09-30 · Endpoint rules, family 4 (interfaces and flows); new pair `exposes`/`exposedBy`.** Spencer's answer "let's add 'exposes' port to port for future use" to my family 4 proposal, together with a question about triggers and guards (answered separately). My reading: the rest of family 4 and moving `source`/`target` to family 6 are approved, as in his earlier answers that named only the changes. Written in `relationships.yaml`: `interfaces` (symmetric) between Ports (W-153; the 109 block-level links stay findings, Post-Import Task 1); `equals` (symmetric, temporary) between Ports (W-153); `transmits`, `receives`, `exchanges` (one-way) Port to Item Flow (W-177); `hasClassifier` (temporary) Port or Object to Object or Item Flow (W-125, W-127, W-131); symmetric entries take `between` (both notes must be in the list). New pair `exposes`/`exposedBy`, Port to Port, written on the outer Port pointing at the inner Port, placed after `hasFlow` (W-126 order); this also settles the name of the directional pair that Post-Import Task 8 asked to log. Nothing in the import writes it. `source`/`target` get no rule: nothing in the import writes them since W-177 moved Item Flow direction to the Port, so they join the keep-or-remove question with the four unused one-way fields (family 6). Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.28, rules, new pair, guidance `exposes`); new `Definitions/Properties/exposes.md` (20260930174529866skellyspencer); `Post-Import Tasks.md` (Task 8); the Open list; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-282 · 2026-09-30 · New pair `triggeredBy`/`triggers`; the one-way field `trigger` is removed; guards stay as text.** Spencer asked whether behavior should have triggers/triggeredBy and guards/guardedBy for Functions, Designs and States, and answered "yes" to my recommendation. Decided: a new pair `triggeredBy`/`triggers` that says what starts a behavior; `triggeredBy` is the forward field, written on the triggered Function, Design or State (its start condition is part of its own definition) and pointing at a Function, Design, State or Item Flow (Signals map to Item Flow, W-131; Designs already act as states in 5 StateFlows, W-175); `triggers` is generated. It is element-level: when a State is reached by several transitions it does not say which trigger belongs to which, so the transition-level `Trigger`, `Guard` and `Effect` lines of W-175 stay. The unused one-way field `trigger` (no class or writer since W-237, open since W-263) is removed in favor of the pair. Guards are held as text: a guard is usually a condition, not a note; a `guardedBy` link is reconsidered when there is a concrete case whose condition is a model element (in effect, enabled only while in a State). Nothing in the import writes the new pair. Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.29, the pair after `precedes`, `trigger` removed, guidance `triggeredBy`); new `Definitions/Properties/triggeredBy.md` (INFO-00067); `Definitions/Properties/trigger.md` deleted; the Open list; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-283 · 2026-09-30 · Endpoint rules, family 5 (description and issues); `drives` widened to any to any; format key `importOnly`.** Spencer's answer to my family 5 proposal: "conflicts with should be any to any; also, back to 'drives' - I think we should leave that any to any to cover 'causes'; otherwise good", with two questions to me (whether a Use Case should `affect`, and my view on Document as a source of `describes`), which are answered separately and not part of this entry. Written in `relationships.yaml`: `describes` Info or Artifact to any (W-161, W-166); `affects` Issue or Failure Mode to any (W-166, W-240); `supersedes` any to any, same class only (W-191); `conflictsWith` (symmetric) between any classes (Spencer widened my proposal of same class only); `tracesTo` any to any, `importOnly` (the translator still writes the links it maps to `tracesTo`; a person, AI or Workbench does not create new ones). W-279 is amended: `drives` is any to any, and its guidance now reads gives rise to or causes. New format key `importOnly` (W-277 format). Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.30, rules, guidance `drives` and `endpoints`); `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md` (do not create `importOnly` relationships); the Open list; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-284 · 2026-09-30 · A Use Case may `affect`; a Document may `describe`; `affects` means impact, good or bad.** Spencer asked whether a use case is an issue once it goes wrong and whether `affects` should allow Use Case as a source ("standard use can still have an impact"), and asked my view on Document as a source of `describes`. My answer: a use case stays the intended use; how it goes wrong is a Failure Mode and what was found is an Issue; normal use does have impact, so a Use Case may `affect`. Spencer answered "yes" to adding Use Case to `affects`, with its meaning set to impact good or bad and kept distinct from `drives`, and Document to `describes`. Decided: `affects` from Issue, Failure Mode or Use Case to any class; meaning: the source has an impact on a target that exists anyway, good or bad; `drives` (any to any, W-283): the target exists or happens because of the source; to design against an impact, the preferred path stays Use Case drives Requirement, applied to the element. `describes` from Info, Artifact or Document to any class. Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.31, the two rules, guidance `affects`); the meanings in `Definitions/Properties/affects.md`, `drives.md` (left out of W-283, corrected here) and `describes.md`; the handoff. No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-285 · 2026-09-30 · Endpoint rules, family 6: `source`, `target` and `sequence` are removed; `initialState` and `finalState` are kept, State Machine to State.** Spencer's answer "yes" to my proposal. Removed: the pair `source`/`target` (nothing writes it since W-177 moved Item Flow direction to the Port) and the one-way `sequence` (Sequence connectors write messages on the diagram note, W-176); no template lists them. Kept: the one-way `initialState` and `finalState`, written on a State Machine and pointing at a State; `finalState` may hold several values. Post-Import Task 7 gains item 14: the EA initial and final nodes, now `modelCheck` notes linked by `precedes` (W-175), are replaced by these fields and the node notes retired. Every relationship in `relationships.yaml` now has an endpoint rule (W-272 complete). Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.32; fields removed; rules; guidance `states`; guidance `source_target` removed; guidance `flow` corrected to W-177); `Definitions/Properties/source.md`, `target.md`, `sequence.md` deleted; meanings written in `initialState.md` and `finalState.md`; `hasFlow.md` corrected to W-177; `Post-Import Tasks.md` (Task 7 item 14); the Open list; the handoff; the Workbench Decision Log (WB-053). No stage 1 rule changed.

**W-286 · 2026-09-30 · The definition-note key `appliesTo` is renamed `usedOn`.** Spencer's answer "ok" to my proposal, after he noted that `appliesTo` is used for requirements; my reply made clear the relationship `appliesTo` (Requirement to any, W-279) is unchanged and only the key on definition notes is renamed. Reason: every note in `Definitions/Properties` carried a frontmatter key `appliesTo` saying which notes the property is used on; it has the name of the requirement relationship, so a Base, query, Workbench index or AI search for `appliesTo` would also pick up the definition notes and their text values. Decided: the key is `usedOn`; on plain property notes it keeps its text; on relationship notes it reads `see relationships.yaml`, so the endpoint rules (W-272 to W-285) stay in one place. Applied: the 36 relationship notes and 7 plain property notes in `Definitions/Properties`; the `Property Definition` template; the `Property Dictionary` base column; the Open list; the handoff.


**W-287 · 2026-09-30 · Native importer v0.4 and the base-vault snapshot are synchronized to relationship schema 1.32.** Spencer asked that his relationship updates be included in the next import tool and that a new vault be created if the edits made the old one stale. The old W-270 base ZIP is stale because it includes `Definitions`, the Property Definition template and `99_System/03_Schemas/relationships.yaml`, all changed by W-277 to W-286. Applied: `99_System/09_Tools/EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.4.html` is a new version; v0.3 is left unchanged. v0.4 embeds the complete schemaVersion 1.32 relationship vocabulary and property order, including `includes/includedIn`, `exposes/exposedBy`, `triggeredBy/triggers`, the endpoint rules and `importOnly`; it removes the retired `source/sourceOf`, `target/targetOf`, `sequence` and `trigger` fields. It implements the one Stage-1 mapping change from those decisions: shared EA Aggregation (DestIsAggregate 1) writes `includes/includedIn` instead of `hasChild/childOf` (W-277); EA Use Case include remains `hasChild` (W-278). Endpoint-rule violations are Review findings, not import failures, matching W-277; v0.4 reports them in the structural slice manifest and deduplicates symmetric findings. The writer still persists paired inverses and symmetric mirrors under the W-275 trial and validates them before writing. v0.4 refuses to generate into a base vault whose `relationships.yaml` is not schemaVersion 1.32, preventing a stale tool/base combination. Static checks pass: the 63 stored relationship fields in v0.4 match `relationships.yaml` exactly and in the same order, no retired field remains, and the JavaScript parses. A replacement clean base-vault snapshot is maintained on branch `base-vault-2026-09-30-rel132-v04`; it follows the W-250/W-270 contents, ships `.vault.yaml` as `UNINITIALIZED`, includes the current Definitions, W-286 `usedOn` property-definition change, relationship schema 1.32 and v0.4, and excludes EA evidence, translator governance, import outputs, archive and MDSE Workbench. The first real acceptance run remains `02 Product Context`; v0.4 is still structural-acceptance scope, not a release importer.

**W-288 · 2026-09-30 · `tracesTo` is the provisional relationship: anyone may create it, and every link is a Review finding until replaced.** Raised in the Workbench review: Workbench decisions WB-054 to WB-056 need a provisional relationship for "these two are related, the relationship is not chosen yet", and the schema had none. I offered A (add a temporary pair `newRelationship`) or B (drop the idea). Spencer's answer: "A, but we can also just use the existing trace relationship as the 'newrelationship'". My reading: the capability is kept, carried by `tracesTo` rather than a new field. Decided: `tracesTo` loses `importOnly` (W-283) and gets `provisional: true`; the translator still writes it for EA connectors that fit no rule (284 links), a person, AI or Workbench may write it when unsure, and every link, imported or new, is a Review finding until replaced by a stronger relationship. The format key `importOnly` is replaced by `provisional` in the `endpoints` guidance. Applied: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.33, the `tracesTo` entry, guidance `endpoints` and `tracesTo`); `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md`; `Definitions/Properties/tracesTo.md`; the handoff; the Workbench notes (WB-054, Part C, M4, the acceptance scenario, Review category renamed Provisional Relationships). No stage 1 rule changed.


**W-289 · 2026-09-30 · Import filenames are human-oriented; v0.5 adds a fresh-vault whole-model assessment mode.** The first populated `02 Product Context` slice showed that Port notes mixed with their owning Objects are difficult to browse and that the W-198/W-199 duplicate-ID suffix is visually noisy. Spencer decided: (1) every Port filename is `i<owner> - <port name>`; the note heading may use the same generated name; (2) when two Ports still have the same generated name, use `_<connecting object>` when exactly one clear connected Object distinguishes that Port, then deterministic `_1`, `_2`, ... for any collision that remains; if there is not exactly one clear connected Object, go directly to the numeric suffix; (3) when a Requirement collides with another natural filename, use `<name>_r`, then `_r_1`, `_r_2`, ... if needed; (4) every other unresolved duplicate uses deterministic `_1`, `_2`, ... rather than exposing the MDSE id in the filename. The oldest EA source in a remaining collision keeps the unsuffixed candidate and later sources take the numeric suffix. IDs remain unchanged in YAML and remain the stable model identity. This supersedes the filename-suffix portion of W-198/W-199; their whole-vault, case-insensitive collision detection principle remains. Applied in `EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.5.html`. v0.5 also incorporates relationship schemaVersion 1.33/W-288, reports every provisional `tracesTo` as a Review finding, and adds an explicit **Whole model** output scope. A whole-model run still numbers/identifies from the whole model and writes every planned element note; it remains structural-assessment scope, with diagrams/canvases, attachments, the four final review tables, conveyed InformationFlow direction fields and some retained EA evidence still incomplete. The importer must not run over an existing generated model: before writing, it rejects an output root that already contains any model root folder it intends to create. Therefore the populated `20260930` vault is evidence/reference only and is not the target of the whole-model run; use a fresh disposable base-vault copy.


**W-290 · 2026-09-30 · v0.5.1 resets stale browser state and rejects the translator workspace as an import target.** The first v0.5 whole-model attempt exposed two implementation faults, not model faults. Spencer's log showed a fresh preflight PASS but no new whole-model-plan PASS before Generate, so v0.5 had allowed the prior plan/output directory handle to survive when Analyze was rerun on the already-selected QEAX. The selected local path was also the `Test_Vault_` translator workspace; because it has `.vault.yaml` and relationship schema 1.33, v0.5's base check accepted it even though its README explicitly says it is not the vault to be built. Decided/applied in `EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.5.1.html`: starting a new preflight invalidates the prior plan, planner context, output handle and result; changing the source does the same; starting Build Plan clears any earlier output handle so output must be selected after the PASS plan; the base-vault validator requires an MDSE Base Vault README and rejects a target containing the Workspace Decision Log; filesystem read/write/check failures identify the path that failed. The failed run did not change the GitHub copies of `Test_Vault_` or `spencerskelly/20260930`. The next whole-model attempt uses a fresh disposable v0.5.1 base copy, kept closed in Obsidian until generation finishes.

**W-291 · 2026-10-01 · `hasState`/`stateOf` is restored (endpoints amended by W-292).** Spencer's decision: "we should have a relationship hasState/stateOf between states and state machines to objects. this was my mistake. please add this back to our rules, instructions, and tools." This reverses the `hasState`/`stateOf` part of W-185; `hasStateMachine`/`stateMachineOf` and `hasTransition`/`transitionOf` stay removed. My reading of the endpoints: `hasState` is written on an Object or a State Machine and points at a State (`from: [Object, State Machine]`, `to: [State]`), and `stateOf` is generated on the State. Applied as consequences: `relationships.yaml` (schemaVersion 1.34) has 27 paired pairs, 1 temporary pair, 3 symmetric and 6 one-way fields, 65 stored fields in all, with a guidance entry; `.obsidian/types.json` gains `hasState` and `stateOf` (90 properties); `Definitions/Properties/hasState.md` is new (INFO-00068, `uid` with Spencer's author code because he directed it); the `State Machine` template lists `hasState` instead of `hasChild`, which reverses the template change made in W-185 and amends W-235; the importer is `EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.5.2.html`, a copy of v0.5.1 that carries the pair, its order and its endpoint rule and requires schema 1.34 (v0.5.1 stays as the earlier baseline); the handoff and the Translator Definition are updated. Not changed: no import rule writes `hasState` (States placed under an owner stay `hasChild`/`childOf`, W-149), `hasChild` still excludes only Object to Object, the earlier importers still require 1.33, and the populated `20260930` keeps its copy of the schema at 1.33. Because v0.5.2 refuses a base vault whose `relationships.yaml` is not 1.34, the clean base vault needs the new file before the next run. MDSE_Workbench test fixtures and README follow the schema. Not run: v0.5.2 has not been run against the `.qeax`; I checked its script syntax and that its relationship tables agree with each other.

**W-292 · 2026-10-01 · An Object `hasState` a State or State Machine, never `hasChild`.** Spencer's correction to W-291: "object should hasState a state or state machine, it should not be a hasChild relationship." This answers the placement question left open in W-291. Rule: `hasState` is written on an Object and points at a State or a State Machine; `stateOf` is generated on the State or State Machine. My reading, which also stands from W-291 ("between states and state machines to objects"): a State Machine `hasState` a State, so `hasState` is `from: [Object, State Machine]`, `to: [State, State Machine]`, with a State Machine not having a State Machine (`excludePairs: [[State Machine, State Machine]]`). `hasChild` now excludes Object to State, Object to State Machine and State Machine to State, in addition to Object to Object, so no tool writes the generic link for those pairs. Applied as consequences: `relationships.yaml` is schemaVersion 1.35 (the same 27 paired pairs and 65 stored fields); the `hasState` definition note and guidance entry follow; importer `EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.5.2.html` is amended in place (it had not been run) and now decides ownership through one function (Object to Object `hasPart`, Object to State or State Machine and State Machine to State `hasState`, everything else `hasChild`), used for ParentID placement, Part folding, the independent Nesting connectors and composite Aggregation, and it requires schema 1.35; `ea-connector-mapping.yaml` and the Translator Definition say so; the handoff is updated. In the EA export the rule reaches few notes: of 1,112 State elements 12 sit under a StateMachine and 2 under a Class, and 1 of the 2 StateMachine elements sits under a Class (a Class may become an Object or a Port, so the Object count is not known before the plan runs). Not changed: States under another State or under a Function stay `hasChild`; the populated `20260930` and the importers up to v0.5.1 keep schema 1.33; existing notes are not rewritten. Checked: the importer's script parses, and for every owner and child pair the field it chooses is allowed by its own endpoint rules. Not run: v0.5.2 against the `.qeax`. Applied later the same day, on Spencer's "accept it": the clean base vault repository `spencerskelly/Test_Vault_-base-vault-2026-09-30-rel133-v051` (commit `f2d7f16`) now carries schema 1.35, `types.json`, `Definitions/Properties/hasState.md`, the State Machine template and importer v0.5.2, and its README says so; the repository name still says rel133-v051 because a repository cannot be renamed by the token I have, and the name is no longer true. A disposable copy of it is ready for the next import run.


**W-293 · 2026-10-01 · Reusable Object uses are represented as local part occurrences when contextual identity matters.** Spencer approved the occurrence architecture after testing the reuse case conceptually ("awesome, i think this is going to work"). A reusable Object/assembly is defined once. When it is used inside another Object/system and that specific use must be distinguished, the containing context owns a local part occurrence with a stable local ID and a `definition` reference to the reusable Object. Invariant structure and interfaces remain on the reusable definition; integration-specific connections belong to the containing product/system/configuration. This prevents reuse collapse when the same Assembly/J4 connects to different partners in Product 1 and Product 2, and distinguishes two copies of the same Assembly in one product. A local part occurrence does not require a standalone Markdown note solely because it is contextual usage. The durable conceptual identity is owner note UID + part local ID. Exact local-ID and body syntax remain open.

**W-294 · 2026-10-01 · Local endpoints, connections and flows are contextual addressable records; the connection owns its local flows.** Spencer approved the combined interface/flow occurrence direction before moving implementation directly into the importer. A local endpoint has a stable local ID separate from its visible identifier, uses `definition` for the reusable interface/Port/connector concept, preserves source identifier/kind/multiplicity/provenance and may inherit nested addressable interface members from its reusable definition. A local connection has its own stable local ID and is owned by the lowest meaningful common Object/system/configuration context in which the participating endpoint occurrences are brought together; neither endpoint arbitrarily owns it and no standalone Interface note is required solely for a local binding. Local flows are allocated to that specific connection, may be multiple per connection, use `definition` for the reusable Item Flow concept, and use endpoint roles `transmit`, `receive`, `exchange` or `unspecified`; local flow identity is connection-scoped. Requirements keep the existing `appliesTo` semantics and may target an addressable local occurrence. Authoritative local occurrence detail is intended to live as structured, human-readable Markdown body records while frontmatter remains note-level. Exact body schema, anchor syntax, local-ID token format, local-override rules, note-promotion criteria and Workbench storage/indexing contract remain open.

**W-295 · 2026-10-01 · The occurrence model is validated through the real native importer; v0.6/v0.6.1 are experimental implementations.** Spencer chose to go directly to the importer rather than build a separate toy vault first ("I'd like to go right to the importer. I really want to see this work in the real model"). Experimental importer v0.6 was built on `spencerskelly/20260930` branch `handoff/full-import-2026-10-01` from the older v0.5 / relationship-1.33 line. It adds local part/endpoint/connection/flow body records, conveyed InformationFlow-item extraction from `t_xref`, connection-owned flows, local Requirement applicability evidence, source Multiplicity on local records, repeated-Part-link deduplication, the interface-Class-to-Port subtype fix, engineering/source headings and local-ID diagnostics. Its isolated critical-case test produced 2 part occurrences, 2 endpoint occurrences, 1 connection, 1 conveyed CAN flow, 1 local Requirement applicability record, 0 unresolved conveyed xrefs and 0 warnings. v0.6.1 adds only output-folder handle hardening for macOS/browser `InvalidStateError` and `NotFoundError`. These builds prove the occurrence logic direction but are not accepted release/importer baselines.

**W-296 · 2026-10-01 · Cross-repository audit sets the merge-forward baseline: v0.5.2/schema 1.35 is authoritative; occurrence work must be merged onto it.** A review of every GitHub repository currently accessible found that `Test_Vault_` main is newer than the baseline used for experimental v0.6. The authoritative translator workspace has relationship schema 1.35 and importer v0.5.2, including W-291/W-292 `hasState/stateOf` ownership and stronger base-vault identity/state-reset protections. Experimental v0.6/v0.6.1 require schema 1.33 and therefore regress W-292 state placement; they also lack v0.5.2's full README/workspace rejection check. Decision/continuation rule: the next importer must start from v0.5.2/1.35 and merge the W-293/W-294 occurrence implementation forward, not continue directly from v0.6.1. The current clean base-vault repo is `spencerskelly/Test_Vault_-base-vault-2026-09-30-rel133-v051` main; despite its stale name, its README and content are schema 1.35 / v0.5.2. `20260930` and `261001` are partial/reference artifacts, not accepted outputs; neither has complete Run Manifest/Ledger reconciliation, and sampled `261001` notes have no `## Local Model` records. `MDSE_Workbench` 0.1.14 already carries schema-1.35 fixtures but does not yet index local body occurrences. Older `EA_Import`, `EA_Model_Import` and `PosiBattery` are historical for this translator; `EA_Model_Evidence` remains source evidence; `Ampure_Data` is the company/common methodology vault, not the live native-translator implementation workspace. The detailed audit and fault history are in `EA Native Importer Comprehensive Handoff - 2026-10-01.md`.


**W-297 · 2026-10-01 · The approved full-import usability/governance amendments from the 20260930 review are adopted into the authoritative workspace.** This records decisions already approved during the full-scale import review so they no longer live only on the experimental `20260930` handoff branch. Folder structure is navigation, not semantics; do not split by count alone and do not create arbitrary overflow folders. Aim for roughly 5–6 meaningful levels in ordinary model content, with deeper formal/source structure allowed, and collapse a one-child intermediate folder only when it has no independent value. Do not generate README/Base/Canvas scaffolding in every folder; automatically generate the standard set only for primary MDSE top-level domain folders, with lower-level artifacts exception-based. Package notes are exception-based rather than automatic. Naming is human-oriented: soft targets are about 40 characters for folder names and 80 for filenames; filename and visible engineering title are separate; Port files use `i<shortest unambiguous owner> - <Port>`; Requirement `_r` is collision-specific; use EA Name by default and Alias only when Name is clearly machine/noise; preserve original Name as provenance; reserved infrastructure prefixes are `Template -`, `Rule -`, `README_`, `BASE_`, `CANVAS_`; collision checking covers imported, system and user-authored content and should prefer meaningful context over blind numeric suffixes. Apply approved readable shortening before path evaluation; path preflight happens before writing and any path still over the final hard repository-relative limit blocks the run. The exact hard limit remains open. Do not use `unnamed/` as a fallback for a known root package. Import/bootstrap housekeeping initializes/validates `.vault.yaml`, removes tracked OS junk such as `.DS_Store`, enforces reserved infrastructure naming and records actions. Run completion is reconciliation-based: every planned entity must end written, intentionally transformed/suppressed, or failed, with zero unexplained remainder; a partial writer is `INCOMPLETE / FAIL`. Keep the Run Manifest concise and put detailed path/collision/transformation/model-check evidence in separate audit outputs. Repeating the same semantic relationship target does not express quantity; preserve unresolved occurrence/quantity evidence separately. Applied as consequences: Ruleset 1.22 sections 9 and 15 are now synchronized in `Test_Vault_`; Translator Definition and the comprehensive handoff are amended to point at these rules. Open: exact hard path limit and final contained-occurrence quantity syntax.



**W-298 · 2026-10-01 · Local Model content is a distinct governed note region and receives a separate Workbench interface surface.** W-293/W-294 local part occurrences, endpoint occurrences, local connections, connection-scoped flows and local relationship targets remain structured, addressable, human-readable Markdown owned by the containing note. They are model content, but they are not ordinary narrative text and they are not note-level frontmatter relationships. MDSE Workbench must expose them as a separate **Local Model** surface alongside Properties and Relationships. In normal/view mode the Local Model is a structured read-only view with navigation to reusable definitions and addressed local records. When the user enters Edit, ordinary note-text editing must exclude/protect the governed Local Model region; Local Model changes are made only through structured Local Model controls after the canonical body schema is frozen and validated. Until that contract is frozen, Workbench may index and display Local Model records but must keep them read-only. This closes the Workbench/interface-boundary portion of the W-294 open item. The exact managed-region marker syntax, canonical record fields, anchor syntax, local-ID token format, override rules and promotion criteria remain open.

**W-299 · 2026-10-01 · Native importer and clean base-vault releases are a matched pair and share one release version.** From the next newly issued importer/base pair onward, the native importer release and the clean base vault intended for that importer use the same semantic release number. The importer file/version and the base vault's machine-readable release declaration must match before planning or writing; a mismatch is a blocking compatibility error. The base vault will carry this value in `.vault.yaml` as `mdse_release`. Relationship-schema and element-schema versions remain independently versioned because they describe different compatibility surfaces and are not forced to equal the importer/base release number. Existing historical artifacts are not retroactively renamed: v0.5.2 remains the accepted fallback importer, v0.7 remains a merge candidate, and the stale-named clean base remains historical/current fallback evidence. The first newly issued synchronized pair after the v0.7 candidate will be **v0.8.0**: importer `EA_to_MDSE_Native_Importer_v0.8.0.html` plus base vault `mdse_release: "0.8.0"`. Run Manifest/diagnostics must record importer release, base release, relationship schema and element schema separately.

**W-300 · 2026-10-01 · v0.8.0 is a complete model-and-attachment import; diagrams are deliberately deferred to a selectable post-import pass.** The first keepable v0.8.0 import must carry the complete accepted Stage-1 semantic model plus all approved linked-document/attachment content. It does **not** write EA diagrams/Canvas files during the initial import. Every source diagram is still reconciled and represented in run evidence as intentionally deferred, so diagram omission is explicit scope rather than an unexplained remainder. After the model import is accepted, the same importer/toolchain provides an additive **diagram import pass** that can be run one or more times against the existing vault. The user can select which diagram categories/types to bring in; only the selected diagrams are created, existing model notes remain authoritative, and diagram import must not duplicate or alter semantic model content. Diagram companion notes/Canvas behavior continues to follow the approved diagram rules when a diagram is actually imported.

**W-301 · 2026-10-01 · Deferred diagram import is selected by native EA diagram type.** The additive post-import diagram pass introduced by W-300 uses the preserved EA diagram types as the primary selection categories. The selectable categories are: Custom, Logical, Use Case, CompositeStructure, Statechart, Activity, Sequence, and Package. The user may select one or more categories for a pass. Selection by higher-level engineering/domain context is not required for v0.8.0. Re-running the diagram pass must be idempotent for diagrams already imported and must not alter accepted semantic model content.

**W-302 · 2026-10-01 · v0.8.0 Local Model uses explicit managed-region markers.** The canonical Local Model body region is bounded by HTML comment markers immediately under the `## Local Model` heading:

```markdown
## Local Model

<!-- MDSE:LOCAL-MODEL START schema=0.1 -->

...structured Local Model records...

<!-- MDSE:LOCAL-MODEL END -->
```

The markers are tooling/editing boundaries only; they do not carry engineering semantics. The authoritative model content remains the structured human-readable Markdown records inside the region. Workbench, importer and other MDSE tooling use these markers to parse the governed Local Model safely, exclude it from ordinary text editing, and detect the local-body schema version. Content outside the markers is ordinary note body content and is not part of the Local Model. Missing, duplicated, nested or mismatched Local Model boundary markers are model-health errors and block structured Local Model editing. A note may contain at most one managed Local Model region in v0.8.0.

**W-303 · 2026-10-01 · Local Model records use type-prefixed stable local IDs independent of visible engineering names.** v0.8.0 local records use stable owner-scoped IDs with a type prefix and opaque token, for example `part-a7c31f`, `ep-42bd90`, `conn-917e2a`, and `flow-6cc318`. Imported records derive the token deterministically from immutable EA source identity/provenance so re-importing the same source occurrence yields the same local ID even if ordering or visible names change. Visible identifiers such as J4 or P2 and local engineering names remain separate display/edit fields and are never the durable identity. Future Workbench-created local records use the same prefixes with generated stable tokens. The token is opaque to users and must not encode sequence position or mutable engineering meaning. Durable addresses remain owner UID plus local ID, with endpoint/member and connection/flow scoping as already approved.

**W-304 · 2026-10-01 · v0.8.0 freezes the canonical Local Model record pattern and keeps EA provenance out of engineering notes.** Local Model records use Markdown headings plus named fields. The stable local ID is in the record heading, optionally followed by the human-facing name. Canonical record kinds remain part occurrence, endpoint occurrence, connection and connection-scoped flow; do not add a separate kind merely for a nested/sub-interface. A nested interface/member is another endpoint record using the same endpoint schema plus a `parent` address to its containing endpoint. This recursive endpoint pattern supports connectors, pins, contacts, terminal positions, mating features and other sub-interfaces at any required depth. Part occurrences keep `definition` links to reusable Object/assembly notes so the same definition can be reused in new assemblies/products without duplicating invariant structure. Endpoint occurrences keep `definition` links to reusable Port/interface definitions and may be local to a part occurrence or nested under another endpoint. Connections reference the participating local endpoint addresses. Flows remain authored once, under the specific connection that carries them, with a reusable `definition` plus endpoint roles (`transmit`, `receive`, `exchange`, `unspecified`). Workbench/indexing must expose those connection-owned flows from each participating interface/sub-interface so an engineer can see what flows on that interface without duplicating authoritative flow records. EA GUIDs, EA object/connector identifiers and other EA-only provenance are **not written into Local Model note records**. Instead the importer writes `99_System/11_Import/Local Model Source Map.csv`, keyed by owner note UID + local ID, containing the source EA identity/provenance needed to return to EA. This source map is import evidence, not engineering semantics, and Workbench does not require it for normal model operation. The managed region therefore remains compact and reusable after EA is retired.

Canonical shape:

```markdown
### Part Occurrences

#### part-a7c31f — Main Control PCBA
- definition: [[Main Control PCBA]]
- name: Main Controller
- multiplicity: 1

### Local Interfaces

#### ep-42bd90 — J4
- part: part-a7c31f
- definition: [[8-Pin Circular Connector]]
- name: J4

#### ep-51c8ad — Pin 2
- parent: ep-42bd90
- definition: [[Connector Pin]]
- name: Pin 2

### Connections

#### conn-917e2a
- endpointA: ep-51c8ad
- endpointB: ep-d08371

##### flow-6cc318 — CAN_H
- definition: [[CAN_H]]
- endpointA: transmit
- endpointB: receive
```

Optional fields are written only when meaningful; do not emit empty placeholders. Exact anchor/link rendering may be implemented by Workbench/importer so long as the durable semantic address remains owner UID + governed local ID/path.

**W-305 · 2026-10-01 · Reusable interface members are inherited implicitly and materialized locally only when context requires independent identity.** When a reusable Port/interface definition contains nested members such as pins, contacts, terminal positions, mating features or other sub-interfaces, a local endpoint occurrence inherits those members through its `definition`. The importer/Workbench does **not** create a local `ep-*` record for every inherited member by default. A nested member is materialized as a local endpoint record only when the containing assembly/product context needs to address that member independently—for example because it is connected, locally overridden/renamed, targeted by a Requirement, given context-specific multiplicity/attributes, or otherwise referenced by local model content. A materialized nested endpoint uses the same endpoint schema and a `parent` address to the containing endpoint. Materializing a nested endpoint does not duplicate or redefine the reusable member; its `definition` continues to point to the reusable member definition. Workbench may display implicit inherited members when browsing an interface, clearly distinguishing inherited definition content from materialized local records.

**W-306 · 2026-10-01 · MDSE uses an Obsidian-native-first representation policy, and Local Model records use native block IDs as their persisted navigation anchors.** When more than one representation can preserve the required engineering meaning, prefer the representation that is native to core Obsidian and remains readable/useful without MDSE Workbench. Next prefer conventional Markdown/YAML structures that are compatible with broadly used Obsidian plugins. Custom Workbench-only syntax or storage is used only when native/common mechanisms cannot preserve the needed semantics, identity, traceability or editing safety. Workbench should add semantic understanding, validation, indexing and better views on top of native vault content rather than make the underlying model accessible only through the plugin. This principle applies to links, properties, headings, block IDs, Canvas, Bases and future Obsidian features so improvements in the Obsidian ecosystem can benefit MDSE without migration.

For Local Model records, W-304 is amended so the human-facing heading no longer needs to contain the opaque local ID. Each materialized local record uses a native Obsidian block ID equal to its stable W-303 local ID: `^part-*`, `^ep-*`, `^conn-*`, or `^flow-*`. The heading may therefore remain an engineering-readable name such as `#### J4` or `##### CAN_H`. Standard Obsidian links such as `[[Control Assembly#^ep-42bd90|J4]]` navigate directly to the local record without Workbench. Workbench additionally interprets the block ID prefix and owning note UID as the durable semantic local address. Core Obsidian still treats the containing note as the graph node; that is accepted. Do not introduce a parallel custom anchor/link syntax for v0.8.0.

**W-307 · 2026-10-01 · v0.8.0 must eliminate the 88 known over-260-character paths in the Industrial connector structure before a global hard path limit is chosen.** The 20260930 assessment contains 89 model-note repository-relative paths longer than 260 characters; 88 are under the `Connector ASM - Industrial` hierarchy. Treat those 88 as a structural/model-placement defect rather than evidence that all model paths require aggressive global truncation. v0.8.0 planning must remediate them by applying the approved semantic-placement, reuse and Local Model occurrence rules: reusable definitions remain first-class notes in sensible reusable locations; contextual copies/parts/endpoints that do not deserve independent first-class notes are represented through Local Model records; folder hierarchy is not used to restate semantic composition; machine/noise names such as encoded URLs use the approved meaningful Alias/name rule where available. Do not solve these 88 by blind filename clipping or opaque hash renaming. Acceptance target: after v0.8.0 planning, there are **zero** >260-character model-note paths remaining in the Industrial connector structure. After that corrected planner result is measured, choose the vault-wide hard repository-relative path limit from the residual real offenders.

**W-308 · 2026-10-01 · Connector-folder remediation removes repeated parent wording and redundant model numbers before any deeper structural change.** W-307's 88 Industrial-connector path offenders are first shortened using the existing human-oriented folder rule: a child folder does not repeat wording already supplied by its parent context. In this branch, repeated phrases such as `Connector ASM`, `Industrial`, and `Anderson` are removed from lower-level folder names when the parent already makes that meaning unambiguous. For this connector hierarchy, a model number is also omitted from a folder name when that model number is already carried by the engineering note directly beneath/within that folder and the shorter folder remains unambiguous. Folder names remain navigation labels, not semantic identifiers; note titles and model relationships retain the engineering identity. Apply these readable reductions before considering Local Model promotion or any generic truncation. Do not remove wording when doing so would make sibling folders ambiguous.

Example intent:
- `40 Connector ASM - Industrial/Connector ASM Industrial - Variants/Connector ASM - Anderson/Connector ASM - Anderson A/Connector ASM - Anderson A320/...`
- becomes approximately `40 Connector ASM - Industrial/Variants/Anderson/A/...`, with the A320 identity retained by the note rather than repeated in the enclosing folder when that folder would otherwise exist only to repeat the model number.

After this normalization, remeasure the connector paths. Only remaining offenders require semantic-placement/Local Model conversion or further meaningful shortening under W-307.

**W-309 · 2026-10-01 · Regulatory folders that duplicate the directly contained element title collapse to the regulatory section number.** When a regulatory folder exists primarily to contain an element directly beneath it with the same section/title wording, keep the full regulatory engineering title on the element note and shorten the folder to the section designator/number only. The folder remains a navigation aid rather than a second copy of the element name. Examples: a folder `7.4.2 Electrical Properties` containing the element `7.4.2 Electrical Properties` becomes folder `7.4.2`; a folder `9.7.4.3 Verification of ...` containing the same-titled element becomes folder `9.7.4.3`. Apply only when the section designator is clear and unique within the parent context; do not shorten a folder if the number alone would create sibling ambiguity or lose necessary navigation context. The note title, regulatory hierarchy semantics and relationships remain unchanged.

**W-310 · 2026-10-01 · Local part multiplicity groups only genuinely indistinguishable contextual copies; independently addressable copies are separate occurrences.** A Local Model part occurrence may use `multiplicity: N` only when the N copies are intentionally interchangeable for the modeled context and no copy needs its own connection, Requirement scope, state, attribute/override, flow allocation, maintenance identity or other local relationship. If copies differ in any modeled contextual fact, create separate `part-*` occurrences, each with its own stable block ID/address. Repeating the same note-level semantic relationship target never expresses quantity. If a grouped occurrence later needs one or more copies to become independently addressable, split/materialize those copies as distinct local occurrences and update affected local references; do not retain a misleading grouped multiplicity. This rule applies equally to imported EA multiplicity evidence and future Workbench-authored structure.

**W-311 · 2026-10-01 · Assemblies own the connections below their boundary; boundary exposure is distinct from connection.** Each first-class assembly/Object owns the local connections formed among occurrences below that assembly level. Invariant internal wiring remains with the reusable assembly definition. A parent assembly does not reach through a child assembly and connect directly to the child's internal endpoint. When an internal endpoint must be available at the next level, the child provides a boundary endpoint and that boundary endpoint may `exposes` the internal endpoint; `exposedBy` is the inverse meaning. The parent connects to the child boundary endpoint. EA BindingConnector/temporary `equals` is source evidence for this pattern, but Stage 1 does not automatically reinterpret it as exposure merely because an inner/outer containment direction can be derived. Preserve the contextual endpoints and temporary `equals` for review; review resolves it to `exposes` or another appropriate meaning.

**W-312 · 2026-10-01 · Persisted Local Model references use native Obsidian links rather than bare local-ID strings.** W-306's native-first policy applies inside Local Model records. A record's stable local ID is also its native Obsidian block ID. References such as endpoint-to-part, nested endpoint-to-parent, connection endpoint A/B, exposure and temporary local `equals` use ordinary Obsidian block links such as `[[#^ep-42bd90|J4]]` or `[[Owner Note#^ep-42bd90|J4]]`. `definition` remains a normal note wikilink. Human-readable headings are display data. Bare local IDs may be used internally by tooling but are not the canonical persisted relationship/reference representation when a native link is possible.

**W-313 · 2026-10-01 · Local Model schema 0.1 is machine-readable vault configuration shared by importer and Workbench.** `99_System/03_Schemas/local-model.yaml` defines the governed Local Model region, record kinds, native block-ID/reference rules, allowed fields, exposure/temporary-equals semantics, assembly connection ownership, connection-scoped flow roles, multiplicity constraints, provenance separation and validation checks. This file is a tooling/format contract, not a new first-class engineering metamodel. Importer and Workbench must read/follow the same contract rather than each embedding a private body syntax. The exact opaque token-length/collision-extension algorithm remains separately open.

The historical counter readings of W-134/W-138/W-145 may still be checked as source evidence, but they do not control the new collision filename syntax.

What still uses the old property and relationship rules is the translator, which is not written, and existing notes, which the next import replaces.

- Review lines (W-156, W-157, W-216): a spot-check of the multi-level containment rule is still open (the four review tables are set, W-217 to W-220); a spot-check of the multi-level containment rule that derives 60 of the 64 `equals` directions; whether the ledger keeps the ends as drawn after stage 2.

- `Freestyle.md` (found in W-184 review): Spencer's definition is a quick note that holds only a `uid` until it is promoted to an element; the template holds only a title heading and no `uid` line. Whether to add `uid` frontmatter to it is not decided.

**W-314 · 2026-10-01 · Local Model contextual usage, specialization families and configuration resolution.** A reusable definition says what an engineering concept is; a Local Model occurrence says how that definition is used in one owning context. The occurrence field is named `usage` and has three semantic values: `standard`, `variant`, and `option`. Omitted `usage` means `standard`.

- `standard`: the occurrence is required and uses the stated reusable `definition`.
- `variant`: the occurrence is required and resolves to exactly one allowable **concrete** definition from the specialization family rooted at the stated `definition`.
- `option`: the occurrence may be absent; when present it resolves to the stated concrete definition or an allowable concrete specialization of it.
- Candidate specializations are derived from the existing `subtypeOf` hierarchy rather than duplicated as lists on the occurrence. Candidate discovery is transitive, not direct-child-only.
- A non-abstract root definition is itself a valid candidate alongside its non-abstract descendants. A reusable definition may be marked `abstract: true`; an abstract definition organizes/generalizes concrete definitions but cannot be the effective definition of a contextual occurrence. Abstractness is not inherited.
- `subtypeOf` remains definition-level kind-of semantics. `usage: variant` and `usage: option` are contextual configuration semantics and do not create new note-level relationships.
- Optionality is never intrinsic to the reusable definition. The same reusable definition may be optional in one assembly and standard in another.
- Do not connect an assembly to every subtype simply to express a configurable position. The single local occurrence is the configurable position.
- The same usage pattern is semantically applicable to contextual occurrences of parts, endpoints/ports, Functions, Use Cases and States when those occurrence kinds exist. Do not invent new Local Model record kinds solely to make them configurable.
- Do not extend `usage` automatically to connections, flows, transitions or topology. Contextual topology is filtered/derived from the selected or absent occurrences unless a real engineering case later proves a separate topology-variation mechanism is needed.
- A base reusable architecture remains unresolved and must not be mutated merely to explore a configuration. Workbench may hold temporary selections in session state. Persisted named configurations, when implemented, store their selections separately from the base Local Model and identify local positions by their stable native block-link addresses.
- A saved configuration must distinguish **unresolved** from **absent**: no selection entry means unresolved; `present: false` is meaningful only for an option occurrence. A completed configuration resolves every variant and explicitly includes/excludes every option.
- Product/model-number coding belongs to product/configuration knowledge, not to reusable definitions; a reusable component may have different commercial codes in different product families.
- Cross-variant compatibility syntax and occurrence-level `allowedDefinitions` are deferred until a real use case demonstrates the need. The default candidate set is the full concrete specialization family.

The approved theory, examples and implementation impact are recorded in `MDSE v0.8 Design Check - 2026-10-01.md`. W-319 subsequently advances the governed implementation contracts to `local-model.yaml` 0.2 and `element-types.yaml` 1.17. Workbench 0.1.15 contains only the immediate safety corrections; WB-106 remains the parser/index/view implementation gate.


**W-315 · 2026-10-02 · One global 30-character identity-token namespace spans notes and Local Model reference points.** A first-class note stores the 30-character token in `uid`; a Local Model record embeds the same token in its kind-prefixed native block ID (`part-`, `ep-`, `conn-`, `flow-`). The prefix describes the current local representation and is not a separate uniqueness namespace. A token may not be reused by any note or other local record in the destination vault. If a local entity is later explicitly promoted to a note, it retains the token. Promotion is a modeling decision, not an importer inference.

**W-316 · 2026-10-02 · EA8647 first-import identity allocation and source lineage are settled.** The current source lineage identifier is `EA8647`; source identity is `EA8647 + EA GUID` and stays in importer provenance/evidence, not normal engineering metadata. First allocation uses EA Created exactly as written, with no timezone conversion; absent milliseconds become `000`. The existing normalized 13-character EA author code is used; missing/unusable author uses `skellyspencer` for this import. If Created is unavailable, allocation begins at `20260911000000001`. Collisions increment by 1 ms and same-seed records are ordered by EA GUID lexical order. Derived local records inherit the causing source record's seed; several locals from one source order by source GUID, local kind, owning-context GUID and definition GUID. Allocation checks every existing note UID and Local Model token.

**W-317 · 2026-10-02 · Source Map is authoritative for rerun identity and importer field ownership is explicit.** Once a Local Model identity is assigned, `Local Model Source Map.csv` is the rerun authority; timestamp/author are not recalculated. Identity conflicts are hard errors and are never auto-repaired. EA-owned translated fields and source-provenanced relationships may refresh. MDSE identity, existing filename/path, MDSE-only relationships and human-added content are preserved. Human edits overwritten in EA-owned fields are logged. An EA-owned relationship removed from EA is removed and logged. An entity whose EA source disappears is preserved and flagged; if the source returns, its existing identity is reused.

**W-318 · 2026-10-02 · v0.8 naming, folder normalization and path limit are final.** The old ID-based duplicate filename rule is superseded. Duplicate files/folders use `~2`, `~3`, etc. Forced filesystem/path alteration uses `~a`, `~b`, ... with lowercase Excel-column sequencing; an altered duplicate combines them, e.g. `~a~2`. Alphabetic sequencing is scoped to the sanitized base within the target folder; deterministic ordering uses the authoritative source key. Numeric markers go to duplicate-name review; alphabetic markers go to altered-name review. Apply semantic folder shortening before filename shortening: remove repeated ancestor/structural wording; a folder whose purpose is to contain an identically named authoritative note may use the shortest unambiguous designator/model/section identifier; omit redundant model-number folders; collapse one-child folders only when they have no note, independent value or sibling structure; omit empty EA packages. Generated repository-relative paths are limited to 212 characters; unresolved paths over 212 are blocking.

**W-319 · 2026-10-02 · Local Model 0.2 and element schema 1.17 implement W-314; attachment/diagram/evidence disposition is settled.** Local Model 0.2 is the canonical writer format; Workbench readers support 0.1 and 0.2. Part and endpoint records may carry `usage: standard | variant | option`, with omission meaning standard; connections and flows do not. Element schema 1.17 adds sparse optional `abstract: true`; absence means false and abstractness is not inherited. Relationships stays 1.35. BindingConnector context that cannot be reconstructed deterministically remains evidence/review only; the importer does not invent local topology. Failed approved attachment writes are non-blocking but explicitly reconciled as failed attachment imports. Initial diagram creation remains deferred, but every source diagram must reconcile; an unreconciled diagram is blocking. The four empty legacy CSVs (Identity Registry, Model Checks, Pending Relationships, Transformation Log) are retired unless a future unique use is approved. v0.8 is a clean import into a newly produced 0.8.0 base and does not migrate v0.7 output in place.

**W-320 · 2026-10-02 · Documentation authority registry, release manifest, status banners and archive conventions.** `99_System/10_Docs/00 - Current State.md` is the single entry point and registry: it states the release target (0.8.0 pre-release; relationships 1.35, element-types 1.17, Local Model 0.2), where each rule lives, tool status, and which files are current, reference, proposal, historical, superseded or archived. `99_System/03_Schemas/mdse-release.yaml` is its machine-readable twin, and `99_System/09_Tools/check-release.py` verifies schema versions, document registration and banners, Translator Definition currency against this log, and (with `--workbench`) Workbench fixture and version sync; it must pass before commit. Status vocabulary: current, reference, proposal, historical, superseded, retired. A historical, superseded or retired file carries a callout banner naming what governs now and is listed with `supersededBy`; its content is not edited to match new rules. Applied now: banners on Ruleset 1.22 (already), the 2026-10-01 Comprehensive Handoff (already), the 2026-10-01 Design Check, Handoff - Continue Here (body historical) and Git and New Vault Setup; the old translator workspace README in the archive; the four retired empty CSVs (W-319) moved to `99_System/archive/11_Import retired/`; archive README and 11_Import README added; `README_09_Tools.md` lists the status of every importer file. Stale statements in the Translator Definition were corrected (W-303 short local tokens superseded by W-315; Local Model schema 0.2; the W-314 "semantic decision only" wording; the four CSVs settled; "Current through W-320"). `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md` and the Handoff Prompt now start from Current State. Workbench test fixtures had drifted from the authority schemas and were resynchronized (Workbench `WB-108`). The review's critical points and platform roadmap are recorded as a proposal in `MDSE Platform Roadmap and Critical Points - 2026-10-02.md`; no rule changed.


**W-321 · 2026-10-02 · One release contract builds a lean runtime base and mechanically aligns importer, base and Workbench.** `99_System/03_Schemas/mdse-release.yaml` is the single machine-readable release/build authority in the methodology workspace. It now defines the positive runtime-base include list; `build-base.py` consumes that list and `check-release.py --base` verifies the generated base against it. The engineering/base vault intentionally does **not** copy `00 - Current State.md` or `mdse-release.yaml`; those govern the development workspace, not each deployed model. The generated base carries `mdse_release` only in `.vault.yaml` plus the actual runtime schemas themselves, avoiding duplicate version registries. `Initialize-Vault.sh/.ps1` must preserve `mdse_release` while replacing the uninitialized vault identity. The old prose base-content list in Translator Definition is replaced by a pointer to the manifest. Workspace `AGENTS.md` and `AI_INSTRUCTIONS.md` are conditional: read Current State when present; in a lean runtime vault, start from Ruleset 1.23 and the runtime schemas. MDSE Bootstrap is deferred from the v0.8 runtime plugin baseline until a retrievable source/release exists; its spec remains methodology evidence. The final issued base must pin a WB-106-capable Workbench release. Workbench keeps schema fixtures only where independent automated tests require them: current relationships/element-types/Local Model 0.2 must be exact authority copies, while a frozen Local Model 0.1 fixture exists solely for backward-compatibility tests. Importer/base release matching remains `mdse_release = 0.8.0`; importer and Workbench additionally validate the runtime schema files they actually consume. No second methodology/release registry is created in the engineering vault.

**W-322 · 2026-10-02 · Controlled plugin release: plugins ship inside the base; MDSE Bootstrap rebuilt as release check and author registration.** Spencer's direction: the base is a controlled release so everyone has the same experience; it opens fully functional once Restricted mode is turned off, with Templater, Breadcrumbs, Fileclass, Git, Workbench and every other runtime plugin included and configured. This supersedes the W-321 deferral of Bootstrap and the earlier rule that plugin binaries are per-machine installs. (1) Every runtime plugin's `main.js`, `manifest.json` and `styles.css` is vendored in `99_System/09_Tools/runtime-plugins/<id>/` (third-party plugins from their official GitHub release at the pinned version; Workbench 0.1.15 and Bootstrap from their builds) and copied by `build-base.py` into the base's `.obsidian/plugins/`. (2) `.obsidian/plugin-lock.yaml` becomes schema 2: per plugin the version, source, settings policy and SHA-256 of each file, plus `obsidianMinVersion` (1.13.0), required core plugins and core plugins that must be off; `update-plugin-lock.py` generates it and `community-plugins.json` (11 plugins). (3) Governed settings are generated by `build-plugin-config.py` from `relationships.yaml` and `element-types.yaml` and tracked in Git: Templater (templates folder), Fileclass (classes in new `99_System/06_Fileclasses/`, bound by `type`, subtype and status choices, relationship fields limited to allowed target types through `MDSE Link Targets.base`, inverses and the temporary `hasClassifier`/`equals` not offered, no automatic field insertion), Breadcrumbs (all relationship fields as edge fields, grouped, inverse implied) and Obsidian Git (pull on start, pull before push). Other plugins run on defaults. Status choices are the values in use (Draft, Active, Retired); the full status list remains open.
