(4) Core Templates is turned off because it inserts Templater syntax unprocessed; Templater is the template path. (5) The base gets its own generated `.gitignore` (governed `data.json` tracked; author code, Bootstrap and Workbench state, defaults-only plugin settings and `Workbench Views/` ignored); `.gitattributes` marks plugin files `-text` so hashes survive Windows checkouts. (6) MDSE Bootstrap 0.3.0 is a new implementation in its own workspace folder `MDSE Bootstrap/` (source `plugin/`, README, Author Registration Spec moved there from `01_Admin`, Base First-Open Test Sheet). It checks the vault against the lock on every start (status bar, release-check table) and performs author registration per the spec; it never installs or changes plugins. 6 unit tests pass and the build is reproducible. No separate repository for now. (7) `mdse-release.yaml` gains `runtimePlugins` and the Bootstrap/Workbench entries (`wb106Version` stays null); `check-release.py` verifies the generated settings and lock are current, payload hashes, Bootstrap and Workbench version agreement, and that a built base carries exactly the payload. An issued base still requires the WB-106-capable Workbench. Not yet verified in Obsidian: see the Base First-Open Test Sheet.

**W-323 · 2026-10-02 · Linked-document payload decoding and the decode-only attachment check (importer v0.8.5).** (1) EA stores linked-document `BinContent` as ZIP. The importer unwraps it (up to two nested levels) before RTF/image extraction, reading sizes, CRC and flags from the ZIP central directory, and verifies CRC32 and size per entry. Encrypted, ZIP64, unsupported-method and truncated entries fail explicitly. With several file entries, the one entry that decodes as RTF or a known image is used; if none or more than one qualifies, the document fails as `multi_entry_ambiguous`. (2) Every `t_document` row ends in exactly one outcome: `OK`, `NO_PAYLOAD`, `ZIP_ERROR`, `UNSUPPORTED_TYPE`, `IMAGE_ERROR`, `PATH_ERROR`, `OWNER_ERROR` or `SKIPPED`; a failure never stops the loop. (3) A decode-only mode needs only the `.qeax` and a PASS plan. It runs the same identity, path-planning (`planOutputPaths`, extracted unchanged from the write path) and `attachmentReconciliation` code as the whole-model write, writes no vault, and downloads a per-document CSV with container, ZIP entry, method, decoded bytes, payload type, embedded image count, files produced, outcome, reason and SHA-256. (4) Attachment acceptance is a mechanical PASS/FAIL against `attachment_benchmark.json` in the importer version folder: 376 approved documents (247 ModelDocument, 129 ExtDoc), 376 decoded OK, 390 attachment files and zero residual documents. Nothing is built in: no benchmark loaded is FAIL. A `NO_PAYLOAD` document passes only if its `doc_id` is listed in `expectedEmptyDocIds`, which requires a recorded decision. The same summary line goes to the log and the Run Manifest of every full run. (5) The benchmark holds totals from the governed source analysis; it has no per-document expectation. `check-release.py` verifies the benchmark file and that decode-only shares the write path's planning and reconciliation code. Importer v0.8.5 is verified on synthetic fixtures only; acceptance still requires a decode-only run on the real EA8647 `.qeax` and then a full fresh-base run.

**W-324 · 2026-10-02 · No length-driven shortening; hard stop 400; links go to the file name; "Shorten paths" becomes a post-import task (importer v0.8.6).** Spencer's direction: "let's remove destructive rules from the importer regarding name length"; add "shorten paths" to the post-import list; he works on a Mac until the base is ready for the team; "no more cutting names if there isn't a logic rule that means we don't really need it"; "all linking should be done to the filename or using the uid"; "remove the length rule in the importer, 400 characters is fine". Applied: (1) Removed every rule that cut a name or folder to fit a length: the filename cut to the path limit, the v0.8.3 `folder_N` folder compaction (W-318 implementation) and the truncation inside duplicate markers. Kept as logic rules: the semantic folder shortening of W-318 (repeated ancestor wording, connector wording, folder = note), `~a` for forced character alteration, `~2` for duplicates, and the 75-file `folder_N` split, which is about folder size and cuts no name. (2) The path rule is a hard stop at 400 characters that blocks the run and never cuts. In the EA8647 CSV extract the longest planned path is 379 characters before folder normalization, so it should not trigger. This replaces the 212-character limit of W-318 and Ruleset 1.23 section 15.4; the rest of W-318 stands. (3) The one forced cut is the filesystem component limit: a file name over 255 bytes cannot exist on macOS or Windows, so it is cut at a word boundary, by bytes, and keeps `~a`, with a row in the altered-name review. The extract has 2 such note names. A folder name over 255 bytes blocks the run (none in the extract). (4) New review output `Review - Long Paths.csv`: every note and attachment path over 212 characters, with `uid`, `id`, EA GUID, length, path and link target. It changes nothing. It is the input to the new Post-Import Task 9, "Shorten paths", which sets the team's path limit once the vault's location on Windows is known. (5) Links. Finding: importer v0.8.0 to v0.8.5 wrote every link as the full vault path, not the file name, although the base sets `newLinkFormat: shortest`. Now a link is `[[File name]]` when the name is unique across the planned notes and the base vault's existing notes (case-insensitive); where it is not, the shortest trailing path that is unique is used, which is what Obsidian's shortest-path format writes. Pure file-name links are not possible everywhere: in the CSV extract about 17% of notes (27% counting Ports before their `i<owner> -` prefix) share a name with another note elsewhere, and Obsidian would resolve such a link to one of them without warning. Identity stays in `uid` and `id`; no link carries it. Evidence CSVs keep full paths so each line stays resolvable (W-156). The Run Manifest reports both link counts. (6) The 9 attachment failures of the v0.8.5 decode-only run (paths of 221 to 262 characters) are under 400, so the benchmark of W-323 should now pass; this is expected, not yet run. (7) Open: whether Workbench WB-106 and the other plugins resolve links with Obsidian's resolver and not by comparing path text is not checked; the readers are in the `MDSE_Workbench` repo.

**W-325 · 2026-10-03 · Release chain aligned before the next importer run: importer v0.8.6, Workbench 0.1.16, Bootstrap 0.3.0, base rebuilt.** Spencer's direction: bring importer, base vault, Workbench and Bootstrap up to date before the next run so each run is worth it. (1) Workbench 0.1.16 (`spencerskelly/MDSE_Workbench`, WB-111, WB-112) is vendored in `runtime-plugins/mdse-workbench/`, pinned and hashed in `plugin-lock.yaml`, and set in `mdse-release.yaml`. It reads Local Model 0.1/0.2, reports findings with **Check Local Model**, and writes links Obsidian-style. This closes open point 7 of W-324: the Workbench indexer already resolved links through Obsidian, so file-name, path and block-fragment links read correctly; the writer was the fault (it always wrote the bare file name, wrong where names repeat) and is fixed. (2) WB-106 is not complete: the occurrence-aware views, the Local Model popup and Review integration remain, so `wb106Version` stays null and no whole-model import is kept yet. (3) Bootstrap 0.3.0 needs no change: its 6 tests pass and a fresh build is byte-identical to the vendored payload; it reads the lock and does not depend on path length or link format. (4) The base builds with importer v0.8.6 and passes `check-release.py --base`; the base's `app.json` already sets `newLinkFormat: shortest` and `alwaysUpdateLinks: true`, which W-324 relies on. (5) What the next run should bring back: the Run Manifest, `Attachment Reconciliation.csv`, `Review - Long Paths.csv`, and the Workbench `Local Model Findings.md`, which is the first independent read of the importer's Local Model output.

**W-326 · 2026-10-03 · Documentation clean-up, archive moves and the plan to a golden model.** Spencer's direction: make sure everything is documented and all references are up to date; move files that are completely outdated and not used into the archive; write a detailed plan covering post-import and everything needed before a model is "golden", including improvements to the importer, base vault, Workbench and Bootstrap. Applied: (1) New current document [[MDSE Plan - Path to a Golden Model]]: proposed golden definition, gates G0 to G5, the checklist before each run, what each run brings back, the freeze point (rule-level fixes by re-import before a run is kept, note-level fixes after), the golden checklist, team rollout, improvements by component (I1 to I9, B1 to B6, W1 to W9, S1 to S4, X1 to X4) with priorities, decisions needed (D1 to D8), risks and a run log. It replaces the 2026-10-02 roadmap proposal and code-gap audit. (2) Archived with banners, nothing current depending on them (links still resolve by file name, W-324): importers v0.1 to v0.7 and v0.8.0 to v0.8.5 (`archive/09_Tools retired importers/`, with a README of their roles; v0.5.2 was the accepted fallback but cannot produce a 0.8.0 model); `Handoff - Continue Here`, `EA Native Importer Comprehensive Handoff - 2026-10-01`, `MDSE v0.8 Design Check - 2026-10-01`, `MDSE Modeling Ruleset 1.22`, `Git and New Vault Setup`, `MDSE v0.8 Toolchain Review - 2026-10-02`, `MDSE Platform Roadmap and Critical Points - 2026-10-02` (`archive/10_Docs retired/`); the Ampure Cross-Vault Resolver record (`archive/01_Admin Archived Plugins/`); `vault-registry.yaml`, a cross-vault registry nothing reads (`archive/03_Schemas retired/`). `mdse-release.yaml` drops `acceptedFallback` and `evidenceOnly` for one `history` pointer and registers the moved files as retired. (3) Kept: `99_System/CSV_EA/` (rule evidence), the two `99_System/11_Import` snapshots ([[Post-Import Tasks]] Tasks 1 and 2 use them), every property definition (each matches a field or property in the schemas) and every template (each matches a class or a system note type). The 24 empty `00 - Views and Bases`, `00 - Folder Contents` and `00 - Folder Map` stubs in `99_System` stay, because Ruleset 1.23 section 9 asks for them; whether that rule stays is decision D7 in the plan. (4) References brought up to date: Current State (read order, tool table, file registry, change-protocol step for the plan), README, tools README, Translator Definition (archived paths; check 2 now says no two notes share a path, matching W-318 folder-scoped duplicates and W-324 links), Handoff Prompt (current importer path, plan, and a copy-ready prompt for a Workbench chat), Workbench README, Post-Import Tasks (pointer to the freeze point). [[Post-Import Tasks]] becomes a current document. (5) Plan items are proposals until logged; D1 asks Spencer to confirm the golden definition.


**W-327 · 2026-10-03 · State and State Machine do not satisfy Requirements; Requirement applicability carries state scope.** The authoritative satisfaction relationship remains `Function satisfies Requirement` and `Design satisfies Requirement` only. A Requirement that is scoped to a State or State Machine uses `Requirement appliesTo State` or `Requirement appliesTo State Machine`. Workbench views, tests, documentation, importer behavior and future tools must not infer or display State/State Machine satisfaction. Historical archived references that recorded the superseded State-satisfaction rule remain unchanged as evidence, but they have no authority and are superseded by this decision, Ruleset 1.23 and `relationships.yaml` 1.35.

**W-328 · 2026-10-03 · Tool authority is explicit; Test_Vault_ governs every MDSE tool except the standalone Workbench implementation repository.** `spencerskelly/Test_Vault_` is the authority workspace for the importer, Bootstrap, base/release builder, runtime-plugin payload/configuration, schemas, tool boundaries and deferred cross-vault infrastructure. `spencerskelly/MDSE_Workbench` is the authoritative implementation repository for Workbench source, tests and release artifacts; Workbench product/interface decisions remain mirrored/registered in the `MDSE Workbench/` workspace in Test_Vault_ so they can be compared with the other tool definitions. Cross-vault resolution remains a future capability and its archived resolver is retained as reference, not part of the current v0.8 runtime. The current comparison/start point for tool ownership and boundaries is `99_System/10_Docs/MDSE Tool Definitions and Boundaries.md`.


**W-329 · 2026-10-03 · Test_Vault_ is organized as an intentional tool-development workspace, with each long-lived tool owning its top-level area or repository.** Workbench remains in its standalone `spencerskelly/MDSE_Workbench` repository and that repository owns its implementation and product-definition material. Test_Vault_ owns the other tool domains in top-level folders: `Importer/`, `Bootstrap/`, `Base Vault/`, plus `00_Workspace/` for cross-tool current state/roadmap/governance and `Cross-Vault/` for deferred cross-vault work. `99_System/` is reserved for shared MDSE semantic/runtime core such as schemas, templates, author/AI support and core modeling rules; tool source, test material, revision history and release-building machinery do not belong in generic System/Tools folders. Each tool top-level folder separates Definition, Tools/revisions, Testing and History where applicable. A tool revision is represented by an explicit revision folder (for example `Importer/Tools/v0.8.6` or `Bootstrap/Tools/v0.3.1`) rather than by keeping alternative implementations on long-lived branches. `main` is the normal working truth. Branches are used only when isolation is materially necessary (for example a risky parallel change or PR review), not as the normal way to present versions/options. Historical revisions move to the owning tool's History area and remain non-authoritative.

**W-330 · 2026-10-03 · Bootstrap uses safe activation repair (option B), while packaged files and governed settings remain release-controlled.** After the user performs Obsidian's unavoidable **Trust author and enable plugins** action, Bootstrap may automatically repair activation state: enable every community plugin listed in `.obsidian/plugin-lock.yaml`, enable each required core plugin, and disable each explicitly prohibited core plugin (currently core Templates). Bootstrap does not download, replace or update plugin binaries and does not silently rewrite governed `data.json` settings. Missing, modified or wrong-version plugin files and changed governed configuration remain release drift and are reported for restoration from the controlled vault/Git. Extra community plugins are reported but are not automatically removed or disabled. Bootstrap 0.3.0 remains the currently pinned release; the first implementation of W-330 is developed/tested as candidate 0.3.1 under `Bootstrap/Tools/v0.3.1` before promotion into the Base Vault runtime payload.


**W-331 · 2026-10-03 · Governed plugin configuration is part of the controlled runtime hash contract.** A plugin marked `settings: governed` in `.obsidian/plugin-lock.yaml` must lock the SHA-256 of its governed `data.json` in addition to its runtime plugin files. Bootstrap therefore detects configuration drift as release drift; the Base Vault release checker verifies the payload against the same hashes. Default/per-machine plugin settings remain outside this rule. The first implementation is in `Base Vault/Tools/v0.8.0-r2/update-plugin-lock.py`; the existing four governed plugins are Templater, Fileclass, Breadcrumbs and Obsidian Git.

**W-332 · 2026-10-03 · The EA importer is external to the operational engineering vault.** A fresh Base Vault is the target into which the importer writes the candidate/golden model, but the importer executable itself is not copied into the generated runtime base and is not shipped to ordinary engineering users. Importer source, versions, benchmarks and test evidence remain under `Importer/` in Test_Vault_. This separates model-construction tooling from the long-lived operational vault while preserving the same fresh-base import workflow.


**W-333 · 2026-10-03 · Bootstrap safe activation repair may only load an intact locked community plugin.** Before automatically enabling a disabled community plugin, Bootstrap verifies the plugin version and every file hashed by `plugin-lock.yaml`, including governed `data.json` under W-331. If any locked file is missing or differs, Bootstrap leaves that plugin disabled and reports release drift; it never loads the mismatched plugin as part of repair. Core-plugin activation repair remains governed by the required/disabled core lists because those plugins are part of Obsidian itself. An explicitly prohibited core plugin being on is a release **error**, not merely a warning. Extra community plugins remain warnings and are not automatically disabled or uninstalled.


**W-334 · 2026-10-03 · Distinguish the clean Base Vault build artifact from an operational model repository.** `build-base.py` produces a deterministic **clean build artifact**: `.vault.yaml` is intentionally `UNINITIALIZED` and the output is not a Git repository. `check-release.py --base` validates that artifact and must not require Git or an initialized vault identity. Before importer/runtime first-open testing, the release owner turns a copy of that artifact into a **candidate model repository** by initializing the vault identity, placing it under Git (and a remote when sync behavior is being tested), and then running the importer against it. The full Bootstrap First-Open Test Sheet applies to this initialized repository stage, not to the raw build artifact. The accepted golden repository retains its initialized vault identity and is what engineers clone; ordinary users never initialize the vault themselves.


**W-335 · 2026-10-03 · Release validation distinguishes candidate tooling from pinned runtime and scopes freshness checks to the owning authority.** During `releaseStatus: pre-release`, `check-release.py` may validate a Bootstrap candidate when the plugin lock version matches `tools.bootstrap.candidateSource`; it reports that state as a warning rather than pretending the candidate is promoted. An issued release must still match the pinned runtime source/version. The checker no longer requires an importer definition to claim the latest global W-number when later decisions belong to other tools; importer freshness is enforced by importer-specific static/semantic contract checks. Repository-root README files are descriptive navigation and are not required to duplicate the exact release/schema tuple held by `00 - Current State.md`. Exact copied runtime files are compared byte-for-byte so non-UTF-8 content cannot crash validation.


**W-336 · 2026-10-03 · Base initialization scripts are release-validated for host-shell syntax.** The macOS/Linux `Initialize-Vault.sh` must parse successfully under `sh -n` as part of `check-release.py` before a Base Vault is considered valid. Embedded helper code must avoid shell-sensitive quote patterns even inside here-documents. This gate was added after the first 0.3.1 candidate Base exposed a quoting defect before changing `.vault.yaml`; initialization remains fail-safe and refuses partial identity creation.


**W-337 · 2026-10-03 · Pause creation of the next integration vault until Workbench and importer are aligned.** Base/Bootstrap candidate work has reached a stable source/build-validation boundary: Bootstrap 0.3.1 builds and passes 9 tests, the candidate Base builds, and release validation reports 0 failures with only expected pre-release warnings. Do not spend time creating another disposable/integration repository solely for Bootstrap first-open testing. Finish Workbench WB-106 first, then align importer v0.8.6; use one fresh integrated candidate vault to test Bootstrap first-open persistence, importer execution and Workbench behavior together. Bootstrap 0.3.0 remains the official pin until that integrated test passes.

**W-338 · 2026-10-03 · OS metadata is never governed Base Vault content.** Base build tooling must ignore platform metadata such as `.DS_Store` and `Thumbs.db` when copying governed trees. These files are local filesystem artifacts, not MDSE definitions/evidence, and their presence must not affect deterministic release contents.


**W-339 · 2026-10-03 · WB-106 expands from the completed read/navigation gate into the structured Workbench editor before the next integration vault.** Spencer reviewed the WB-106 occurrence UI/UX and then made the high-impact decision that Workbench is not merely a viewer: it should edit both Local Model context and reusable MDSE definitions through clearly separate edit surfaces. This does **not** change relationships 1.35, element-types 1.17, Local Model 0.2, the global UID/token contract, or importer semantics. It changes the Workbench release gate and golden-model sequence. The standalone 0.1.17 candidate remains valid evidence that the original occurrence-aware Structure/Interfaces/Where Used/Requirements/Canvas/local-details/Review gate works, but it is no longer sufficient to set `wb106Version`. The expanded gate follows standalone Workbench WB-114 and later decisions: UI/Canvas never write Markdown directly; semantic operations go through a pure model-edit/transaction service; atomic edits may apply immediately; structural edits stage Review/Apply/Cancel with continuous validation and blocked Apply on required errors; Git is durable history while Workbench supplies semantic history and one undo/redo stack; occurrence/context data stays separate from reusable-definition data; definition edits write the canonical note; independently referenceable Local Model entities use the existing governed global 30-character identity token inside their kind-prefixed block IDs; no second Workbench identity system is created. Per W-337, still do not create the next integration vault until this expanded Workbench gate and importer v0.8.6 are aligned enough for a meaningful combined test. The Translator Definition marker does not advance because W-339 changes Workbench/toolchain behavior, not stage-1 importer semantics.


**W-340 · 2026-10-03 · Internal Structure is a Workbench presentation of one Local Model context; curated Canvas geometry is never model semantics.** For an Object/note that owns Local Model records, Workbench may provide an **Internal** view whose outer Canvas group represents the selected note/context, with local part occurrences inside, compact boundary endpoint occurrences on the outer edge, part-owned endpoints near their part, context-owned connections between endpoints, connection-owned flows shown with their connection, and `exposes` between outer and inner endpoint occurrences. This introduces no new MDSE element, relationship or Local Model property. The authoritative semantic facts remain the note and its Local Model 0.2 records. Canvas position, size, spacing and routing are presentation only. An engineer may manually curate those values for readability. Refresh/regeneration must use stable ModelRef/local identity to preserve surviving manual placement where practical; it must not silently convert drawn Canvas geometry into model relationships or discard curated layout. Deeper structure is reached by changing context to the reusable definition/occurrence's next Internal view rather than flattening arbitrary depth into one diagram. This is a Workbench/view decision and does not advance the Translator Definition stage-1 W-marker.

**W-341 · 2026-10-03 · A controlled Workbench user guide ships in every generated engineering Base Vault and is cross-repository checked.** Ordinary engineers need operating instructions in the vault they actually use, not only in the standalone development repository. The canonical development copy is `spencerskelly/MDSE_Workbench/docs/User Guide/MDSE Workbench User Guide.md`; Test_Vault_ carries the release-managed copy at `Base Vault/Definition/MDSE Workbench User Guide.md`, mapped by `mdse-release.yaml` into `99_System/01_Admin/MDSE Workbench User Guide.md` in generated engineering vaults. The guide must distinguish pinned/runtime behavior from candidate source behavior. `check-release.py --workbench <clone>` compares the two source copies exactly so they cannot silently drift. This changes release/documentation packaging only, not MDSE model semantics or importer behavior.


**W-342 · 2026-10-03 · Release validation distinguishes the standalone Workbench candidate from the pinned Base Vault runtime.** The Base Vault remains pinned to Workbench 0.1.16 while standalone `spencerskelly/MDSE_Workbench` main/package remains 0.1.17 during the expanded WB-106 development line. This is an intentional pre-release candidate state, not repository drift. `mdse-release.yaml` therefore records `candidateVersion: "0.1.17"`. During `releaseStatus: pre-release`, `check-release.py --workbench <clone>` accepts package/manifest equality to that candidate version as a warning while still reporting that the Base pin is older. An issued release must still match the promoted pinned version and `wb106Version`. This mirrors the candidate-vs-pinned treatment already established for Bootstrap and prevents the release checker from falsely requiring development main to masquerade as the currently vendored runtime.


**W-343 · 2026-10-03 · MDSE runtime stability is governed by a persistent, disposable semantic-cache architecture rather than repeated whole-vault startup scans.** The Markdown/YAML vault and governed Local Model records remain the only engineering authority. Bootstrap remains a small environment-integrity/author-registration component; Workbench owns one shared semantic model service used by occurrence-aware views, validation and editing. Workbench startup is staged: register commands/status/listeners immediately; restore a compatible local derived cache when available; become usable before exhaustive verification; reconcile only changed/new/deleted files in bounded yielding batches; run expensive global checks asynchronously, at idle, or explicitly. Cached indexes/findings/views are disposable, versioned against their parser/schema inputs, excluded from Git, safe to delete and rebuild, and never rewrite model content merely because they were restored. One malformed note or cache failure must degrade locally rather than disable the vault. Generated Canvas views remain demand-driven projections and preserve curated geometry by stable ModelRef/local identity. Bootstrap may later reuse prior SHA verification only when the lock and locked file evidence prove nothing changed; unconditional full verification remains available and W-322/W-331 integrity is not weakened. The implementation plan is tracked in `spencerskelly/MDSE_Workbench/docs/Architecture/MDSE Runtime Architecture.md` as RTA-1 through RTA-7. RTA-1 begins immediately with explicit Workbench runtime status/observability; persistent warm-cache restoration is not enabled until cache-format, invalidation and corruption tests exist.


**W-344 · 2026-10-03 · Workbench semantic-cache safety uses bounded A/B commit slots, vault/schema binding and conservative reconciliation.** The disposable Workbench cache is bound to one initialized `vault_uid`, an explicit cache-format version, an explicit semantic-parser contract version, relationship/element schema versions and a deterministic signature of the parsed schema semantics. Cache persistence is bounded to two fixed A/B slots. Each target slot writes generation-tagged shards first and its small manifest last; the opposite slot remains a complete fallback if the target write is interrupted or corrupted, and mixed-generation shards are rejected. Warm reconciliation may be incremental only when the Markdown path set is unchanged and the changed-file count stays within the governed incremental threshold. Any added/deleted/renamed Markdown path forces the proven full chunked rebuild until Workbench caches sufficient authored-link evidence to safely re-resolve unchanged wikilinks, because a path-set change can alter link resolution without modifying the linking note. Cache failure never changes model files or blocks ordinary Markdown access. Warm restore remains disabled until save-only persistence, cache inspection, test/typecheck/build, and representative runtime checks pass.


**W-345 · 2026-10-03 · Semantic-cache v2 retains authored relationship-link evidence so path add/delete/rename events can reconcile safely without rereading unchanged model notes.** W-344's conservative full-rebuild fallback for any Markdown path-set change is superseded where the v2 cache is available. Each cached note now retains the governed relationship field, original wikilink text and Obsidian linkpath in addition to the resolved target. After an added/deleted/renamed note, Workbench reparses only changed/new notes, removes deleted notes, then re-resolves cached authored relationship links for unchanged notes through Obsidian's current metadata cache. This updates forward/inverse relationship targets, broken references, duplicate-source evidence and local block targets without rereading unchanged Markdown bodies. Local Model definition references already retain authored link text and resolve against current context on demand. Cache semantic version advances to 2 so older cache state cannot be mistaken for re-resolvable evidence. The bounded incremental threshold still applies; a large change set or concurrent instability falls back to the proven chunked full rebuild. This improves RTA-3 without changing Markdown authority or model semantics.


**W-346 · 2026-10-03 · Expensive Workbench assurance is revision-scoped and demand-driven, not recomputed on every UI refresh or metadata event.** Workbench maintains a monotonic in-session semantic revision that advances when indexed model semantics change. Review/global assurance results may be reused while that revision is unchanged; a manual Refresh may force recomputation, and any semantic revision change invalidates the prior assurance snapshot. This keeps global checks separate from ordinary startup/readiness and prevents repeated whole-index/Local Model scans caused by harmless UI redraws or metadata-cache chatter. The revision also coalesces disposable semantic-cache persistence: unchanged semantic state is not rewritten, writes occur after quiet time with a minimum interval, and a change during a cache write schedules a later generation rather than blocking model work. Git and Markdown remain authoritative; revision/finding/cache state is derived and disposable.


**W-347 · 2026-10-03 · Bootstrap startup integrity verification is staged so first-use setup and Obsidian startup are not blocked by a whole-plugin hash scan.** Bootstrap still preserves W-322/W-331/W-333 controlled-release integrity. On normal startup it first performs only the activation work that is actually needed: required/prohibited core-plugin state and, for any disabled locked community plugin, exact version/hash verification before enabling it. Already-enabled locked plugins do not need to be re-hashed merely to remain enabled because Obsidian has already loaded them. Author registration is surfaced immediately after this lightweight activation stage. The complete release integrity scan of every locked runtime/configuration file still runs on every startup, but waits until Obsidian's metadata pass completes (with a bounded fallback) so it does not contend with the heaviest vault-start work. Manual **Show release check** remains an immediate full verification. This changes scheduling only; it does not weaken the release hash contract or permit an unverified disabled plugin to be enabled.


**W-348 · 2026-10-03 · Warm-cache change detection uses Obsidian file ctime + mtime + size, not mtime + size alone.** A warm-start cache must not silently reuse stale semantic state merely because a file was rewritten with the same byte length or with a preserved/coarse modification time. Semantic-cache v3 therefore records Obsidian FileStats `ctime`, `mtime` and `size` for every Markdown path and treats any difference as a changed file; optional content hashes remain available for future stronger evidence where justified. Cache semantic version advances so older fingerprints are rejected rather than guessed compatible. This remains a performance discriminator, not model authority: uncertainty or incompatibility falls back to reconciliation/full rebuild from Markdown.


**W-349 · 2026-10-03 · Whole-model assurance is a shared revision-scoped service, not separate scans owned by Review, diagnostics, or future views.** Workbench now centralizes expensive index findings plus vault-wide Local Model validation in one `AssuranceManager`. The service waits for pending asynchronous Local Model parsing, captures the current semantic revision, computes one snapshot, memoizes it while that revision remains current, coalesces concurrent consumers, and retries once if the model changes during the scan. Review and diagnostics consume the same snapshot; future health/status surfaces must do the same rather than launching their own full-model scans. A forced manual refresh may explicitly recompute. This advances RTA-4 and preserves the boundary that readiness does not depend on exhaustive global verification.


**W-350 · 2026-10-03 · Candidate runtime tools require repository-level automated test/typecheck/build gates before their artifacts are installed into an integration vault.** Workbench already uses GitHub Actions to test and build the checked-in candidate artifact. Bootstrap 0.3.1 now has its own path-scoped GitHub Actions gate in `.github/workflows/bootstrap-candidate.yml`: clean install from `package-lock.json`, unit tests, TypeScript/build, then a second build compared byte-for-byte to prove reproducibility. Source edits are not integration evidence by themselves; only a candidate that clears its automated gate should be copied into a disposable integrated vault or considered for Base-Vault promotion. This formalizes the artifact discipline learned during WB-106 and reduces the chance of source/runtime drift recurring.


**W-351 · 2026-10-03 · The persistent semantic cache uses stable path-hash buckets rather than sequential sorted shards.** Sequential chunking is acceptable for one-time snapshots but does not scale to durable incremental persistence: inserting or renaming an early-sorting path can shift thousands of otherwise unchanged records into different files, forcing broad rewrites and defeating the purpose of a change journal. Cache container v3 therefore assigns note records, Local Model regions, and file fingerprints to fixed deterministic FNV-1a path buckets. The manifest stores only bounded bucket-set metadata rather than every file fingerprint. Bucket identity is stable when unrelated notes are added, which makes later dirty-bucket persistence possible without another cache-format redesign. The current writer still commits a complete inactive A/B slot for recovery simplicity; partial dirty-bucket writes are a later RTA-3 optimization and must retain manifest-last/fail-closed semantics. Markdown/Local Model remain authoritative and the cache stays disposable.


**W-352 · 2026-10-03 · Core semantic readiness is separated from Local Model body hydration on a cold/full rebuild.** Workbench must not make every engineer wait for contextual Local Model body reads before ordinary note/relationship navigation becomes usable. A full build now uses Obsidian's metadata cache to construct the reusable-note graph and file fingerprints first, marks that core semantic state Ready, and then hydrates only metadata-prefiltered Local Model candidate notes in bounded background batches. Occurrence-aware operations, global assurance and semantic-cache persistence explicitly wait for Local Model hydration to settle before consuming/persisting contextual state. The status surface distinguishes core Ready from pending Local Model hydration. Revision/epoch guards prevent an older background read from overwriting a newer edit or rebuild. This is a staged-readiness change only; Local Model semantics remain authoritative Markdown and no occurrence view may silently operate on incomplete contextual data.


**W-353 · 2026-10-03 · Open-vault add/delete/rename events trigger metadata-settled authored-link re-resolution, not stale resolved edges or immediate body rereads.** W-345 made warm-start path-set reconciliation safe by retaining authored relationship-link evidence. The same rule now applies while the vault is already open. A newly created, deleted or renamed Markdown path can change how an otherwise unchanged wikilink resolves, so Workbench marks relationship resolution pending and re-resolves cached authored links after Obsidian reports metadata/link resolution settled, with a bounded fallback timer where that signal is unavailable. The scan is metadata/CPU only, yields in chunks, updates only notes whose resolved evidence actually changed, and participates in the semantic-settle barrier used by assurance/cache/view consumers. Large bursts still fall back to the existing full rebuild policy. This closes a correctness gap where a live rename could previously leave stale relationship edges until a later rebuild.


**W-354 · 2026-10-03 · Controlled runtime dependencies require explicit engineering value and acceptance coverage.** MDSE should avoid multiple plugins independently indexing the same large vault when Workbench or Obsidian already owns the capability. Current classification: retain as essential — MDSE Bootstrap, MDSE Workbench, Templater, Advanced Canvas, Obsidian Git; retain provisionally while Workbench replacement is incomplete — Fileclass and Breadcrumbs; review for retirement after dependency and startup comparison — Dataview (no MDSE queries currently exist), Nodian (trial only), QuickAdd (optional launcher), Table Exporter (optional export). The controlled Base changes only after generated templates/configuration/docs and representative engineering workflows confirm that a reviewed dependency is no longer required. The objective is the smallest dependable runtime surface that preserves engineering capability.


**W-355 · 2026-10-03 · Workbench records a bounded local runtime history so architecture changes are judged by measured user experience, not anecdotes.** The git-ignored Workbench plugin data now retains the latest 20 completed startup samples: startup mode, vault/model size, metadata quiet wait, core semantic-index time, Local Model hydration time/candidate count, and warm-restore outcome. A **Show runtime history** command exposes the latest samples. The history is derived local telemetry only; it is never model authority, never committed to the engineering repository, and can disappear without consequence. This evidence will be used to compare cold/full, warm/restored and reconciled startup behavior, validate W-354 dependency reductions, and establish realistic performance gates on supported hardware.


**W-356 · 2026-10-03 · A Local Model body read failure is a scoped assurance finding and cannot poison a previously valid semantic cache.** One unreadable or transiently unavailable note must not make the entire Markdown vault unusable. Workbench records the affected path as a `local.read-failed` finding, keeps ordinary note/relationship semantics available, and lets Review/diagnostics surface the problem. The background Local Model hydration loop continues with other notes. While any Local Model read error is active, Workbench refuses to overwrite the disposable persistent cache so a previously complete generation remains available for recovery; a later successful read clears the scoped error and allows cache persistence again. This implements the graceful-degradation rule from W-343 without silently treating missing contextual semantics as valid.


**W-351 · 2026-10-03 · Workbench exposes one lightweight runtime-health surface that never triggers expensive model work.** Startup phase, Local Model hydration/read state, schema compatibility, semantic-cache persistence and already-computed assurance freshness are summarized from existing runtime state only. The status bar becomes clickable and reports concise states such as **Workbench ✓**, **Workbench · syncing N**, or **Workbench · N issues**; the detailed health modal remains lightweight and explicitly does not run whole-model assurance. Engineering findings are kept separate from runtime failure: a healthy runtime may show that Review has findings without presenting the tool itself as broken. This keeps health/status useful during normal work without turning status polling into another source of startup or background load.


**W-352 · 2026-10-03 · Live Workbench indexing coalesces rapid editor/metadata events by path before reparsing semantics.** A human typing or an automated edit can cause multiple Obsidian metadata/cache events for the same note in a short interval. Workbench must not turn each event into a separate note upsert, Local Model body read and cache-invalidating semantic revision. Live events are therefore collected in a per-path set and flushed after a short 250 ms quiet window, with bounded yields during multi-file application. Path-set changes still trigger authored-link re-resolution after Obsidian link resolution settles. A burst at or above the existing 300-path threshold abandons per-file replay and schedules the proven quiet-time full rebuild. This reduces editor-time CPU/body-read churn without weakening Markdown authority or delaying normal semantic updates beyond a small debounce window.


**W-353 · 2026-10-03 · Warm semantic-cache restoration uses bounded parallel shard reads rather than fully serial or unbounded I/O.** The sharded cache now restores fingerprint, note and Local Model buckets through small worker pools (four reads per shard family, at most twelve concurrent shard reads across the three families). This reduces warm-start latency without issuing dozens of simultaneous filesystem requests that could recreate startup contention on slower corporate machines. Read order remains deterministic for reconstruction, and any corrupt/missing shard still fails closed to the opposite A/B slot or a vault rebuild. Automated storage tests assert that independent shard reads overlap while the concurrency ceiling is preserved.


**W-354 · 2026-10-03 · MDSE uses staged capability readiness: core model service first, occurrence-aware capabilities later.** Stability takes precedence over having every Workbench feature fully hydrated at the instant the vault opens. A successful core startup means schemas are loaded and the reusable-note semantic graph is ready; Workbench may then report itself usable while governed Local Model bodies hydrate later in the background. Only views whose semantics actually depend on Local Model occurrences—Internal, Structure, Interfaces, Where Used and Requirements—wait for occurrence hydration when invoked. Functional, Design, Verification, Scenario, Behavior, Failure/risk and Evidence views may operate as soon as the core graph is ready. Cold-build Local Model body reads are deferred until after core readiness, then start after a short post-ready delay or immediately when an occurrence-aware consumer/Review requests them. Runtime-history collection must not itself pull deferred capability work into the startup critical path. Cache persistence is scheduled later than occurrence hydration so background cache work does not become the mechanism that forces early Local Model parsing. This explicitly permits partial feature readiness while preserving correctness at each capability boundary.


**W-355 · 2026-10-03 · Heavy MDSE startup work is intentionally staggered into non-overlapping lanes.** Startup stability takes precedence over making every derived capability ready at the earliest possible moment. The intended sequence is: Obsidian/UI and lightweight plugin registration first; after metadata resolution, Workbench waits a short additional lane before core index/restore begins; once core Workbench is usable, Local Model occurrence hydration starts later or on demand; semantic-cache persistence waits for a longer quiet window; Bootstrap's complete controlled-release hash verification is delayed well beyond metadata resolution so it does not compete with Workbench core/occurrence/cache work. Bootstrap still performs lightweight activation repair and author registration early, and a manual **Show release check** still forces an immediate full verification. If Obsidian never emits metadata-resolved, existing bounded fallback delays remain the recovery path. The design goal is deterministic staged load rather than multiple correct subsystems simultaneously saturating filesystem/CPU resources during vault startup.


**W-356 · 2026-10-03 · Long Workbench loops yield by elapsed UI work budget, not a fixed number of notes.** Fixed-count chunking behaves differently across machines and note complexity: 500 lightweight records may be harmless on one system and a noticeable UI stall on another. Core indexing, warm reconciliation, authored-link re-resolution, Local Model hydration and live multi-file application therefore use a cooperative elapsed-time work slice (initially 12 ms) and yield to the event loop when that slice is consumed. Count-based thresholds remain only where they express policy, such as the 300-path decision to abandon incremental replay for a full quiet-time rebuild. This makes responsiveness scale with real processing cost and supports slower managed/corporate hardware without changing model semantics.


**W-357 · 2026-10-03 · Deferred MDSE background work is activity-aware and must not steal responsiveness from active engineering work.** After core Workbench readiness, Local Model occurrence hydration is queued rather than started immediately. Its background timer rechecks recent metadata/editor activity and pending live semantic updates; if the vault is active, occurrence hydration stays queued and retries later. An explicit occurrence-aware command or Review may still request the capability immediately and wait for correctness. Runtime health distinguishes this from startup failure: the status may show **Workbench ✓ · occurrence data loading** or **Workbench ✓ · applying N** while the core model remains usable. Health observation itself remains side-effect free and never invokes settle/hydration work. Cache persistence already follows a longer quiet-window policy. Together these rules make deferred work subordinate to the engineer's foreground activity rather than merely delayed by a fixed timer.


**W-358 · 2026-10-03 · MDSE runtime subsystems fail independently.** Workbench core-start exceptions are caught at the plugin boundary so Obsidian remains usable and explicit rebuild/recovery stays available. Background occurrence-processing errors are contained and surfaced through runtime health rather than becoming unhandled asynchronous errors. Whole-model assurance is also isolated: a validator exception produces an explicit `assurance unavailable` state for that semantic revision; Review and Diagnostics must show it as unavailable rather than as zero findings, and a later model revision or forced refresh retries the check. Cache failures remain disposable diagnostics. This extends graceful degradation across the Workbench runtime.


**W-359 · 2026-10-03 · Runtime health tracks faults by subsystem rather than overloading one generic error channel.** Core model startup, occurrence/Local Model background processing, semantic-cache persistence, schema compatibility and global assurance each have distinct health evidence. A core-start failure can leave Obsidian usable while Workbench reports `core unavailable`; occurrence-processing issues do not masquerade as cache failures; assurance failure remains separate from engineering findings. This keeps recovery guidance accurate and prevents one degraded derived capability from making unrelated subsystems appear unhealthy.


**W-360 · 2026-10-03 · Runtime acceptance measures time to usable core separately from deferred capability completion.** Because W-354 intentionally allows occurrence hydration, assurance and cache persistence to finish after the core model is usable, one aggregate startup time is no longer meaningful. Workbench runtime history now records user-visible `timeToCoreReadyMs` separately from the core indexing/reconciliation time, startup quiet wait and Local Model hydration evidence. Acceptance decisions should optimize first for vault responsiveness and core readiness, then for deferred-capability completion, rather than forcing later background work back into the startup critical path merely to improve one combined number.


**W-361 · 2026-10-03 · The controlled plugin stack is reviewed as an architectural dependency surface, not treated as permanently fixed.** No plugin is removed by this decision. Bootstrap and Workbench form the intended MDSE core runtime; current operational dependencies such as Obsidian Git and Templater remain until their replacement paths are proven. Presentation/navigation/property/convenience plugins are retained while they provide unique user value, but Workbench overlap now justifies a measured one-at-a-time dependency audit. Any removal requires an inventory of current use, a proven replacement workflow, disposable-vault comparison, startup/runtime evidence, and controlled Base/lock/release updates. The goal is fewer independent vault scanners and less runtime/configuration surface without sacrificing engineering capability.


**W-362 · 2026-10-03 · Background occurrence hydration is preemptible by resumed foreground activity and promotable by explicit user demand.** Starting Local Model hydration in a background lane does not grant it uninterrupted priority. Between occurrence-body reads, Workbench checks whether the vault has become active again or live semantic updates are pending; background hydration pauses while that foreground activity continues. If the engineer explicitly opens an occurrence-aware view or Review while hydration is already running, the same task is promoted to demanded work and finishes without background-idle pauses so the requested command receives correct complete occurrence data. This avoids both extremes: background work cannot monopolize a busy vault, and an explicit engineering action is not artificially delayed by the background policy.


**W-363 · 2026-10-03 · Automatic Bootstrap full verification prefers a quiet activity window but has a bounded maximum deferral.** After the normal metadata-resolved/startup lane, Bootstrap does not immediately begin whole-release hashing if Obsidian is still reporting note activity. It waits for a short quiet window (currently 5 s) so release verification does not compete with active engineering work, while a maximum additional deferral (currently 60 s) guarantees that automatic integrity verification cannot be postponed indefinitely. Lightweight activation repair and author registration remain early; manual **Show release check** remains immediate and bypasses this background scheduling policy. This makes release verification subordinate to foreground responsiveness without weakening or eliminating the eventual exact hash check.


**W-364 · 2026-10-03 · Background/cache waits must never promote deferred occurrence work into foreground-priority work.** Workbench now distinguishes a demanded semantic-settle wait from a background semantic-settle wait. Explicit occurrence-aware engineering actions (Internal/Structure/Interfaces/Where Used/Requirements, Local Model check, Review) may promote an already-running background hydration task and wait for complete occurrence semantics. Background occurrence scheduling and semantic-cache persistence use the non-demanded wait, which respects foreground activity pauses. View-current checks only require Local Model settling when the generated view profile actually consumes occurrences. This closes a priority-inversion path where a cache write or background completion observer could accidentally turn deferred work into foreground work.


**W-365 · 2026-10-03 · Disposable cache persistence is single-flight.** Workbench may receive new edits or cache-dirty events while a background cache generation is still waiting for deferred occurrence work or writing shards. It must never start a second persistence generation concurrently. Cache writes therefore use one in-flight task; later changes remain dirty and schedule another generation only after the current task finishes. Manual **Clear semantic cache** refuses while a write is active instead of racing deletion against persistence. Diagnostics distinguish `writing` from `pending/coalesced` and `current`. This prevents overlapping derived-file writes and makes cache recovery behavior deterministic under sustained editing.


**W-366 · 2026-10-03 · Delayed Bootstrap work is lifecycle-safe and full release verification is single-flight.** Startup timers and quiet-window waits must stop when Bootstrap unloads/reloads; a delayed callback from an obsolete plugin instance must not continue activation or hashing work. Bootstrap therefore tracks unload state, cancels its startup timer, and exits deferred loops before full verification when unloaded. Whole-release verification also uses one in-flight task: an automatic check and a simultaneous manual/status-triggered check share the same scan rather than hashing the plugin payload twice. This applies the same single-owner concurrency rule already used for Workbench indexing, assurance and cache persistence.


**W-367 · 2026-10-03 · Cache persistence waits behind deferred capability completion and never initiates occurrence hydration.** If Local Model occurrence work is still queued or active, Workbench leaves the semantic cache dirty and reschedules persistence rather than opening a cache-write task that waits on or promotes occurrence hydration. Occurrence hydration remains owned by its own background lane or an explicit occurrence-aware consumer. This keeps cache state purely opportunistic and prevents derived persistence from lengthening startup, competing with foreground work, or holding a long-lived write task while a capability is intentionally deferred.


**W-368 · 2026-10-03 · Workbench candidate CI includes a representative 60,000-note semantic-cache scale smoke before artifact build.** Unit correctness and TypeScript/bundle success are not sufficient evidence for the target engineering-model size. The candidate workflow now runs the storage-neutral cache benchmark with 60,000 synthetic model notes and a small changed-file set before building the installable artifact. The smoke test is not yet a strict timing gate because hosted-runner timing is noisy; it is a completion/memory/algorithmic regression gate. Real startup timing remains an integration-vault/hardware acceptance concern measured separately through Workbench runtime history.


**W-369 · 2026-10-03 · Workbench view capability dependencies are declared on the view profile, not inferred from the view name.** Profiles that consume Local Model occurrence semantics now explicitly declare `needsLocalOccurrences: true`; the runtime stages/waits based on that declaration. This removes brittle name-based coupling and lets future Physical/Functional or other views opt into occurrence hydration only if their actual semantics require it. Capability loading remains a property of what the view needs, not a special-case list maintained elsewhere.


**W-364 · 2026-10-03 · Startup safety gate: Workbench performs no model/schema I/O in Obsidian's immediate layout-ready callback or before the first-start metadata/quiet gate.** `onload()` is limited to small local settings plus command/UI/listener registration. `onLayoutReady` only schedules a zero-delay startup handoff and returns control to Obsidian. On first start, Workbench waits for metadata resolution (or the existing bounded quiet fallback) before reading schemas, constructing the semantic index, attempting cache restore, hydrating Local Models or running assurance. Manual rebuilds after the vault is already open are not subject to this first-start gate. Startup exceptions remain contained so ordinary Obsidian reading/editing stays available. This is Piece 1 of the Startup Stability Plan and is the only active startup slice until its build gate is clean.


**W-370 · 2026-10-04 · Dataview is removed from the controlled MDSE runtime after replacement/dependency and startup-cost gates both passed.** The controlled vault contains no Dataview queries, so no current engineering workflow depends on Dataview-specific query execution. Native Obsidian Bases cover the tabular/property views currently present, while Workbench owns the schema-aware model index, Explore/Details/Review/navigation surfaces. In the Step 53 real-Obsidian one-plugin-at-a-time benchmark on the 12,000-note integration vault, Dataview 0.5.68 preserved the same 12,000 files, 12,000 elements and 21,491 links but delayed Workbench core/occurrence readiness by **34.246 s** versus the bracketed Workbench-only baseline. Dataview is therefore removed—not merely disabled—from the controlled plugin lock, enabled list and vendored runtime payload. This is intentionally a one-plugin change; Fileclass, Breadcrumbs, Nodian, Advanced Canvas and other plugins remain unchanged until their own replacement/value gates are satisfied. Reintroducing Dataview requires a demonstrated workflow that native Bases/Workbench cannot satisfy and a new dependency-budget decision.

**W-371 · 2026-10-05 · Import writes use a persistent transaction state; a partial vault is never authoritative.** Before the first generated model note or attachment is written, the importer writes `99_System/11_Import/Import State.json` with status `IMPORT_IN_PROGRESS`, importer/release/source/scope metadata and planned output counts. A destination that already contains an import-state file is not a clean base and is rejected for another semantic import. After every planned note, attachment and required evidence file has been written, the importer writes the Run Manifest and then performs the final authoritative write by replacing the transaction status with `IMPORT_COMPLETE`. A Run Manifest that exists without a matching `IMPORT_COMPLETE` state is non-authoritative and the vault is discarded. If a filesystem/write error occurs after the transaction begins, the importer attempts to replace the state with `IMPORT_FAILED` and records the error; if that failure-state write is itself impossible, the earlier `IMPORT_IN_PROGRESS` marker remains authoritative, which is still a failed/incomplete state. Rollback is not required because candidate imports use disposable fresh bases, but an interrupted base may never be resumed or treated as valid output. This closes the persistent partial-write ambiguity identified as IMP-001; broader source/plan/write/semantic acceptance status separation remains IMP-003.


**W-372 · 2026-10-05 · Import status is multi-dimensional; no single PASS may imply semantic or release acceptance.** The native importer reports separate run dimensions: `SOURCE_PASS/SOURCE_WARN` for source preflight, `PLAN_PASS` for deterministic planning, `WRITE_PASS/WRITE_FAIL/WRITE_IN_PROGRESS` for filesystem materialization, `SEMANTIC_CLEAR/SEMANTIC_REVIEW_REQUIRED` for unresolved translation/model findings, and `ACCEPTANCE_PENDING` until external release gates such as post-import review, determinism/headless validation and Workbench acceptance are completed. `WRITE_PASS` is authoritative only when W-371 `Import State.json` is `IMPORT_COMPLETE`. The Run Manifest must list these dimensions explicitly instead of a single generic Result PASS. The importer UI may show a successful write while still using warning presentation when semantic review is required. This implements IMP-003 without changing the underlying semantic mappings; acceptance remains a separate later gate.


**W-373 · 2026-10-05 · WAL-mode EA snapshots are blocked for native import.** A `.qea/.qeax` whose SQLite header indicates WAL mode is not accepted as a complete source snapshot because committed or pending content may reside in the companion WAL rather than the selected main database file. The importer does not attempt to discover, read or merge a companion WAL. Preflight records `SQLITE_WAL_MODE` as a hard failure, planning/writing remain disabled, and the user must close/checkpoint Enterprise Architect and select a clean snapshot. This replaces the earlier warning-only behavior and closes the implementation portion of IMP-004.


**W-374 · 2026-10-05 · Semantic import requires an initialized destination vault identity.** The generated runtime base intentionally ships with `.vault.yaml` `vault_uid: UNINITIALIZED`, but that state is not a valid semantic-import destination. Before a base can be selected for native EA import, the governed vault initializer must assign its vault identity/name while preserving `mdse_release`. The importer validates `vault_uid` alongside the release and schema/plugin gates and rejects a missing or literal `UNINITIALIZED` value. This makes initialization an enforced prerequisite rather than a procedural suggestion and closes the implementation portion of IMP-005.


**W-375 · 2026-10-05 · Every viable QEAX source is cryptographically fingerprinted before planning.** Filename, byte size and modification time are useful operator context but are not sufficient source identity. After source/schema/count/WAL qualification has no hard failure, the importer computes the standard SHA-256 digest of the complete selected `.qea/.qeax` byte stream using bounded chunk reads so fingerprinting does not require loading the database into memory. The digest is stored in preflight source metadata, the Run Manifest and W-371 transaction state. A hashing error fails preflight rather than allowing an unfingerprinted candidate to proceed. The current source-lineage identifier (for example `EA8647`) remains a governed model-lineage label and is not a substitute for the exact-file SHA-256. This implements IMP-006; source-profile separation remains IMP-007.


**W-376 · 2026-10-05 · Reviewed or endpoint-invalid relationships are evidence-only until their MDSE meaning is accepted.** Stage-1 source preservation does not require every EA connector to become an authoritative frontmatter relationship. A connector plan already marked `review: true` is withheld from the canonical relationship graph; its EA GUID, connector type/field, source/target evidence, connector name/notes and review reason remain in the import evidence and note review/source sections. After the canonical graph is built from non-reviewed mappings and structural rules, the importer applies `relationships.yaml` endpoint rules; any remaining provisional or endpoint-invalid edge is recorded in semantic review evidence and removed together with its persisted inverse/symmetric mirror before Markdown is rendered. Therefore Workbench, Bases, AI and other consumers see only relationship fields that satisfy the current accepted schema/endpoint contract. Review evidence is not silently discarded and can later drive an approved Stage-2 correction or importer-rule change. This implements IMP-002 and supersedes the earlier v0.8 behavior that could write the questioned relationship while also adding a REVIEW line.

**W-377 · 2026-10-05 · A contextual Local Model endpoint may exist without a reusable Port definition; this semantic change is introduced only through Local Model schema 0.3.** An EA instance/context Port is first preserved as a contextual endpoint occurrence with its stable local identity, owner/parent placement, identifier, kind, multiplicity and source-map provenance. When deterministic source evidence resolves that occurrence to an existing reusable Port definition, `definition` links to that Port. When no such reusable concept exists, Stage 1 must not manufacture a first-class Port note merely to satisfy the Local Model format and must not suppress the contextual endpoint; `definition` is absent and the endpoint remains usable for local topology. Definitionless endpoints may not carry `usage`; variation semantics require a reusable definition. Existing Local Model 0.2 meaning is frozen: 0.2 endpoints still require a Port definition. The relaxed rule is Local Model 0.3 so older Workbench/runtime behavior is not silently reinterpreted. Workbench must gain readable/writable 0.3 support before the importer may emit 0.3. This resolves the IMP-009 policy question; implementation and compatibility tests remain required.

**W-378 · 2026-10-05 · Importer v0.8.13 implements the W-377 contextual-endpoint boundary without synthesizing reusable Ports.** Deterministic reusable-Port resolution remains limited to W-114.1 `PDATA3` evidence or W-114.2 exactly one same-named Port on the reusable block. When neither rule resolves, the source Port is planned as `local-endpoint-only` and materialized as a Local Model 0.3 endpoint with no `definition`; no `portgroup:` first-class entity or W-276 synthetic Port note is created. Connectors touching a contextual-only endpoint are explicitly review-only at the note-level relationship graph so they cannot be silently skipped or promoted into an unsupported first-class relation; supported occurrence-level connector/binding/flow topology is still reconstructed from source IDs inside the Local Model. The importer writes `Review - Definitionless Local Endpoints.csv` and reports the count in run evidence. v0.8.13 requires a schema-0.3 base and Workbench 0.3 compatibility; static source/syntax checks pass, but a targeted browser run and fresh real-QEAX import remain required before IMP-009 is closed.
